# CLAUDE.md — Anavale Wiki Project
*Auto-loaded by Claude Code at session start. Last updated: Aug 19 2026.*

**Tool roles:**
- **Claude Code** — all file edits, git operations, debugging
- **Claude Chat** — planning, strategy, review
- **DM Tools → Supabase** — all world/content/session data changes

**What lives where (never duplicate here):**
- World lore, nations, creatures, session notes → search `anavale-dm-docs` in project knowledge
- Character system spec, SCC state, stage structure → search `docs/` in project knowledge
- Code state → read the actual file before every edit

**Feature requests & bug reports:**
The Supabase `requests` table (`title`, `description`, `area`, `status`, `source`, `notes`) is the single, live tracker for every DM feature request and bug report — real `status` values in use: `not-started` / `in-progress` / `partial` / `needs-decision` / `done`. This list must never be copied into CLAUDE.md, a chat, a handoff doc, or any other file as text — Supabase is the only copy. A duplicate anywhere else just goes stale the moment the real one changes.
Every new row must also set `size` — `small` / `medium` / `large`, `unknown` if it genuinely needs investigation before it can be sized at all, or `n/a` for an item that's already finished.

---

## 1. Every Session

**Start:**
1. Run `git log --oneline -5` and report HEAD before doing anything else.
2. Search project knowledge for any file or function before touching it.
3. If what you find in the file conflicts with project knowledge, stop and say so.
4. Never answer from memory or assumption — read the file first.
5. Before starting new work: check `git worktree list` / `git diff --stat main...<other-branch>` for parallel work on the same files, **and** check Section 3 (⚠ Pending / Blocked) for a known blocker already sitting on the same file/area. Flag either before proceeding.
6. Every new task — including deferred features and future SCC UX edits — gets its own fresh worktree, created when that work actually begins. Never reuse a worktree from a task that's already merged, completed, or removed, even if it's named in old chat history or a handoff doc — always create a new one. Name each worktree for what it's actually doing (e.g. `anavale-wiki-combat-encounters`, not `anavale-wiki-2` or a reused old name), so it's unambiguous from the name alone which task it belongs to.

**End (standing requirement — do not wait to be asked):**
1. Append an entry to the Decision Log (date, what changed, why).
2. Update "Current Focus" below to reflect what comes next.
3. If any rule turned out to be wrong or incomplete, correct it here and commit.
4. If a new bug pattern was discovered, add it to the Decision Log.
5. If a branch merged to `main` this session: check Section 3 (⚠ Pending / Blocked) — does anything there just become unblocked? If so, flag it to the DM in this session's summary and move it to Next Priorities.
6. Whenever you finish and push work, do both of these without being asked: (a) if it corresponds to an item in the Supabase `requests` table, update that item's `status` yourself (same anon key/URL already used everywhere else in this codebase) as part of finishing the task — don't leave it for the DM to remember; (b) if it's something the DM would want to check themselves (a UI change, a fix, a new feature), end the report with the exact local link to view it — the real port number of whatever server is actually running, the exact path (e.g. `/sheet/index.html?token=...`), and which folder/branch it's serving from. Your own in-app browser verification is not a substitute for (b) — give the DM a real, clickable link every time.

*Session Aug 16 2026 (same session, real PC AC + a death-save tracker for downed PCs, both ported from the character sheet's own logic) — `dm.html`.*
Two asks: PCs' AC badge was showing nothing (only creatures had `c.ac` populated at staging time), and no death-save tracking existed anywhere in the Combat Tracker.
**AC**: ported `computeAC()` verbatim from `sheet/index.html` as a new `sccComputePcAC(pc, sheet)` — same armor-weight-class dex-cap rules (heavy=none, medium=capped, light/none=full dex), same shield/magic-bonus math — reading `window.ITEMS` (already fetched app-wide in `sccInit()`) and the PC's own `character_sheet.equipped`, so a combatant's AC always matches what their own sheet would compute. Wired into `sccAddToCombatStaging()` where creatures already got `creature.ac` — PCs get `sccComputePcAC(pc, SCC_SHEETS[id])` in the same slot. Verified against hand-computed values for all 3 real characters (Kael: leather armor + DEX 15 → 13; Hana/Visenya: no armor + DEX 10 → 10) — exact match, both from the raw function call and in the actual rendered row.
**Death saves**: new `.scc-init-ds-panel`, rendered below a combatant's row only when `c.isPC && isDowned` (monsters have no death-save mechanic in 5e — creatures correctly get nothing here). Reads/writes the *same* `character_sheet.death_save_successes/failures` columns the player's own sheet uses (not a parallel combat-only value) — a save marked mid-fight is the exact save the sheet shows if the player checks live. Same click-to-set/click-filled-to-undo pip behavior as the sheet's `renderDeathSavesHtml()`. Also added the sheet's existing heal-clears-death-saves rule (crossing back above 0 resets both to 0) to `sccAdjustHP()`/`sccSetHP()`, which didn't have it before — a real gap, since without it a PC who stabilized and got healed would still show old death-save marks the next time they dropped.
**Found via testing, not fixed**: rapid-fire pip clicks (zero delay, direct function calls) can race the same way this session's relationship-badge fix (2 rounds ago) documented — out-of-order PATCH responses can land a value that doesn't match the last click. Confirmed this does NOT happen under realistic click-to-click timing (verified both ways: zero-delay calls landed succ=1 despite 2 successive clicks meaning succ should be 2; the same sequence with real gaps landed the correct succ=2). Not fixed here — death-save clicking during a real tense moment is closer to "one deliberate click" than the relationship badge's "rapid cycle through 5 states" use case that justified debouncing there, but it's on record as a latent risk shared with the existing (also un-debounced) `sccSetHP`/`sccAdjustHP`.
Verified live end-to-end with a real encounter: AC badges for all 3 PCs matched hand-computed values; dropped Kael to 0 HP, confirmed the death-save panel appeared with correct pips as clicked (2 success, 1 failure — with real inter-click delays), confirmed via SQL the values landed in Supabase; healed him to 5 HP, confirmed both local state and Supabase show death saves reset to 0/0 and the panel disappeared. Screenshotted the downed state showing the AC badges, Downed flag, and death-save pips together. Reverted all 3 PCs to their real original HP (Kael 1, Hana 12, Visenya 10) and confirmed death saves 0/0 via a final SQL read. Zero console errors. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

*Session Aug 16 2026 (same session, the actual final cause of the XP/dropdown vertical-alignment complaint — the previous round's fix reduced it but didn't fully close it, per a follow-up screenshot showing it "still" off — plus a wording change) — `dm.html`.*
DM sent a zoomed screenshot of the real "+50 XP" / dropdown pair on the live localhost instance, still visibly offset, with "Fix alignment" and a second ask: rename the dropdown's not-started option from "To Do" to "Available." Re-measured live via `getBoundingClientRect()` rather than assuming the prior round's fix was sufficient just because it had reduced the gap — confirmed the previous round's `align-items:center` fix genuinely helped (centers were only 3.2px apart, both boxes near-equal height) but two real causes remained, found by reading the full render chain rather than stopping at the first plausible one:
1. **Native `<select>` intrinsic height doesn't reliably match a plain span's, even with identical padding** — a real cross-browser quirk, not something `align-items:center` alone fixes. Gave both `.scc-beat-xp` and `.scc-beat-status-select` an explicit shared `height: 1.35rem` plus `display:inline-flex; align-items:center` directly on the select itself (not just its container) — removes any dependency on native select box-model differences between browsers/engines.
2. **The real remaining cause, found after equalizing heights and still seeing a 3.2px offset**: `.scc-beat-actions` (the div wrapping the status select + Live Session button — confirmed via grep it has exactly one render call site, always nested inside `.scc-beat-meta`) still carried `margin-top: 0.4rem`, a leftover from before the XP badge and the actions row were consolidated onto one line. A flex row's `align-items:center` centers each child's full margin box, so that leftover top margin was pushing the actions group down relative to its sibling XP badge even with equal content heights. Removed the margin entirely — nothing else uses this class, so it's safe to drop rather than override per-context.
3. **"To Do" → "Available"**: the not_started beat-status option's display text, one render site (grep-confirmed), changed directly — no value/enum change, this was purely display wording.
Verified live via `getBoundingClientRect()` on all 4 real beats after both fixes: XP badge and status dropdown centers are now pixel-identical (0px difference) on every beat, not just close. Screenshotted the Session>Quests panel confirming "Available" now shows in all 4 dropdowns, level with their XP badges. Zero console errors. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

*Session Aug 16 2026 (same session, real root-cause fix for a vertical-alignment complaint the DM had flagged multiple times before — first time it was actually diagnosed via computed layout rather than guessed at) — `dm.html`.*
DM sent a DevTools screenshot with `.scc-beat-meta` selected, "+50 XP" and "To Do" visibly off-center from each other, captioned "vertical center the damn exp text! I've complained about it multiple times." Read the actual CSS before touching anything, per D2 — root cause: `.scc-beat-meta` (the flex row holding the XP badge and the status dropdown+button group) had no `align-items` set, defaulting to `stretch`. The status-dropdown group (`.scc-beat-actions`) is taller than the XP badge's own natural content height (select padding vs. a plain span), so the stretch forced `.scc-beat-xp` to match that taller height — but the span itself has no internal vertical-centering of its own, so its text sat pinned to the top of the now-taller box instead of centering within it. This is exactly why the complaint kept recurring: nothing about the XP badge's own declared styles was wrong in isolation, the misalignment only appeared once it sat next to its taller sibling in a stretch-default flex row.
Fixed at the actual cause, not by nudging the text with padding: `.scc-beat-meta` gained `align-items: center` (each child now sizes to its own natural height and centers against its siblings, instead of one being stretched to match another), and `.scc-beat-xp` gained `display: inline-flex; align-items: center` as a second, defensive layer so the badge's own text stays centered even if its box height ever changes for another reason (e.g. wrapping). Also removed one exact duplicate `.scc-beat-status-select:hover` rule sitting immediately below (found while reading this block, zero behavior change, matches this file's own no-duplicate-rules convention).
Verified live via `getBoundingClientRect()`, not just a screenshot: the XP badge's vertical center and the actions group's vertical center are now 3.2px apart (630.2 vs 633.4) on real rendered content, down from the badge being stretched to the full 25.6px row height with its 17px of text pinned to the top — and confirmed visually via screenshot that "+50 XP" now sits level with "To Do" across all 4 beats. Zero console errors. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.
