Screenshotted the Quest Dashboard showing the new colors and the real Blocked/High-Stakes collision on Anomaly 7-C. Zero console errors.

*Session Aug 15 2026 (same session, a sixth round on the same beat list — investigated a "beat numbers wrong color" report that turned out to already be correct, then two real, direct fixes: XP badges reverted to gold, status dropdown recolored per its current value) — `dm.html`. Full detail in Decision Log.*
DM's screenshot annotated "Change back to yellow-gold" on the beat number labels ("1"/"2"/"3") and repeated "Change to blue" on the XP badges. Investigated the number labels first, per D2 — grepped `.scc-beat-num` and confirmed it had never been touched by any edit this session, still `color: var(--dm-gold)`; live computed-style check on all 4 beat numbers confirmed `rgb(240,180,41)` (`#f0b429`, the app's real gold), matching a fresh screenshot too. Reported this back as already-correct rather than making an unnecessary change — same "verify before assuming a regression" discipline as two earlier rounds this session that also didn't reproduce. DM's next message clarified the real remaining ask directly: XP text should be gold-yellow (reverting last round's move to blue), and the beat-status dropdown's own color should reflect its current value instead of a flat neutral grey regardless of state.
**XP badges**: `.scc-beat-xp` (Quest Dashboard's "Active Quests This Session" beat list) and `.scc-live-beat-xp` (the Live Session panel's independent beat-dot renderer — checked as a sibling per this session's established habit, since the two have drifted before) both reverted from blue back to gold (`var(--dm-gold)`, `rgba(240,180,41,...)` background/border) — undoing exactly the one XP-badge color change from 2 rounds ago, nothing else on either surface touched.
**Status dropdown reflects state, not a flat neutral**: `.scc-beat-status-select` previously rendered identically regardless of whether the beat was To Do/In Progress/Done — the dropdown's *text* said the state but its color didn't. Added 3 modifier classes (`--not_started`/`--in_progress`/`--completed`), reusing the exact tokens the beat rail already uses for the same 3 states (`--scc-beat-todo-*` grey, `--scc-beat-prog-*` blue, `--scc-beat-done-*` green — the grey token was sitting unused since the rail itself moved to all-blue-except-completed 2 rounds ago, now given a real second job here) — render code appends `scc-beat-status-select--' + st` to the `<select>`'s class. Deliberately did NOT make the dropdown follow the rail's own blue-for-not-started choice: the rail's "blue is the whole quest's active theme" reframing was a DM-driven, comprehensive instruction for that specific element; the dropdown is a different control being asked to do a different job here ("reflect current status" only makes sense if its 3 states are visually distinct from each other), so giving it its own honest not_started/in_progress/completed traffic-light coloring using existing tokens is the correct application of the same design language, not a reversal of it.
Verified live end-to-end, not just via computed style on the resting state: cycled a real beat through all 3 values via the actual dropdown's `change` event (not direct function calls) — confirmed not_started renders grey (`rgba(255,255,255,0.04)` bg / muted text), in_progress renders blue (`rgb(96,165,250)`), completed renders green (`rgb(34,197,94)`) — then reverted to not_started the same way and confirmed via a fresh SQL read that all 4 real `quest_progress` rows for the test quest are back to their original `not_started`/`null` state. XP badges confirmed gold (`rgb(240,180,41)`) on both the Quest Dashboard panel and the Live Session panel. Zero console errors. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

*Session Aug 15 2026 (same session, the DM re-annotated the same "make blue" request across all 4 rail segments plus all 3 XP badges — reversing the prior round's declined interpretation) — `dm.html`. Full detail in Decision Log.*
The prior round declined coloring "not started" beat segments blue, reasoning it would collide with "in progress." This round's screenshot annotated literally every rail segment (1-4, including the ones that were never in-progress) and every XP badge with "Make blue" — a DM's repeated, comprehensive annotation overrides a prior inferred design read, so implemented directly rather than re-litigating. Reframed the model: blue isn't "in progress" specifically, it's this quest's whole-card active theme (matching its "Available" status), with only Completed staying green as a real, distinct win-state. `.scc-beat-seg--not_started` and `.scc-beat-xp` both moved to blue. Checking for siblings before calling it done surfaced a third independent render of the same beat-progress concept — the Live Session panel's own beat dots/XP text — still gold; fixed there too so a DM switching tabs mid-session doesn't see the collision reappear. Also tightened `.scc-beat-meta`'s top margin (0.4rem → 0.2rem) per "too much empty space on bottom of text." Verified live across both surfaces via computed style and a real dropdown-driven state change.
"Fix alignment" pointed at a real, measured bug: the active beat's `+XP` badge sat 3px right of every other beat's, because `.scc-beat-item--active`'s 4px left border (vs. the base item's 1px) pushed its flex content over — confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` before touching anything, not assumed from the screenshot alone. Fixed by reserving the same 4px on every beat item (transparent when inactive), so becoming active no longer shifts the content. The "▶ In Progress" dropdown reported as still gold did not reproduce — checked via computed style and a fresh screenshot on the exact code just shipped, confirmed neutral/grey both ways; most likely a stale browser cache, an on-record recurring issue in this file. Declined, with reasoning given back: making the "not started" rail segments (2/3/4) blue too, per two of the screenshot's arrows — blue is now specifically "in progress," and coloring "not started" the same blue would recreate the exact one-color-two-meanings collision this session has spent three rounds fixing.

*Session Aug 15 2026 (same session, one more annotated screenshot — a real gold/gold collision on the same "Active Quests This Session" card, plus a click-to-cycle button replaced with a dropdown) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`. Full detail in Decision Log.*
The prior round's status-badge parity fix turned out not to be the whole story — the DM's "this should be Blue" annotation pointed at the beat-progress rail's "in progress" highlight, which was still gold, sitting directly under the "Mid Stakes" badge (also gold) on the same card. Recolored `--scc-beat-prog-bg/text/border` to blue, plus a second `!important` gold override (`.scc-beat-item--active`, the expanded beat box's pulsing highlight) that the token change alone wouldn't have reached. Separately, replaced the per-beat status button (click cycled through To Do → In Progress → Done with no visible indication of what clicking would do) with a real 3-option dropdown, mirroring the quest-status dropdown pattern from 2 rounds ago. Verified live: both the compact rail segment and the expanded beat box now render blue, confirmed via computed style; the dropdown correctly reads/writes the same `quest_progress` table the old button did.
`sccRenderQuestPanel()` (Session → Quests, not the Quest Dashboard) has its own independent quest-card renderer, `sccRenderQuestPanel()` — already reused the shared `q-status-*`/`q-stakes-*` CSS classes correctly, so status color was already consistent, but had two of its own bugs the Quest Dashboard fixes never reached: the "Local/Regional/Arc/Campaign" beat-count tier badge hardcoded the *exact* same blue as the Available status badge sitting right next to it (a real collision, not just similar), and the stakes badge still showed the raw lowercase enum (`"mid"`) instead of the `stakesLabel()` fix from the Quest Dashboard round. Tier badge recolored to the same neutral grey used for other non-status informational badges (Suggested Level, First Available); stakes badge switched to `stakesLabel()`. Verified live: "Regional"/"✦ Available"/"Mid Stakes" now three visually distinct badges, confirmed via computed style.

*Session Aug 15 2026 (same session, live DM review of the Quest Dashboard pass above, from an annotated screenshot) — `dm.html`. Full detail in Decision Log.*
Two direct annotations plus one real color collision surfaced by a third: (1) added a status-colored top border to each quest card in the list (`quest-card--status-*`, reusing the exact status colors already established, not new hex) — a second always-visible signal beyond the badge text. (2) Moved the status dropdown from the very bottom of the selected quest's detail panel to the top right, next to the title — matches the same "move it to where a DM looks first" reasoning as the Suggested Level move earlier this round. (3) The DM asked "why is this blue?" about the Suggested Level card badge — real answer: no reason, it was reusing the exact same blue as the Available status badge, which became a genuine collision the moment status also started driving the card's top border. Recolored to a plain neutral grey (matching the sibling "First available" badge's own existing color) since Suggested Level isn't a status and shouldn't borrow one's hue.

*Session Aug 15 2026 (same session, 5-item DM feedback round — Deity reclassified as an entity type not a relationship, AC/Stats fixed in active combat, a global Quick Note button, and a Quest Dashboard UX pass) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`, `session_notes.npc_relationships`. Full detail in Decision Log.*
**Deity is an entity type, not a relationship — fixed at the data-model level, not just relabeled.** Removed `deity` from `SCC_REL_CYCLE` entirely (now `neutral/ally/caution/hostile`, 4 states) and added a separate, purple, non-cyclable "✦ God" badge driven by the real `world_characters.category === 'god'` field, shown alongside the relationship badge wherever it renders. Oro's relationship (previously `'deity'`, now an invalid enum value) migrated to `'ally'` — his god-status is now correctly carried by his real `category` field instead. Also deleted a fully dead legacy CSS block (`.scc-npc-card.rel-*`/`.scc-rel-badge.rel-*`) discovered while touching this — referenced `--scc-rel-*` tokens removed as dead code last round, confirmed via grep to have zero real consumers even before that.
**Combat: AC and Stats were only ever wired into the pre-combat staging list, never the live initiative tracker.** Real bug, not a design choice — once "Start Combat" ran, staging hid and the initiative list took over, and neither AC nor the Stats toggle+panel (both built for staging 2 rounds ago) ever made the jump. Both now render on every combatant row during actual combat, reusing the exact same `sccBuildCreatureStatBlockHtml()` staging already used — verified live with a real creature, mid-fight, both AC and full stat block (abilities/attacks/traits/actions) confirmed showing.
**Quick Note — global entry point, real UX complaint.** DM's own words: getting to a quick note required Session tab → Notes sub-tab → scroll past 5 fields → find the button, three steps for something meant to be instant. Added a floating "📝 Quick Note" button fixed to the bottom-right corner, visible from every tab (page-level, same placement pattern as the lookup drawer) — opens a small self-contained modal (category + title + body), posts directly to `session_note_entries`, closes. The Notes tab's own Quick Notes section (filter/search/browse all of them) is untouched — still the review surface, just no longer the only way in. Verified live: opened and submitted from the Manage tab (nowhere near Notes), confirmed the row landed in Supabase, confirmed it appeared correctly in the Notes tab's own list afterward, reverted.
**Quest Dashboard UX pass**, 4 items:
1. Stakes text was inconsistent between panels — left card showed the bare enum ("high"), right detail appended " stakes" itself ("high stakes"). One new `stakesLabel()` used by both now — "High Stakes" everywhere.
2. Suggested Level added to the left-panel card preview (a new `Lvl X-Y` badge) and moved to the top of the right panel, directly under the status/stakes badges — was previously the very last thing on the page, below the status selector.
3. Quest text (Hook/Summary/DM Notes/Reveals) now supports the same `[[type:id|Label]]` inline-link markup Field Guide already uses — same reasoning for manual-over-auto-detection as that build (short real names in this exact dataset risk false matches). New `sccLinkifyEscaped()` is a sibling of `sccFgLinkify()`, not a reuse — Field Guide content is trusted raw HTML and re-escapes its link label on the way out, quest text is already HTML-escaped by the time linkify runs, so re-escaping there would double-escape it. The DM's own example ("Osric") turned out to have no backing `world_characters` row at all — confirmed via SQL, not assumed — so linkify alone can't fix that specific case; flagged rather than fabricating a new NPC record unprompted. Verified the capability end-to-end with a real, temporary `[[character:drak|Drak]]` marker in a real quest's DM notes (Drak is real, confirmed via SQL) — clicked it, confirmed the lookup drawer opened showing his real card, then reverted the marker, matching this file's own established test-then-revert convention for Field Guide's link feature.
4. Answered directly, not just fixed: DM Notes and Reveals both being genuinely DM-eyes-only content, they used to get different visual weight (DM Notes: full border, icon, uppercase label; Reveals: thin left-accent bar, plain-text caption) purely because they were built at different times, not for any real reason. Reveals now renders through the same `.scc-dm-block` treatment as DM Notes. Related-entry tags also got a check for `collection === 'quests'` — a distinct gold "🔗 Related Quest" style, though no real quest currently links to another one to show it live; the schema also has no direction field (blocks vs. blocked-by), only a flat `related[]`, so a real quest-to-quest link would show as related but not as which way the dependency runs.
DM's own words: "Dormant implies the players worked on it and then forgot. Horrible UX." Investigated before renaming, per D2 — checked whether "blocked by completion of a separate quest" (the DM's own conditional framing) is a real mechanism anywhere in the schema: it isn't. `quests` has no prerequisite/`blocked_by`/`depends_on` column, and the 3 real dormant quests' `related[]` arrays only ever point at plain lore entities (cities/characters/organizations), never at other quests. The only real gating mechanism is `first_available` (a session number) — confirmed again against the same 3 quests from the round that first answered this question. Renamed the enum value itself, not just the display label — `status:'dormant'` → `'blocked'` on all 3 real rows, plus every code reference (filter dropdown, CSS class, `statusOrder`, `statusOptions`, `labels`) — keeping the stored value and the displayed word in sync was the whole point of this session's earlier `--cyan`-rename fix, so leaving `dormant` as the internal value while showing "Blocked" on screen would have reintroduced the exact same class of problem one message later. New color is red-orange (`#ff8066`) — reused, not invented: it's the exact hex Active used before this session recolored Active to green, freed up rather than left orphaned.

*Session Aug 15 2026 (same session, DM color-scheme rework driven directly by the DM's review of the published Color Ledger artifact — 4 hue reassignments, one status question answered, a real token rename, and a full pass of dead-code/duplicate cleanup across the color systems touched this session) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`, `field_guide_sessions` (Supabase data). Full detail in Decision Log.*
Direct, itemized DM instructions, all executed as given: Quest Stakes Low/Mid/High recolored to a plain green/gold/red traffic-light scale, Campaign stakes removed entirely (dead option, 0 real quests ever used it); Quest Status Active/Available/Completed recolored to green/blue/grey (Dormant's own real meaning — gated behind a `first_available` session number that hasn't arrived yet, confirmed against all 3 real dormant quests — answered directly, kept its own muted blue-grey tone since Completed just claimed plain grey); Field Guide box colors reworked — Lore (blue) dropped entirely (9 real boxes migrated to grey/no-color), Comedy moved from pink to a genuine bright yellow, Loot & Shop moved from terracotta to gold, Purple removed outright (0 real usage, freed for religion/deity per the DM's explicit reservation); NPC Relationship Neutral/Ally swapped to grey/blue. Also done, explicitly instructed rather than optional: `--cyan`/`--cyan-dim`/`--cyan-border` (holding magenta since last round, a real "variable name doesn't represent what it is" case) renamed to `--dm-only`/`--dm-only-dim`/`--dm-only-border` across every consumer; the duplicated NPC-relationship color object (2 identical copies) and the triplicated region-health color ladder (3 identical copies) both consolidated into single shared functions (`sccRelColor()`, `sccRegionHealthColor()`) matching the `sccConditionColor()` pattern from 2 rounds ago; confirmed-dead code removed outright — 20 unused `--scc-rel-*` tokens, 3 unused `--currency-*` tokens, the unused `SCC_REL_ICONS` object (also a latent C2 violation, literal emoji in an unused JS object), and the fully-dead `.scc-flag`/`.scc-flag--vareth`/`.scc-flag--formery` CSS (re-confirmed zero consumers before deleting, not assumed from memory).
Direct payoff of the consolidation work one round earlier: this was purely a token-value edit, 8 lines in tokens.css, zero changes to dm.html — confirmed via grep that no hardcoded DM-Only hex/rgba literal exists anywhere outside tokens.css anymore. Covered `--cyan`/`--cyan-dim`/`--cyan-border` (confirmed via grep these are 100% DM-Only-scoped, no other meaning shares them), `--scc-dm-only-bg/border/label` (`--scc-dm-only-text` already derives from `--cyan`, no separate edit needed), and `--dm-reveal-bg/border` (the quest "Reveals — DM eyes only" boxes, a DM-Only-meaning surface that was never wired through the other two token families). Verified live across 3 independently-rendering surfaces — NPC-card DM-only block, organization Quick Look DM-Only badge — both confirmed `rgb(255,0,243)`/`rgba(255,0,243,...)` via computed style and screenshot.

*Session Aug 15 2026 (same session, consolidating the repeat-bug pattern instead of patching each instance) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`. Full reasoning + all 5 fixes in Decision Log.*
**Architecture call: consolidate, don't just patch.** This exact session had already found and fixed the same bug shape four separate times (one meaning, several independent hardcoded copies, silently drifting) — condition colors, DM-Only, and Read-Aloud all had the shape again. Investigated feasibility before touching anything: all three were small, real-source-already-exists consolidations, not a rewrite. Did the consolidation instead of patching each spot.
**Conditions**: deleted two local hardcoded palettes (`COND_DATA` in the Live Session panel, `COND_COLORS` in the combat initiative tracker) in favor of one shared `sccConditionColor(name)` reading the real Supabase `conditions` table everything already fetches once at init. The tracker had disagreed with the real table on 10 of 15 core conditions and had no entries at all for the 3 Anavale-specific ones — now reads the same source as every other surface.
**Read Aloud**: new shared tokens (`--scc-read-aloud-bg/border/text`) in tokens.css; Field Guide's document box, its sidebar block, and the Creature/Item Quick Look card (previously gold, the one outlier) all point at them now.
**DM-Only**: the 3 newly-found stale surfaces (Manage tab's hidden badge, Field Guide sidebar's DM Notes block, Quest Dashboard's DM Notes) all repointed onto the existing shared `--scc-dm-only-*` tokens instead of getting their own fix. Quest DM Notes specifically now reuses the literal `.scc-dm-block` markup already used by the other 2 quest DM-notes surfaces, so there's one class instead of three.
Net effect: 69 lines removed, 48 added — real consolidation, not a like-for-like patch. Verified every fix live via computed style + screenshots.

*Session Aug 15 2026 (same session, building the 5 items investigated above) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`. All 5 built and verified live; full detail in Decision Log.*
**Field Guide DM-Only background fixed** — was hardcoded to the old pre-recolor cyan hex; now correctly the teal token, matching the border/label that were already right.
**DM-Only made structurally distinct, not just differently colored** — applied across all 3 real DM-Only surfaces (Field Guide box, NPC-card block, lookup-drawer block): a full border instead of left-accent-only, a genuinely filled background (up from ~6-7% opacity to 15%), and a bigger/bolder warning icon (a real `::before` corner badge on Field Guide boxes since their label text is DM-authored data; a resized `<span>` icon on the NPC-card title). Verified live on all 3 surfaces via computed style + screenshots — real, visible differentiation now, not dependent on precise hue perception.
**Loot/shop icon discoverability fixed** — renamed the bare "Edit" toggle to "Edit Document", and added a small "✎ Edit Box" link directly on every rendered box (all 48, any color) that opens the editor already scrolled to and highlighting that exact box's row — expanding whichever of the 15 sections holds it automatically. For orange (loot/shop) boxes specifically, also focuses the subtype field directly. Verified live end-to-end on Mira Pont's real shop box: one click landed in edit mode with the correct row highlighted and its subtype `<select>` genuinely focused.
**Relationship badges now auto-save — real data-loss bug fixed, highest priority this round.** Cycling a badge only ever mutated in-memory state before; now saves to Supabase automatically, matching how everything else in this app already behaves. A first version fired one network request per click and hit a real race condition under rapid clicking (confirmed live: 4 quick clicks meant to land on "deity" instead saved "hostile" — whichever response arrived last, not whichever was sent last) — fixed by debouncing to one save 400ms after the last click, so only the state a DM actually lands on ever gets written. Verified: rapid 4-click cycling now saves the correct final value every time, survives a full page reload, and a sanity check across Dran/Mira Pont (in addition to Oro) confirmed multiple NPCs' relationships coexist correctly without clobbering each other. Oro's now genuinely, persistently set to "deity" — the real QA reference this was always meant to be, not just a recommendation.
**Combat stats: AC now shown by default.** Added to the collapsed staging row next to CR/DEX — no click needed. Deeper stats stay behind the "Stats" toggle, per instruction. Verified live: Grusk Enforcer's row now reads "CR 0.5 · AC 11 · DEX +0"; a creature with no AC data correctly shows no AC badge at all rather than a placeholder.
Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 17 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty. Supabase confirmed clean afterward — only `session_notes.npc_relationships` differs from before this round, correctly `{"oro":"deity"}` and nothing else.

*Session Aug 15 2026 (new session, 2 confirmed builds + 5 fresh investigations) — `dm.html`. 3 real bugs found this round that the last two rounds' "verified correct" checks had missed — none of them color-perception issues, all concrete code/UX gaps. Full detail in Decision Log.*
**Built — Tagged Notes conceptual fix.** DM's own words: "I do not know what it is, what it is for, and how to use it" — root cause was that nothing on the page ever explained why a second note system exists right below the 5 structured recap boxes. Added a comparison strip between the two ("the 5 fields above are for your end-of-session write-up... Quick Notes below is for jotting something down the instant it happens") and renamed the section "Quick Notes." Replaced the bare "No tagged notes yet" empty state with a real-looking, clearly-flagged, fully-disabled worked example card (dashed border, reduced opacity, gold "EXAMPLE" badge) so a DM sees a finished note before making their first one — verified the filtered-to-nothing empty state ("No notes match") still shows plain, not the example.
**Built — Suggested Level input removed entirely.** Per explicit DM confirmation. The manual number input, its save function, and its Supabase write path are all gone — the computed range + one-line reasoning (built last round) is now the only display, fully read-only.
**Investigated — DM-Only color, re-tested fresh, found a real bug the last two "verified" passes missed.** Dran's actual NPC card is genuinely, fully teal (re-confirmed via computed style + screenshot). But the *Field Guide's* own DM-Only boxes (e.g. "EXACT RECOVERY SEQUENCE") have a real bug: the border and label text are correctly teal, but the box's *background fill* is still hardcoded to the old pre-recolor cyan hex (`rgba(34,211,238,0.07)`) — never updated when the border/label were fixed in an earlier round. Not yet fixed, reported first per instruction.
**Investigated — DM-Only vs Read-Aloud color distinguishability.** Confirmed the two hues sit only ~30° apart on the color wheel (teal ~172°, green ~142°) despite being "technically distinct" — a real, quantified closeness, not an exaggerated complaint. Given the app's palette is already fully claimed (blue=neutral, red=hostile/danger, purple=deity/high-stakes, pink=comedy, orange=loot, gold=brand), recommending a non-color differentiator (solid filled background + a larger/bolder icon) over hunting for a new hue, since every unclaimed hue would either collide with an existing meaning or need yet another repaint round.
**Investigated — loot/shop subtype icon "not working."** Confirmed via a genuine cold-start click-through (not direct function calls) that the feature itself works correctly — the real problem is pure discoverability. Nothing on the page ever uses the term "block editor" (that was my own description, not UI copy); the actual entry point is a small, unlabeled "Edit" button next to the Field Guide's session selector, and once inside, the DM has to expand the right one of 15 collapsible sections and scroll to find the specific box, with no search and no visible label on the subtype dropdown beyond "Icon: coin (default)." Recommending a visible hint near "Edit" plus better in-context labeling — not yet built.
**Investigated — Oro's deity badge "still blue."** Root cause found and it isn't a render bug: `sccCycleRelationship()`/`sccCycleRelationship2()` (the relationship badge click-to-cycle) only ever update the in-memory `SCC_NPC_RELATIONSHIPS` object — there is no auto-save. The value only reaches Supabase if the DM separately visits the Setup tab and clicks "Save Setup." Confirmed live: cycled Oro to "deity," watched the badge correctly turn purple in the moment, reloaded the page, and watched it silently revert to empty/neutral — confirmed via SQL that `npc_relationships` has been `{}` the entire time. This is inconsistent with almost everything else in the app (loot, notes, quest status all auto-save on their own action) and is very likely exactly what happened to the DM. Not yet fixed.
**Investigated — Combat stats, AC "not showing."** Confirmed AC *is* correctly included in the Stats panel's output when a creature has real data (verified live: "AC 11 (leather armor)" rendered correctly for Grusk Enforcer) — no data or rendering bug there. The real gap: the default collapsed staging row shows only Name/Type/CR/DEX — no AC, no HP, nothing — until the DM clicks "Stats," which is easy to miss mid-session. Recommending core stats (AC at minimum) show by default without a click, given the DM's stated real-table use case — not yet built.
Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 17 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

*Session Aug 15 2026 (same session, building the 5 items approved from the investigation round above) — `dm.html`. All 5 built and verified live; full detail in Decision Log.*
**Loot/shop subtype icons**: new `subtype` field on orange (loot/shop) Field Guide boxes, DM-assigned via a second dropdown in the block editor (Drink/Food/General Store/Weapons/Armor/Magic, only shown when the box's color is orange) — icon in the rendered box label switches accordingly (🍺/🍽/🛎/⚔/🛡/🔮); no subtype (including all pre-existing orange boxes) falls back to the original coin icon, so nothing already migrated needed touching. Verified live end-to-end: set Mira Pont's "AVAILABLE TO PURCHASE" box to General Store, saved to Supabase, confirmed 🛎 renders in the real document view, then reverted (removed the `subtype` key entirely via SQL) since this was a test pick, not a real DM categorization decision — block count still 140, nothing else touched.
**Roll Initiative copy**: button now reads "Start Combat — Sort by Initiative" with a `title` tooltip spelling out the real behavior ("Uses any initiative you've already typed as-is; rolls a d20+DEX for anyone left blank, then sorts the full list"). No behavior changed, `sccStartCombat()` untouched.
**Per-creature combat stat-blocks**: new "Stats" toggle next to "Loot" on every creature's staging row, opening a panel that renders only the fields actually populated on that specific creature — AC/HP/Speed/CR/ability scores/saves/skills/resistances/immunities/senses/languages/attacks/traits/actions/reactions/legendary actions, each line omitted entirely when null/empty, nothing invented or estimated. Verified live with both a fully-statted creature (Grusk Enforcer — full block rendered, screenshotted) and a lore-only creature (Fluffets — clean "No stat-block data recorded for this creature yet." message, no placeholders). Combat staging is pure client-side state, cleared after testing, nothing to revert server-side.
**Tagged Notes fixes**: (a) creating a note now always resets the active category filter and search text to "All"/empty, so a freshly-created note can never be hidden by whatever filter was active — chose reset-over-auto-switch since auto-switching would itself hide whatever the DM was previously looking at. (b) `sccAddTagNote()` now requests `return=representation` (not the shared helper's `return=minimal`) to get the new row's real id back immediately, auto-focuses that exact card's title input, and shows a "Note added ✓" toast on success. Verified live against the exact reported failure mode: set the active filter to "Player Actions," picked "DM-Only" in the new-note category select, clicked the real "+ New Note" button — screenshotted before (filtered view, 0 notes, "No notes match") and after (filter auto-reset to "All (2)," new note visible at top with a focused, ready-to-type title field). Both test notes deleted afterward via SQL (matched on blank title+content, confirmed nothing else in that table matched).
**Oro logged as the deity QA reference** (no code change) — see the investigation entry below for detail; recorded here so future QA involving deity-relationship NPCs starts from a known-real example instead of re-deriving it.
Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 17 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty (one real near-miss caught before it shipped: a `✓` landed as a literal character in a new `showDMToast()` call via the same Edit-tool escape-normalization issue on record from prior sessions — fixed via the established Python exact-string-replacement script, confirmed via grep afterward).

*Session Aug 15 2026 (new session, structured investigate-then-report QA round) — `dm.html`. Environment check first, then 7 investigate-and-report items, 2 build items. Full findings in Decision Log; summary here for continuity.*
**Environment check, confirmed clean**: the local-dev banner from the prior round is real and renders correctly on a fresh server/reload — rules out "banner never built" as an explanation for any "still looks wrong" report this round.
**Dran's DM-Only box "yellow/gold" report — did not reproduce.** Expanded Dran's real NPC card live and read `getComputedStyle()` on the box border, title, and all 36 internal colored elements (icons/labels) — every single one resolves to `rgb(45,212,191)`, the correct teal, confirmed by screenshot too. No third hidden color rule exists in the current code. Most likely explanation: browser cache of an old `dm.html`/`tokens.css` on the DM's machine (an on-record recurring issue in this file), a stale local checkout, or the actual live GitHub Pages site (which has none of this branch's fixes, since this branch has never been merged to whatever branch Pages deploys from).
**Loot/shop category icons — no existing sub-categorization to build on.** `field_guide_sessions` block schema stores only a single semantic `color` field per box (`grey/green/cyan/blue/purple/orange/pink`) — there's no drink/food/general/weapon/armor/magic distinction anywhere in the data, and the block editor's box row is just a Label input + one Color dropdown. Recommended, not built: add a `subtype` field to orange (loot/shop) boxes specifically, surfaced as a small dropdown in the block editor next to the existing color select, DM-assigned per box — explicitly not auto-detected from box text (too unreliable to trust silently, per instruction).
**Roll Initiative — real behavior confirmed via `sccStartCombat()`.** It does NOT override manually-typed init values: for each combatant it reads the init input field, uses the typed value as-is if present, and only rolls a random d20+DEX for blanks — then sorts everyone (typed and rolled together) by initiative descending. So a DM who's typed every value gets a pure sort with nothing rolled; a DM who's typed none gets everyone rolled. Recommended clearer copy: "Start Combat — Sort/Roll Initiative" or a tooltip reading "Uses your typed values; rolls a d20+DEX for anything left blank, then sorts" — current copy ("Roll Initiative") overstates what happens when values are already filled in.
**Creature stat-block coverage — same 4/52 creatures for everything, confirmed field-by-field, not assumed.** Queried every field the DM listed: type/AC/CR/size/all 6 ability scores/languages/speed/senses/attacks/traits/HP are all exactly 4/52 (Grey Fluffets, Still Frogs, Grusk Enforcer, Grusk Color Extractor — same 4 every time). Deeper into those same 4: skill proficiencies 2/52, saving throws 1/52, actions 2/52, reactions 0/52, damage resistances/immunities/condition immunities all 0/52, legendary actions 0/52. No `spells` column exists at all — nothing in `attacks`/`actions`/`traits` jsonb for any creature contains spellcasting data. Weapon/damage data lives inside `attacks` jsonb (name/damage dice/type/attack bonus) — populated for the same 4. Also confirmed the combat encounter builder today only ever pulls `cr`/`hp`/`dexMod` from a creature into a combatant object — nothing else from the stat block reaches the UI at all currently, so a full stat-block display would be new UI work on top of the data gap, not just a data-entry task. Realistic picture for the DM: full-detail combat stat blocks are viable today for exactly 4 creatures; everything else needs real data entry first (AC/HP/type/size/ability scores at minimum) before any display work matters.
**Tagged Notes confusion — root-caused, not just "confusing."** Walked the actual click path: `sccAddTagNote()` posts a blank note and re-fetches, ordered `updated_at.desc`, so a new note *should* land at the very top — but two things make it feel broken. (1) If a category filter chip is currently active and the DM creates a note in a *different* category via the dropdown, the new note is created successfully in Supabase but immediately filtered out of view — the DM sees literally nothing happen. (2) Nothing auto-focuses the new note's title field or scrolls it into view or shows any success confirmation — it just silently appears (or doesn't, per #1) after a brief loading flash. Recommended: reset/match the active filter to the new note's category on create, auto-focus the title input immediately after render, and add a brief visible confirmation (toast or highlight) — not a cosmetic tweak, a real interaction-completeness fix.
**Deity NPC for QA reference, confirmed real.** `world_characters` has 5 real `category:'god'` rows — Oro, Nara, Thyun, Solvara, Grak — but relationship state (`ally/neutral/caution/hostile/deity`) is a per-session DM-toggled cycle stored in `session_notes.npc_relationships`, not tied to NPC identity; that field is currently `{}` (empty) for the one real session, so no NPC anywhere is *currently* set to "deity." Recommended for future QA: add Oro (most prominent, benevolent, widely-worshipped) to a session roster and click his relationship badge 4 times to cycle to "deity."
**Built (no investigation gate): Suggested Level range + reasoning.** New pure `sccSuggestedLevelRange(quest)` — never writes `quest.suggested_level`, only computes a display-only range + one-line reasoning shown next to the still-manually-editable field. Baseline from `first_available` ("session-N" → lvl N to N+1, modeling roughly one level per session), nudged +1 for `high` stakes / +2 for `campaign` stakes (`low`/`mid` unchanged), widened to include a related creature's CR-derived level when real CR data exists on a `related[]` creature entry (checked: today only one quest, The Greytalon, has any creature relation at all, and that creature's CR is null — so the CR nudge is real, wired, and correctly inert on all 10 current quests until CR data exists). Verified live on 3 real quests via direct function calls and the rendered UI (e.g. "Suggested range: lvl 1-2 (session 1, low stakes)" next to an untouched, still-blank "Not set" input).
**Built (no investigation gate): Combat tab layout.** Moved "+ Add All Players" into the top row next to the search box (was a separate row below the staging list). Added `margin-top:0.75rem` spacing both above and below the combatant list so it's no longer flush against the search row and the button row. "Start Combat — Roll Initiative" pushed to the far right of its row via `margin-left:auto` (Clear All stays left). Verified live with 2 real combatants staged, screenshotted, then cleared (client-side-only state, nothing to revert server-side).
Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 17 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

*Session Aug 14 2026 (new session, local-dev environment banner) — `dm.html`. Added a persistent, impossible-to-miss red banner at the very top of the page, auto-detected purely from `window.location.hostname` — no manual per-checkout setup, no build step. Shows "⚠ LOCAL DEV BUILD — NOT LIVE — {hostname}:{port}" whenever the hostname isn't exactly `carlosknightiii.github.io`; silent (no banner, zero layout shift) on the real live site. True git branch/folder name isn't detectable from a static HTML file served with no backend — confirmed this isn't achievable client-side rather than faking it, so the fallback is hostname:port exactly as the fallback the ask specified. Push-down handled via a `--dm-env-banner-h` CSS variable set from the banner's real measured height (not a hardcoded pixel guess), applied to both `.gate-overlay` (position:fixed, needs an explicit `top` override) and `.dm-app` (normal flow, needs `margin-top`) so neither the password gate nor the app header ever sits under the banner. Verified live: banner renders correctly on `localhost:PORT` with the exact right text, gate card and app header both cleanly offset below it with pixel-exact alignment (33px banner height, 33px `--dm-env-banner-h`, 33px `.dm-app` top — zero gap, zero overlap), zero console errors. Live-domain behavior verified by calling the exact same condition function used in the shipped code against `'carlosknightiii.github.io'` directly (returns `false`, correctly hidden) rather than by actually deploying, since this session works on a feature branch that was never merged to the branch GitHub Pages deploys from. One real near-miss caught by the standing pre-push check: the ⚠ warning-sign character landed as a literal glyph in the new inline script (Edit tool normalizing the escape on write, same recurring issue on record) — fixed via the established Python exact-string-replacement workaround before it ever reached a commit.*

*Session Aug 14 2026 (new session, DM's second QA pass — found real gaps in the first pass's own coverage, all investigated fresh and fixed) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`. Explicit instruction not to trust prior "verified" claims; re-investigated everything from scratch.*
**Colors — the first purple/blue/orange audit genuinely wasn't exhaustive.** A fresh, complete grep of `var(--purple)`/`#c084fc` (not the narrower search used last time) turned up a 4th real usage the first pass missed entirely: `--dm-stakes-high-text: var(--purple)` — quest "high stakes" badges, reached through a token indirection (`.q-stakes-high` → `--dm-stakes-high-text` → `--purple`) the first audit's direct-usage grep didn't follow. Recommended and (confirmed) implemented: reuse `--orange-vivid` (same hex Combat's "high" difficulty already uses) rather than inventing a 10th distinct hue in an increasingly crowded palette — the two badges never appear in the same view, so sharing a hue for the same underlying idea ("elevated severity") is a feature, not a collision. The reported DM-Only-vs-Vareth collision **did not reproduce** — investigated every real Vareth-related visual element (the location-card VARETH badge is a plain grey `#a0a0b0`, unrelated to teal; two `.scc-flag--vareth`/`.scc-flag--formery` CSS rules exist but are dead code, never referenced by any HTML/JS) and found no actual overlap; reported this back rather than inventing a fix for a collision that isn't there.
**A much bigger gap: two entire red "DM-only" surfaces the first pass never found.** Grepping the actual red hex (`#d62b2b`, `#f87171`) instead of relying on memory of what had already been fixed turned up: (1) `.scc-npc2-dm-block` — the large "DM Only — Do Not Read Aloud" card on NPC/location panel cards, with its nested "Secret"/"Notes"/"Vareth Presence" row labels (`.scc-npc2-red`) — a completely separate rendering surface (NPC/Location panel expanded cards, not the lookup drawer) that neither of the two earlier DM-Only recolor passes had touched, still hardcoded to `#d62b2b`. (2) Quest "Reveals" boxes (`.quest-reveal-item`, explicitly labeled "DM eyes only" in its own header) — also still on the pre-recolor `rgba(248,113,113,...)` red. Both converted to the teal system. Separately, `.scc-dm-text` (the body-text rule inside the `.scc-dm-block` box fixed last round) turned out to still hardcode `color: var(--dm-red)` — the earlier fix only touched the shared `--scc-dm-only-*` token set for background/border/label, never noticing this one rule bypassed that token set entirely — exactly explaining the reported "teal border, red/brown body text" inconsistency. Fixed by pointing it at `--scc-dm-only-text` (a token that already existed, previously unused).
**Suggested Level**: confirmed genuinely interactive (real `<input type="number">`, not disabled/readonly, exposed correctly as an accessible textbox) — the "looks non-interactive" read was about visual affordance, not function. Added a dashed border while empty (`:placeholder-shown`) plus a hover cue, so an unset field visibly invites a click instead of reading as static label text.
**Field Guide bleeding into every Session sub-tab — a real, confirmed CSS bug, not intentional.** `#scc-panel-fieldguide { display: flex; ... }` was a bare ID rule with higher specificity than the base `.scc-panel { display: none; }`, so it stayed visually present (search bar, filter chips, permanently-stuck "Loading Field Guide…") underneath every other Session sub-panel regardless of which one was actually `.active`. Gated the rule on `.active` like every other panel; confirmed both that the leak is gone on Quests/Combat/Notes and that Field Guide itself still renders correctly (grid layout, real document content) when it is the active tab.
**Combat roster quick-add chips**: confirmed they hard-disabled after one click (`SCC_COMBATANTS.some(id match)` guard, plus the render logic swapping to a permanently non-interactive `<span>`) — unlike the search box, which the previous round fixed. Made the chips stay clickable indefinitely, replacing the old checkmark-and-disable with a live `×N` count, so the DM keeps the "already used" signal without losing the ability to add more.
**Tagged Notes**: moved the whole section (hint text, add-row, search/filters, list) to sit between the 5 structured recap fields and the Save/Copy/Session-Completed controls — during-session content before end-of-session actions, not after. Split the cramped single-row toolbar into a visually distinct gold-boxed "add a note" row (category select + primary-styled "+ New Note" button) sitting above a plainer search/filter row, so adding a note reads as the section's primary action instead of one more filter control.
Verified every fix live (screenshots + computed-style checks, not assumed), zero console errors, all test data cleaned up. Pre-push checks clean — the only emoji hits in the diff were 3 pre-existing characters relocated by the Tagged Notes reposition (confirmed present in the prior commit via `git show HEAD:dm.html`, not newly introduced).*

*Session Aug 14 2026 (new session, DM QA pass — 3 color collisions investigated + fixed, 6 bugs investigated + fixed, 2 combat features built) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`, `field_guide_sessions` data. Full findings + all fixes in Decision Log; summary here for continuity.*
**Colors (investigated fresh, reported, confirmed before touching hex):** purple's 3-way collision (deity/Combat-high/Field Guide comedy, all literally `#c084fc`) resolved by keeping purple for deity (rarest, best semantic fit) and moving Combat-high to vivid orange `#fb923c` (freed up by the loot/shop change below — gives a real green→gold→orange→red difficulty ramp) and Field Guide comedy to pink `#f472b6` (a hue with zero prior semantic meaning). DM-Only's cyan — now confirmed reading as "blue" for the *second* time against Field Guide lore boxes and the NPC "neutral" badge — moved to a true teal (`--cyan` token redefined to `#2dd4bf`) *and* paired with a 💠 icon added to every player_facing "DM Only" text badge (character/organization/region/religion/species), not just Field Guide boxes, since hue alone had already failed once. Loot/shop's alarm-toned orange desaturated to a terracotta `#d9a66c` (kept the hue family rather than switching, per the DM's own instinct minus gold, which is already overloaded 5+ ways) — a 🪙 coin icon was added to loot/shop box labels unconditionally, per instruction, regardless of the color outcome.
**Bugs:** Suggested Level's blank input was confirmed as-designed (all 10 quests genuinely have no value set) with a weak empty state — fixed to a real "Not set" placeholder. Mira Pont's/Goldenway's missing Shop sections were confirmed to be the vendor_stock cleanup from the original build's own verification pass (0 rows for everyone) — the section and its add-stock search box both render and work correctly right now; no code bug found or needed. Nations were reachable via global search but mislabeled "Type: Location" (a real, previously-flagged-but-unfixed bug in the city/POI/nation lookup branch's `_srcType` check) and missing nation-specific fields entirely — fixed the type-label logic to a reliable direct-array-membership check and added Government/Capital/Beliefs/Gigglegloom Relationship fields. Item (and Quest/Creature/Organization) DM Notes/Secret blocks were still rendering in the pre-recolor red — the shared `--scc-dm-only-*` token set was never touched by the original cyan recolor pass (only the separate badge color was) — fixed once at the token level, fixing all 6 call sites simultaneously. Manage tab name truncation was a real CSS bug: 3 stacked hardcoded pixel caps (220px/240px duplicate `.cm-td-name` rules plus a 180px `.cm-name-link` cap) truncating names regardless of available space — removed all three, also cleaning up the genuine duplicate rule this file's own conventions forbid.
**Features:** Combat's search-based creature add was silently blocked from adding the same creature twice (`sccPickerSelect`'s dedup guard, meant for the other 4 picker types, had no real purpose for combatants — `SCC_COMBATANTS` is array-indexed everywhere, never looked up by id) — carved out a combatant-specific bypass so 3x Grusk Enforcer works via repeated search-and-click. Added a "Clear All" button next to Add All Players, confirm-gated, wired into the same show/hide logic as the other encounter-builder buttons.
Verified live end-to-end for every item (see Decision Log), zero console errors, all test data (vendor_stock rows, combat staging) cleaned up afterward. Pre-push checks clean — one real near-miss caught and fixed before it shipped: the DM-Only icon edit initially landed as a literal emoji character in 5 JS string literals (Edit tool normalized the `💠` escape into the literal glyph on write) — caught by the standard post-edit emoji grep, fixed via a Python script for exact byte-level replacement, per this file's own established C2 workaround.*

*Session Aug 14 2026 (same session as the investigation below — DM confirmed the block-based storage shape and the manual `[[type:id|Label]]` link syntax — built and shipped) — `dm.html`, new `field_guide_sessions` table. This closes out the last remaining goal from the Aug 12 2026 DM Tools UX/IA investigation. New `field_guide_sessions` table (`session_number unique`, `blocks jsonb`, RLS mirroring the anon-all-access pattern), replacing the fully static 505-line hardcoded Session 1 document with a data-driven renderer. Session 1's real content was parsed out of the old hardcoded HTML with a one-off Python/BeautifulSoup script (scratch-only, not committed) into 140 ordered blocks — 48 boxes, 30 h2, 20 standalone tables (2 more genuinely nested inside orange shop boxes, matching the investigation's own finding), 14 h1, 15 p, 4 italic, 4 lists, plus title/subtitle/tagline/meta/footer — and inserted as one row via direct SQL. **Verification was rigorous, not visual**: fetched the real *pre-migration* `dm.html` (via `git show HEAD:dm.html`) into a hidden fragment, extracted its `textContent`, extracted the new block-rendered version's `textContent` the same way, and confirmed they're identical once whitespace is stripped entirely (34,733 non-whitespace characters, exact match) — the only difference was insignificant block-boundary spacing from the old source's manual indentation, not content. New `sccFgRenderBlock()`/`sccFgRenderDoc()` reconstruct the exact same CSS classes the static markup used (`scc-fg-doc-h1`, `scc-fg-box scc-fg-box--{color}`, `scc-fg-table` + variants, etc.) so zero CSS changed. A new session selector (`<select>` sourced from the already-loaded `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` — no new fetch needed) sits above the document; switching it also fixes the real pre-existing bug the investigation found — `SCC_FG_DATA` (the sidebar's session-scoped NPC/creature/location/item cache) is now explicitly reset to `null` on every session switch, so the sidebar actually refetches for the newly selected session instead of staying stuck on whichever session loaded first. A DM-facing editor groups blocks by their nearest preceding `h1` into collapsible sections (same shape as tonight's `session_scenarios` editor, but with a richer per-row type set: text inputs for headings, HTML-capable textareas for prose, and genuinely structured row/header editors for tables — including the 2 real box-nested-table cases — rather than raw-HTML editing anywhere). Inline entity links use manual `[[type:id|Label]]` markup (per the DM's confirmed choice over auto-detection), parsed at render time into a span wired to the existing `sccOpenLookup()` drawer — paired with a link-insert helper reusing the `.scc-picker-search`/`.scc-picker-dropdown` UI already established this session, so the DM finds the right entity by name instead of typing raw ids. Verified live end-to-end: the byte-for-byte content check above; a real link inserted via the actual search-and-click UI (linked "Pellam Voss" text to the real NPC), saved, and clicked in read view — correctly opened the real lookup drawer showing Pellam Voss's full card; the empty-session state (`SCC_FG_DOC_SESSION_NUM` pointed at a session with no row) correctly rendered "No Field Guide written for Session 2 yet" instead of erroring; the session selector correctly shows just "Session 1" today with no crash. All test edits (the inserted link) reverted afterward, confirmed via SQL the real document is back to its exact 140-block migrated state. Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean (raw parse, `new Function()` on all 16 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty — the box labels' real emoji characters, e.g. 💠, are Supabase data fetched at runtime, not literal characters in `dm.html`'s own JS source, so C2 doesn't apply to them).*

*Session Aug 13 2026 (new session, Field Guide multi-session support — investigation + 2 open design questions, no code changed) — confirmed the current Session 1 Field Guide document (`dm.html` ~3236-3740) is a single, fully hardcoded 505-line HTML block with real richness: 14 numbered `scc-fg-doc-h1` sections, 30 `scc-fg-doc-h2` subsections, 48 colored callout boxes across 6 CSS color variants (green/yellow(renders cyan)/blue/purple/orange/grey), 22 tables (2 genuinely nested inside orange "shop" boxes — Mira Pont's stock, the Inn's drinks — the rest standalone 2-3 column key/value or scenario tables), 4 bullet lists, plus document-level title/subtitle/tagline/meta/footer text. The color legend is itself a real, migratable sentence inside the grey "HOW TO USE THIS GUIDE" box — not app chrome, so it isn't being hardcoded separately, it moves with Session 1's content like everything else. Confirmed the sidebar's session-scoped fetch (`sccRenderFieldGuide()`, keys off `SCC_SESSION.session_number` to filter NPCs/creatures/locations/items down to what's assigned in Setup) is real and reusable, parameterizable to any session number with a small change — but it's completely disconnected from the static document today, and a real pre-existing bug was found in the process: `SCC_FG_DATA` is cached forever and never reset to `null` anywhere, so the sidebar wouldn't actually refresh even if something did point it at a different session. Recommended storage: a new `field_guide_sessions` table, one row per `session_number`, holding the entire document as one ordered `blocks` jsonb array (title/subtitle/tagline/meta/h1/h2/italic/p/list/table/box/footer block types) rather than a handful of named columns — reasoning: the document's real content is too heterogeneous (arbitrary mixes of prose, boxes, and nested tables per section) for a clean typed-column schema, and a flat ordered block list is both the safest way to guarantee lossless migration (each block stores its real inner HTML/text close to verbatim) and a natural fit for a collapsible-by-h1-section editor mirroring tonight's `session_scenarios` pattern. Tables get genuine structured sub-fields (header row + data rows, both top-level and nested inside a box) rather than raw HTML, since they're a large, editable-worthy fraction of the real content (NPC reference cards, price lists). One open design question sent for confirmation: inline entity links — manual `[[type:id|Label]]` markup (DM-controlled, paired with a searchable link-insert helper so the DM doesn't need to memorize IDs) recommended over auto-detecting entity names in prose text, since auto-linking risks false positives on short, common-word-adjacent real names in this exact dataset (Gerald, Grey, Vel, Fess, Mira) and this app has consistently favored explicit DM-authored links (`quest.related[]`) over inferred ones everywhere else. Waiting on confirmation of the storage shape and the link mechanism before writing any schema or UI code, per instruction. Full findings in Decision Log.*

*Session Aug 13 2026 (same session as the investigation below — DM confirmed Tier/Category/Region dropdowns plus the widened text search, explicitly skipped the CR sort toggle — built and shipped) — `dm.html`. Scoped exactly to what was confirmed. New `.scc-combat-filters-bar` (Tier/Category/Region `<select>`s + Reset) added above the existing combat-add search box, options populated live from `CREATURES`/`REGIONS` each time the Combat panel opens (`sccCombatPopulateFilterOptions()`, same never-hardcode convention as the global search filter bar 2 sessions ago) — Region shows real region names (Caparia/Jugabi/Nombi/Sohot) via a `REGIONS` id→name lookup since `creatures.regions` only stores ids. Built a dedicated `sccCombatantSearch()` rather than extending the shared `sccPickerSearch()` used by 4 other picker types (location/npc/quest/item) — kept those completely untouched. New search matches name **and** `habitat`/`description`/`tags` text (previously name-only), and applies the 3 filters to creatures only — a PC has no tier/category/region so it's correctly excluded from results whenever any filter is active, no special-casing needed beyond a `filtersActive` flag. Also replicated the "browse with an empty query once a filter is active" behavior the global search got 2 sessions ago — a DM can now see e.g. "every Tier: humanoid creature" with nothing typed. No CR filter or sort was built at all, per the DM's explicit choice to skip it this pass. New `.scc-audit-mode .scc-combat-filters-bar { display:none }` rule added alongside the existing hidden-in-audit-mode controls, so the filter bar disappears the same way the search box already does when viewing a locked past session. Verified live: all 3 dropdowns populate with the real values (11 tiers, 12 categories, 4 regions); Tier=humanoid with no typed query correctly returned exactly the 2 real humanoid creatures and zero PCs; Region=jugabi returned real jugabi-tagged creatures; typing "rainforest" with no filters active correctly surfaced 3 real creatures via their habitat text, none of which match by name (confirms the widened search is doing real work, not coincidental); a filtered result (Mirrenflow Puffalumps, Category=aquatic) was clicked through the real UI and correctly landed in the real encounter roster with the difficulty badge updating, then removed; audit-mode hiding confirmed via computed style. Zero console errors, zero Supabase writes needed cleanup (combat staging is pure client-side state until "Start Combat," unchanged by this pass). Pre-push checks clean (raw parse, `new Function()` on all 16 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty).*

*Session Aug 13 2026 (new session, Combat Tracker filters — investigation only, no code changed, waiting on go-ahead for a recommended filter set) — confirmed the CR sparsity is real and unchanged (4/52 creatures), and confirmed it's the *same* 4 creatures that have any real stat-block data at all (`ac`/`hp_max`/`speed_ft`/`creature_type`/`size`/`creature_alignment`/`xp_value` are all also exactly 4/52 — Grey Fluffets, Still Frogs, Grusk Enforcer, Grusk Color Extractor — everything else is a lore-only entry with no D&D mechanics). Recommended CR as a **sort** (Name/CR toggle, no-CR creatures sort last), not a filter — a CR dropdown would only ever bucket the same 4 creatures into 3 values, no real utility. Bigger finding: `tier` and `category` (already flagged as usable) are genuinely 52/52 populated with clean, well-distributed enums (tier: 11 values, common 21 down to unseen/unknown 1 each; category: 12 values, region-exclusive 9 down to the-unseen 1) — real, working dropdown filters, just never wired into the Combat tab's own `sccPickerSearch('combatant', ...)` box (they're already exposed on the *global* header search from 2 sessions ago, a completely separate UI surface — this pass is additive, not a duplicate). `regions[]` (array, 52/52 populated) resolves to exactly the 4 real region ids (caparia/nombi/sohot/jugabi) confirmed against the `regions` table, each creature tagged to 2–3 on average — confirmed as a genuinely usable "find creatures near here" filter, and confirmed as the correct substitute for "nation": nations belong to regions (15 real nations, each pinned to exactly one of the 4 regions via `nations.region`), but creatures carry zero nation-level tagging, only the coarser region level — building a nation filter would need new schema, building a region filter doesn't. `habitat` is also 52/52 populated but every single value is unique free-text lore prose (52 distinct values, confirmed via a full group-by) — useless as a dropdown, but real material to fold into the search box's own text match (currently name-only) alongside `tags`/`description`, which would make typing e.g. "rainforest" or "vareth" actually surface something without any new UI at all. `tags[]` (52/52 populated) is real but overlaps heavily with tier/category plus a long tail of one-off flavor tags (`omen`, `prophetic`, `wanderkeep-indicator`) — not clean enough for its own dropdown, but same free-text-search value as habitat. Confirmed (not re-derived from the task's own framing) that `world_characters` has zero HP/AC/CR-shaped columns — NPCs-in-combat is out of scope this pass per instruction, not touched. Recommended filter set sent for go-ahead: Tier dropdown, Category dropdown, Region dropdown, a Name/CR sort toggle, and widening the existing search box to match habitat/description/tags text — nothing invented for army/nation/race/level, since none of those exist as real, populated, filter-worthy data today. Full findings in Decision Log.*

*Session Aug 13 2026 (same investigation thread as below, DM answered all 4 design questions — fixed per-vendor list, no new vendor flag, buy-only, woven into the lookup drawer — built and shipped) — `dm.html`, new `vendor_stock` table. Scoped exactly to the 4 confirmed decisions, nothing more. New `vendor_stock` table (`entity_collection`/`entity_id`/`item_id`/`price_gp_override`/`quantity`/`notes`/`sort_order`), RLS mirroring `session_note_entries`' anon-all-access pattern, `updated_at` trigger reusing that same table's existing `update_session_notes_timestamp()` function rather than writing a duplicate. No new flag on `pois`/`organizations` — vendor-hood is purely "has rows in `vendor_stock`," per the DM's own confirmed answer to question 2. `sccInit()`'s existing `Promise.all` gained one more `sccFetch('vendor_stock', ...)` call into a new `window.VENDOR_STOCK` global, same pattern as `species`/`items`/`conditions`. New `sccVendorShopSectionHtml(collection, entityId)` renders a "Shop" section — shared buyer `<select>` (real `SCC_PLAYERS` roster), one row per stocked item (thumbnail, name, catalog price shown alongside an editable price-override input and an editable quantity input — both blank = "use catalog price"/"unlimited," matching the confirmed fixed-list-with-overrides design), a qty-to-buy input + Buy button (hidden, replaced with "Out of stock"/"No price set"/"No players" when purchase isn't currently possible), and a search-to-add-stock box reusing the exact `.scc-picker-search`/`.scc-picker-dropdown`/`.scc-picker-option` markup/CSS `sccLootItemSearch()` already uses — zero new dropdown CSS needed. Wired into `sccOpenLookup()`'s existing `city` branch (line ~13817) and `organization` branch (line ~13968), appended after each branch's existing Tags section. **Real gating bug caught before shipping, not after**: the `city` branch's own `loc` variable is pulled from a fresh `CITIES.concat(POIS).concat(NATIONS)` built inline in that function — it does **not** carry the `_srcType` tag a separate, unrelated function attaches to its own independently-built array elsewhere in the file (confirmed via grep before trusting it), so `loc._srcType === 'poi'` — the check the branch's own pre-existing (and, it turns out, already-silently-broken) "POI" type-label logic relies on — would never actually be true here. Used a direct, reliable check instead: `POIS.some(function(p){ return p.id === loc.id; })`, scoping the Shop section strictly to real POI rows and correctly excluding cities/nations that happen to render through the same branch. Buy transaction (`sccVendorBuy()`) mirrors `sccAwardCoins()`'s gold-arithmetic pattern and `sccAwardItem()`'s inventory-insert shape exactly, composed together with an affordability check (blocks and toasts if the player's real `gold_cp` can't cover it, zero writes) and a stock-quantity check (blocks if buying more than what's left) before either `sccPatch`ing gold down or `sccPost`ing the real inventory row (`dm_granted:false`, since this is a real purchase, not a DM grant) — decrements `vendor_stock.quantity` afterward only when it isn't null (unlimited stock never depletes). Add/remove/edit-stock and the buy transaction all end by re-invoking `sccOpenLookup()` on the same entity so the drawer reflects the fresh state immediately, matching the re-render-in-place pattern this file already uses elsewhere (tag notes, scenario editor). No sell-back path built at all, per the confirmed buy-only scope. Verified live end-to-end against real Supabase data via a fresh local server (a stale server was already running on the old dev port from an earlier session — started a new one on a new port and confirmed via `curl` before trusting it, per the standing lesson): opened Mira Pont's Sundry Goods POI (the same entity whose informal `"shop"` tag drove the original investigation) through the real search-and-click UI, added a real Torch to its stock via the real search box, confirmed the row landed in Supabase with `price_gp_override:null, quantity:null` (catalog price, unlimited). Bought 1 as Hana Sable through the real Buy button — confirmed via SQL her `gold_cp` dropped 900→899 (1cp, matching Torch's real 0.01gp price) and a real `inventory` row was inserted. Set a deliberately unaffordable price override (10000gp) and clicked Buy again — confirmed via SQL zero writes happened (gold and inventory both unchanged), toast correctly blocked it. Set quantity to 0 — confirmed the row correctly flipped to "Out of stock" with the Buy control hidden. Opened a real Organization (Goldenway) and confirmed its own independent Shop section renders correctly scoped to `entity_collection:'organizations'`. Opened a real City (Gobblewump Crossing, confirmed via `POIS.some()` that it is NOT a real POI) and confirmed the Shop section correctly does not render — the POI-scoping fix holds. Zero console errors throughout. All test data (the stock row, the inventory row, Hana's gold) reverted via SQL afterward, confirmed clean via a final read. Pre-push checks clean (raw parse, `new Function()` on all 16 extracted script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty).*

*Session Aug 12 2026 (new session, Vendor/Shop — investigation only, no code changed, waiting on 4 real design questions) — confirmed fully net-new as expected: zero vendor/shop flag or field anywhere on `pois`/`organizations` (`poi_type` real values are town/dark-zone/site/landmark/waystation — nothing shop-like), Mira Pont's real shop stock exists only as hardcoded HTML prose in the Field Guide document, not linked to the real `items` catalog at all. One soft signal found: her POI row already carries a free-text `"shop"` tag in its `tags` array — informal, not structured, but a real existing DM convention worth reusing rather than inventing a parallel one. Confirmed `items.cost_gp` (127/139 non-null, 18 of those are genuinely 0 — mostly cosmetic accessory-slot rows from the appearance-catalog migration, not shop-worthy — and 12 carry real fractional PHB-accurate prices like 0.01/0.05/0.5 gp) and `character_sheet.gold_cp` (integer copper-pieces, `renderGold()`/`onCurrencyBlur()` in `sheet/index.html`) as the two real quantities a purchase would need to reconcile. Bigger finding than expected: the low-level transaction mechanics already exist in `dm.html`, just never composed into a priced flow — `sccAwardCoins()` already does `character_sheet.gold_cp` arithmetic via `sccPatch()`, `sccAwardItem()` already POSTs a real `inventory` row (`character_id, token, item_id, quantity, dm_granted`) — a "buy" action is these same two calls with a cost check and subtraction instead of addition, not new plumbing. Neither call is wrapped in a real DB transaction (two independent REST calls) — a pre-existing pattern, not a new risk this would introduce, but worth having agreed with the DM going in. `sheet/index.html`'s `runInventorySearch()`/`selectCatalogItem()` (the +Add Item modal) is the closest reusable browse-and-buy UI pattern — name+category search against `items`, qty input, one-click add. 4 real design questions sent to the DM (stock model, whether a new vendor flag is needed, buy-only vs. buy+sell, and UI placement) — waiting on answers before writing any schema or code, per instruction. Full findings in Decision Log.*

*Session Aug 14 2026 (new session — Handoff B of two, Class tab: Spell Slots redesign + Spells Prepared + Spells Learned) — replaced the old pip-based Spell Slots stopgap with the real design: folded Spell Slots and a new Spells Prepared tracker into the same "Features" accordion Handoff A built, as sibling groups next to Class-Specific, plus a new full-width Spells Learned browser card — `sheet/index.html`, `css/tokens.css`, `classes.spell_slot_progression`/`classes.prepared_count_formula` (new columns), `character_sheet.prepared_spells`/`learned_spells` (new columns), and a full data-population pass on `spells.school`/`casting_time`/`range`/`components`/`duration`/`classes` (all 6 were 100% empty across all 414 rows going into this session). The doc's own 5 open questions were resolved via one round of DM answers, and one answer directly overrode my recommendation: DM chose "Category" = `school` (Abjuration/Conjuration/etc.) over my recommended Gigglegloom `types`, which meant `school` had to be populated for real before Spells Learned could filter by it at all — DM's follow-up instruction was to populate the *entire* empty-column set with real 2024 PHB data, not stub or skip it. Delegated the 414-spell research to parallel background agents (split by level, cross-verified against aidedd.org/dndbeyond.com/roll20 2024 compendium, not memory) — 411 of 414 fully populated, the remaining 3 (`dark-star`, `temple-of-the-gods`, `zeynith-s-ward`) confirmed non-core/homebrew via direct research and correctly left NULL rather than guessed. Verified the full 2024 PHB spell-slot-progression and prepared-spell-count tables myself via direct WebFetch for all 8 casting classes before writing `classes.spell_slot_progression`/`prepared_count_formula` — caught two real errors in the handoff doc along the way: Paladin was wrongly grouped with the full-level "prepare any number" casters (Cleric/Druid/Wizard) when it actually shares Ranger's half-level formula, and Ranger's own rounding was stated as "round up" when the real 2024 rule rounds down (both corrected to `level_divisor:2, round:"down"`). Max values for both Spell Slots and Spells Prepared are derived live from class + character level (same "derive, don't cache" principle as `hp-calc.js`'s HP), not stored/editable — the old stopgap's manual "Set Max" input is gone entirely. `character_sheet.spell_slots` is repurposed from "current available count" to "spent count" (matching the Class-Specific tracker's segment convention) — confirmed safe via SQL first, all 3 real characters' columns were still empty/zero, never used in real play. Spells Learned is hard-scoped to the character's own class spell list (via the now-real `spells.classes` column) — the doc's original "any spell, unrestricted" recommendation was written when that column was still empty for every row; restricting to the character's actual class list is both the more correct 5e behavior and the natural reading of the doc's own filter-column list (no separate Class dropdown was ever specified, meaning the scoping was expected to be automatic). A spell must be Learned before it can be Prepared — a shared picker modal (Prepare a Spell) lists only learned-but-unprepared spells, reusing the existing `#cs-modal-root`/`.cs-equip-results` patterns rather than inventing new modal chrome. Verified end-to-end on all 3 real characters, one per casting shape: Hana (Druid, full caster, ability-mod-plus-full-level formula, 2 level-1 slots) — full learn→prepare→unprepare→spend-slot cycle including the shared picker modal, all DB writes confirmed via SQL then reverted; Kael (Ranger, half-caster, a genuine 0-slots-at-level-1 edge case matching the real 2024 rule that Rangers don't get spells until level 2, rendered correctly as "No spell slots at this level" rather than an empty/broken bar) — hand-verified his real WIS 16 against the corrected half-level-round-down formula (0/3 prepared, exact match); Visenya (Warlock, Pact Magic's genuinely different slot-table shape, fixed-table prepared formula) — hand-verified against her real CHA-derived values. Also simulated (client-side only, no real data touched) a non-caster/no-features class to confirm the Features accordion still skips emitting entirely when empty, and a non-caster-with-limited-use-features class to confirm it correctly shows Class-Specific only with no Spell Slots/Prepared groups — extending Handoff A's "skip emitting rather than emit-then-hide" rule to the new casting dimension, not just the limited-use dimension. Zero console errors, zero horizontal overflow at 1600px desktop or 375px mobile (confirmed via `document.body.scrollWidth`, after an initial false-positive 319px reading traced to the Browser pane tool reporting `window.innerWidth:0` before an explicit resize — not a real bug). `new Function()` syntax check clean. See Decision Log for full detail.*

*Session Aug 13 2026 (continuing the same working session — Handoff A of two, Class tab restructure + Class-Specific tracker) — restructured the Class tab's Overview card into a two-column "Overview" accordion (Class details left, Key Features right) and added a new "Features" accordion holding a Class-Specific limited-use resource tracker (Rage, Channel Divinity, Second Wind, etc.) — `sheet/index.html`, new `classes.limited_use_features`/`character_sheet.limited_use_spent` columns. Pulled the real Figma frame (52:2705) directly via `get_design_context`/`get_screenshot` rather than trusting the handoff doc's paraphrase, and found several real discrepancies: the doc claimed the accordion wraps only Class details + Key Features with Basic staying outside — the actual Figma has the Accordion wrapping Basic too (collapsing would hide the class name/image); kept Basic outside per the doc's explicit, deliberate UX instruction anyway, flagged the conflict rather than picking silently. The doc's own restated field list for the Overview column silently matched what the real Figma shows (no "Hit die:" row) even though current code had one — removed it, doesn't lose data since the header's Hit Dice tracker already shows it. The doc claimed the limited-use feature name renders in Cinzel Decorative — real Figma shows plain Roboto Bold; used the real value. Verified all seed data (Rage, Channel Divinity ×2, Wild Shape, Second Wind, Action Surge, Arcane Recovery) against live web sources rather than memory, and caught real problems along the way: Bardic Inspiration's max uses is Charisma-modifier-derived, not level-derived, so it doesn't fit this schema's `max_at_level` shape at all — skipped and flagged rather than force-fit; the doc's own illustrative example table for Rage was factually wrong (claimed unlimited at level 20; real cap is 6, reached at level 18, not 17). Three real open questions resolved with the DM before writing code (Ranger's Favored Enemy has no uses in 2024 — skip it; defer all point-pool features like Lay on Hands/Sorcery Points/Ki Points to a future handoff; skip emitting the Features accordion entirely when empty rather than add a hideIfEmpty flag to the shared `accordionShell()`). Verified end-to-end on Hana (real level-1 Druid — a genuine 0/0 edge case since Wild Shape isn't unlocked until level 2, rendered correctly as an empty bar) and via an in-browser-only simulated Fighter (no real data touched) confirming multi-feature rendering, click-to-spend, click-to-un-spend, and live level-up growing the segment count while preserving spent count. Zero console errors, zero overflow at any width. Pushed live. Handoff B (Spell Slots redesign, Spells Prepared, Spells Learned) intentionally not touched. See Decision Log for full detail.*

*Session Aug 13 2026 (continuing the same session — bug fix, DM-reported) — fixed Story-tab card title clipping (`sheet/index.html`): a one-word title in an unbreakable-word title (e.g. background name "Greywitnessed") could get pushed past the card edge and clip raggedly, no ellipsis, because `.cs-story-card-headtext` (a flex child) had no `min-width:0` and neither `.cs-story-card-title` nor `.cs-past-card-title` had any overflow/wrap handling. Confirmed root cause by reading the actual CSS first, not assuming — exactly the gap the DM described. Fixed with `min-width:0` on the flex wrapper plus `overflow-wrap: break-word` on both title classes (wrap, not ellipsis-truncate — these are lore names a player should be able to read in full, per the DM's own reasoning). Checked all 16 real background names for other long single-word cases, and confirmed region/org names are naturally safe already (multi-word, wraps fine at spaces without any fix — only unbreakable single words were ever at risk). Verified against Visenya's real data (background: Greywitnessed) at three widths, including a forced worst-case 240px (`.cs-story-card`'s own min-width floor) where `scrollWidth === clientWidth` confirmed zero overflow; also confirmed all 7 Your Past cards (both the shared-headtext layout and the `--full` layout, which needed the title-level fix specifically since its container already had `min-width:0` but the text itself didn't wrap) show zero overflow. Zero console errors, zero page-level overflow at 375px mobile. Pushed live. See Decision Log for full detail.*
