**2026-08-19 (continuing the same session — verified and landed the concurrent session's DM Tools Players → Currency block, `renderPlayerCurrencySection`/`sccPlayerCurrencyBlur`, sitting untouched and unstaged since 2 rounds ago) — `dm.html`.**

Read the current code fresh before testing anything, per C1 — `renderPlayerDetailBody()`'s new sibling `.scc-pd-section` (Currency, alongside the existing Spell Assignment), `renderPlayerCurrencySection(charId)` (3-field GP/SP/CP editor over the single real `character_sheet.gold_cp` column, reusing `.scc-loot-coin-row`/`.scc-coin-input-wrap` verbatim from Combat's existing coin-award UI), and `sccPlayerCurrencyBlur(charId)` (reads all 3 fields on any one's blur, recombines into one total, PATCHes only if it actually changed). Logic read correctly on inspection — verified it actually works end-to-end, not just that it reads correctly.

**Setup — confirmed which `sheet/index.html` is the real counterpart before testing.** The ask says the DM Tools block is "the counterpart to the lockdown already shipped on main" — checked this branch's own `sheet/index.html` first rather than assuming: it still has the old, fully-editable `renderGold()`/`onCurrencyBlur()`, no `readonly` anywhere near currency. The real lockdown only exists in `main`'s checkout (`git log --all` showed `main` at a new commit, `84a6f18`, "Round 3 QA fixes + Add Item modal/currency-lockdown follow-up" — its own message: *"lock player-facing Gold/Silver/Copper to read-only (DM-only editing moves to DM Tools' Players page, on the anavale-wiki-scc-ux branch)"*). Confirmed via direct read of `main`'s `sheet/index.html`: `renderGold()` now renders `<input ... readonly aria-readonly="true">`, `onCurrencyBlur()` is gone entirely. Since both branches' `sheet/index.html` point at the identical live Supabase project (same hardcoded URL/anon key), tested against `main`'s local worktree copy — the one that's actually real/deployed-shape — rather than this branch's stale one, which would have given a false "not read-only" result for reasons unrelated to the code being verified. Started two fresh local servers (`:8801` this branch's `dm.html`, `:8802` main's `sheet/`) rather than reusing several already-running servers found via `lsof`, per the standing "may be serving another session" lesson — several concurrent `claude` processes were confirmed running.

**Verification, all 5 steps, real Supabase data throughout.**
1. **Real DM Tools UI edit.** Captured baseline first (`select gold_cp from character_sheet`): Hana 900, Kael 880, Visenya 5400. Opened Kael Evander's real "✎ Edit" panel on the Players page, confirmed the rendered Currency block correctly pre-filled GP 8 / SP 8 / CP 0 (= 880cp, matching the real baseline exactly — confirms the render half is already correct). Edited to a deliberately distinct value (GP 12 / SP 3 / CP 7) via real interaction — one real snag, disclosed rather than glossed over: a `type="number"` input's spinner arrows sit close enough to a triple-click's hit target that two of my early clicks landed on the arrow instead of selecting text, incrementing the field instead of selecting it (an artifact of coordinate-based clicking on this specific control, not an app bug — confirmed via `document.activeElement` that the correct field had focus throughout). Switched to `form_input` (sets `.value` directly, the same end-state a real typed value produces) to set the fields precisely, then triggered the actual save via a **genuine mouse click elsewhere on the page** — exercising the real `onblur` handler exactly as a human tabbing/clicking away would, not a direct `sccPlayerCurrencyBlur()` console call. Confirmed all 3 fields showed 12/3/7 before triggering blur.
2. **Supabase confirmation.** `select gold_cp from character_sheet where character_id='kael-evander-a2382a'` → `1237`, exactly `12*100 + 3*10 + 7`.
3. **Real character sheet reflects it.** Opened Kael's real sheet via his real token (`?token=a2382a...`) against `main`'s live-shape checkout, navigated to the Inventory tab — Currency block reads "Gold 12 / Silver 3 / Copper 7", exact match.
4. **Read-only confirmed, both halves agree.** DOM check: all 3 `cs-currency-*` inputs have `readOnly:true`, zero `onblur`/write handler on any of them (`getAttribute('onblur')` null). Then proved it isn't just the attribute claiming so — real triple-click + real typed "999" into the Gold field — value stayed "12", completely unaffected; confirmed via a follow-up SQL read that `gold_cp` was still `1237`, untouched by the attempt. This is the exact real-world check for "can a player edit their own gold": they genuinely cannot, from either the DOM or the database's point of view.

Re-opened Kael's real sheet fresh (full navigation, not cached state) and confirmed it now shows Gold 8 / Silver 8 / Copper 0 too — the revert is real, not just locally cached. Final sweep across all 3 players confirmed byte-identical to the pre-test baseline (900/880/5400). Zero console errors on either tab throughout.

Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty. `git diff dm.html` confirmed unchanged from the concurrent session's own version — this round only ran the app against real data, no code edits of my own to the Currency block itself.

**2026-08-19 (continuing the same session — Scenarios display-accuracy fix, the one "found, not fixed" item flagged at the end of the urgent write-path round) — `dm.html`.**

**1 — Confirmed the bug and its root cause before writing any fix, per the explicit instruction.** Grepped every read of `session_scenarios` in the file. `sccLiveRenderScenarios()` (the Scenarios accordion section's renderer) and `sccFilterScenarios()` (its filter box) both had `var session = SCC_SESSION;` — and `SCC_SESSION` is confirmed, repeatedly, throughout this session's own prior audits, to never repoint away from the live session during audit view (`sccLoadHistorySession()` deliberately doesn't touch it, by design, per several earlier fixes' own comments). Confirmed this is exactly the same class of bug as the Copy Post-Session Summary header fix from 2 rounds ago — same root cause, same fix pattern applies, as the ask itself suspected.

**2 — Fixed with a new shared helper, not a param threaded through 6 call sites.** `sccRenderLivePanel()` (the shared render orchestrator both `sccLoadLatestSession()` and `sccLoadHistorySession()` call) has no session-awareness of its own and is invoked from 6 different places — adding an optional override param and correctly threading it through every call site would have been more surface area to get wrong than necessary. Instead, added `sccResolveViewedSession()`: reads `#scc-session-number` (kept correct in both live and historical view by `sccSetSessionDisplay()`, called from both load paths) and looks it up in `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` — the exact same pattern `sccPreviousSessionRow()` and `sccCopyPostSessionSummary()` already established for this identical reason, reused rather than inlined a 3rd/4th time since Scenarios needed it in two places (render + filter). `sccLiveRenderScenarios()`'s `var session = SCC_SESSION;` and `sccFilterScenarios()`'s identical line both changed to `sccResolveViewedSession()`.
**Confirmed safe for the live-editing case, not just the audit-viewing case, before shipping.** `sccSaveScenarios()` (Setup's own scenario editor save) mutates `SCC_SESSION.session_scenarios` directly, in place, after a successful PATCH. Traced whether this object mutation would still be visible through the new lookup — confirmed yes: `SCC_SESSION` is always assigned the literal same object reference as its own matching entry inside `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` (both set from the identical fetch in `sccLoadLatestSession()`), so a live edit via Setup is reflected in `sccResolveViewedSession()`'s result immediately, not stale, with no extra sync code needed.
**Checked Setup's own separate Scenario Editor for the same bug shape, confirmed it's a non-issue, left untouched.** `sccRenderScenarioEditor(s)`/`sccDrawScenarioEditor()` populate a working-copy editor (`SCC_SCENARIO_EDIT`) from whichever session object `sccPopulateSetupForm(s)` was called with — meaning, like the bug just fixed, it technically loads the *audited* session's scenarios into its working copy during history view. But every actual editable field in it (`class="scc-input"`/`class="scc-textarea"`, group-name input, action input, response textarea) is already covered by the existing blanket `.scc-audit-mode .scc-input`/`.scc-textarea { display:none !important; }` rules applied to `#form-setup` — confirmed via reading the render function directly, not assumed — so the editor is already fully inert and invisible during audit mode, the same way every other Setup field already is. No fix needed there; not a live gap.

**3 — Verification, real interaction, genuinely distinct data, not just a distinct session number.** Confirmed first that the real, live Session 1 has 8 real scenario groups (At the Forres Brothers Inn, At the Formery Office, At the Wanderkeep Rest Station, At the River / Bridge, In the Forest / Off the Path, Creature Encounters, Questions About the World, Combat — Extraction Site) — opened the real Live Session Scenarios section via a real click and confirmed all 8 render correctly before touching anything, establishing a genuine "before" baseline. Pushed a synthetic `session_number:999` into `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` (client-side only, same established safe pattern from the urgent-fix round) with its own fabricated `session_scenarios` — 2 groups, "SYNTHETIC — Test Group Alpha"/"Beta", content unrelated to any real group — specifically so the fix could be *proven*, not just plausibly asserted: if the bug were still present, the section would show the real 8 groups even while "viewing" 999. Entered history view via `sccLoadHistorySession(999)`, confirmed via a real click on the Scenarios toggle and a full-page screenshot that only the 2 synthetic groups render, with `SCC_SESSION.session_number` confirmed still `1` throughout (the live session, correctly never touched). Typed "ruins" into the real filter box (real keystrokes, not a direct function call) — confirmed it correctly filtered against the *synthetic* session's data (matched "SYNTHETIC — Test Group Beta"'s one scenario mentioning ruins, correctly hid Group Alpha), proving `sccFilterScenarios()`'s fix works too, not just the initial render. Clicked "Back to Current Session" (real click) — confirmed `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY` false, `SCC_SESSION.session_number` back to `1`, and the Scenarios section correctly restored to the real 8 groups, screenshotted. Noted, not investigated further (pre-existing, unrelated to this fix, out of scope): the filter input's own text ("ruins") visually persists after the session switch even though the displayed groups are no longer filtered by it — a minor cosmetic staleness in the filter *box*, not the *data* it's meant to filter, and not something this round's ask was about. Zero console errors throughout. Confirmed via a final SQL read that `session_notes` still has exactly 1 real row (session 1) — the synthetic session never touched Supabase.

**4 — Found a concurrent session actively editing `dm.html` mid-task; handled via a partial commit, not a conflict or a discard.** Reviewing the full diff before committing (per this file's own standing habit) surfaced 2 unexpected hunks: a "Currency" feature added to the Players panel (`renderPlayerDetailBody`'s new sibling `.scc-pd-section`, plus new `renderPlayerCurrencySection()`/`sccPlayerCurrencyBlur()` functions, per their own in-code comment tied to a `sheet/index.html` session making player currency DM-only-editable) — unrelated to anything asked this round, and never written by this session. Confirmed via `git log`/`git fetch origin anavale-wiki-scc-ux` that nothing new had landed upstream (ruling out a remote pull as the source), and via `ps aux` that multiple `claude` CLI processes were genuinely running concurrently on this machine (started at distinct times over the preceding hour) — confirming this is real, live, in-progress work from a parallel session in this exact same worktree, not stray/orphaned state to clean up. Per this project's own standing git-safety habit (investigate before touching unfamiliar working-tree state, treat another session's in-progress work as never mine to discard or silently bundle), left the Currency code completely untouched in the working tree — did not revert it, did not include it in this commit. Isolated exactly which of the diff's 4 hunks were mine (confirmed via `git diff dm.html | grep -c '^@@'` → 4; hunks 1–2 the Currency addition, hunks 3–4 the Scenarios fix) and staged only mine via a hand-built patch applied with `git apply --cached` (verified `--check` first, then confirmed via `git diff --cached` that the index held exactly the 2 Scenarios hunks and via `git diff` that the working tree still carried the Currency hunks, unstaged and undisturbed) — rather than `git add`-ing the whole file and risking committing someone else's mid-edit, untested code under this commit's message and attribution.

Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty (checked against the full working-tree diff, including the concurrent session's own hunks, to confirm neither introduced anything since both would block the shared file either way).

**2026-08-19 (continuing the same session — URGENT: fixed the Live Session audit-mode write-path corruption bug found in the immediately prior audit round) — `dm.html`.**

DM's ask was explicit and scoped to 3 items, plus "anything more I find while checking." Delivered all 3, found and fixed a 4th during verification, and found (but per instruction did not fix) a 5th, display-accuracy-only issue.

**1 — `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY` guards, swept for every real write path, not just the 6 originally named.** Read every function reachable from the Live Session tab's control strip and its 5 accordion sections before writing any guard, rather than trusting the original audit's own function list at face value. Confirmed the control strip's 6 (`sccLiveAdjustHp`/`sccLiveSetHp`/`sccLiveAddCondition`/`sccLiveConfirmCondition`/`sccLiveRemoveCondition`/`sccLiveToggleInspiration`) and the 2 already named for NPCs/Quests (`sccCycleRelationship` — `sccCycleRelationship2` just calls it, one guard covers both call paths; `sccSaveBeatStatus` — `sccCycleBeatStatus`'s only caller). Then traced the shared card-body functions built in Phase 3b (`sccBuildLocationCardBodyHtml`) and found a real write path the original audit never named: `sccVendorShopSectionHtml('pois', loc.id)` renders the full POI Shop UI — add/edit/remove stock, and a real Buy button that deducts a player's real `gold_cp` and grants a real `inventory` row — inline inside any expanded Location card, including Mira Pont's Sundry Goods, which is assigned to the one real session and would render directly inside the Live Session Locations section. Guarded all 4 (`sccVendorAddStock`/`sccVendorSetField`/`sccVendorRemoveStock`/`sccVendorBuy`) at the function level (not per call-site) since the same functions are also reachable via the global lookup drawer, and the intent of "locked for audit" should hold regardless of which UI surface triggers the write. Also checked Field Guide's own editor (`sccFgToggleEditMode`/`sccFgSaveDocument`) — confirmed its `SCC_FG_DOC_SESSION_NUM` is a genuinely independent session selector unrelated to which session `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY` is auditing, but concluded editing *any* session's document while the DM believes they're locked into audit view still violates the same principle, so gated both: `sccFgToggleEditMode()` at the entry point (blocks the picker from ever opening) and `sccFgSaveDocument()` as defense in depth, matching this file's own established pattern of gating both the entry point and the actual write. Checked and deliberately did NOT gate `sccToggleScenarioUsed()` (the Scenarios section's "mark as used" click) — confirmed via reading the function it's pure in-memory state (`SCC_USED_SCENARIOS`), never touches Supabase, so there's no corruption risk to guard against. 14 real guards added total.

**2 — Accordion toggles.** `.scc-audit-mode .scc-collapsible-toggle { pointer-events:none; }` removed outright rather than scoped with `:not()` — confirmed via grep that the class has exactly 2 real uses in the whole file (Setup's prep-notes toggle, the 5 Live Session accordion toggles), and both are pure open/close toggles with zero write behavior of their own (`sccTogglePrep()`/`sccLiveToggleSection()`), so there was no correctness reason to block either. The actual write actions inside each section are now individually gated per item 1, which is the real protection — the toggle itself was never the risk.

**3 — Setup's Premise + Prep Notes.** Both fields live in the same `#scc-prep-body` collapsible, both had the identical gap (`.scc-input`/`.scc-textarea` just vanish outright in audit mode with no readonly fallback) — the DM's ask named only Prep Notes, but fixing it alone would have left Premise rendering as dead empty space in the same block, so both got the same `-display` swap treatment as Notes' 5 fields, added to both `sccLoadHistorySession()` (swap in) and `sccLoadCurrentSession()` (swap back).

**4 — Found only by testing the fix, not the original bug: switching tabs while auditing silently exited audit mode.** While verifying fix #3 (which requires navigating to the Setup tab to see the readonly Prep Notes), found via direct state inspection that `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY` had flipped to `false` and `SCC_SESSION` had repointed to the live session — despite the readonly banner and every readonly display still showing the historical session's data on screen. Root-caused to a pre-existing `switchTab` wrapper (`var _origSwitchTab = switchTab; switchTab = function(tab) {...}`, predates this session, missed by an earlier literal-string grep for `sccInit()` since the wrapper calls it as `setTimeout(sccInit, 0)` — a bare reference, not a call with parens) that unconditionally re-runs `sccInit()` → `sccLoadLatestSession()` on every Setup or Session tab click, to keep those tabs' data fresh. `sccLoadLatestSession()` sets `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY = false` and repoints `SCC_SESSION`, but was never written to also strip `.scc-audit-mode` or restore the hidden inputs — because until this session, nothing needed it to, since audit mode's own write paths were never gated in the first place and this desync was invisible. With fix #1 in place, this became a real, severe problem: a DM auditing a locked session who clicked Setup (even just to glance at something, not intending to leave audit view) would be silently dropped back into a fully live, fully writable session, with every visual cue still claiming they were safely locked into the historical one — making all 14 of fix #1's guards trivially bypassable via a two-click round trip. Fixed at the wrapper: skip the `sccInit()` re-sync while `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY` is true (a locked historical session has no live data that needs refreshing, so this costs nothing real). Confirmed this is genuinely a *found* bug from testing rigor, not a hypothetical — reproduced the desync live before fixing it, then reproduced the fix closing it, both via direct state inspection, not assumption.

**5 — Found, explicitly not fixed, per the instruction not to bundle.** Confirming all 5 accordion sections render *correct* historical content (not just that they open) surfaced that the Scenarios section specifically reads `SCC_SESSION.session_scenarios` directly (`sccLiveRenderScenarios()`) rather than resolving from whichever session `sccLoadHistorySession()` actually loaded — the same "`SCC_SESSION` never repoints during audit view" fact behind several of today's other fixes, just surfacing a 3rd time in a place nobody had checked yet. Since `SCC_SESSION` stays pointed at the live session throughout audit view by design, a DM auditing a *different*, non-live session sees the *live* session's real scenarios rendered in this one section — a real display-accuracy bug, not a write/corruption risk (nothing here writes anything), so left flagged rather than fixed, matching the explicit "don't bundle with anything else this round" instruction.

**Verification, exhaustive, real Supabase data throughout — not UI appearance.** Used the established safe-synthetic-session pattern from prior audit rounds: pushed `session_number:999` into `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` client-side only (confirmed via a final SQL read that `session_notes` never gained a 999 row — genuinely never touched the database), copying real Session 1's actual assigned NPCs/Locations/Quests/Creatures (`gobblewump-gerald`/`dran`, `gobblewump-crossing`/`mira-pont-sundry-goods`, `something-is-wrong-with-gerald`, `grusk-enforcer`) so the accordion sections would render genuine historical content while `SCC_SESSION` stayed correctly pointed at the real live session 1 throughout — the exact separation needed to prove a guard blocks writes to the *right* target, not just that it blocks *some* write. All 14 guarded functions tested: HP adjust/set, Add/Confirm/Remove Condition, and Toggle Inspiration via a mix of real UI clicks (the HP stepper button, confirmed 12/12 held after a real click) and direct calls (to reach every function precisely, including paths with no single obvious UI trigger in the test fixture) — every one confirmed a genuine no-op via in-memory `SCC_SHEETS`/`SCC_NPC_RELATIONSHIPS`/`SCC_PROGRESS` state comparison (before/after identical) and the correct, specific toast message firing each time. The relationship-cycle guard was confirmed via a real click on Gobblewump Gerald's actual rendered badge in the NPCs section, not just a direct call. The 4 Vendor functions confirmed via direct calls against Mira Pont's real (0-stock) shop, `VENDOR_STOCK.length` unchanged before/after each. Field Guide's editor confirmed via a real click on the actual "Edit Document" button — `SCC_FG_EDIT_MODE` stayed `false`, button text stayed "Edit Document" rather than flipping to "Close Editor". All 5 accordion sections confirmed opening via real clicks and rendering correct real historical content, screenshotted in one full-page capture: NPCs (Dran, Gobblewump Gerald, correct "Neutral" badges), Locations (Gobblewump Crossing, Mira Pont's Sundry Goods with its real Visualization/Summary text), Quests ("Something Is Wrong With Gerald" fully expanded with beat rail and all 4 beats), Scenarios (real Session 1 groups — see finding 5 above for why), Field Guide (the real, full Session 1 document). Setup's Premise/Prep Notes confirmed readable via a real toggle click, screenshotted showing the exact synthetic historical text with both fields genuinely non-editable (no `<input>`/`<textarea>` present, plain text divs). The tab-switch fix (finding 4) confirmed via the full real round trip: entered audit view on Session tab, clicked Setup (real click) — confirmed `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY` stayed `true`, `SCC_SESSION.session_number` stayed `1` (never reset to the audited 999, correctly never touched), `.scc-audit-mode` still present, Premise/Prep Notes displays still showing the correct synthetic text (not stale, not the live session's real text) — then clicked back to Session tab and confirmed a write guard still fired correctly (proving the fix didn't accidentally leave a different hole). "Back to Current Session" confirmed via a real click: `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY` correctly `false`, readonly banner hidden, `.scc-audit-mode` removed — then confirmed live HP editing genuinely still works outside audit mode (a real -1/+1 round trip, both via direct call since the UI stepper's click coordinate had drifted after a layout change, confirmed the underlying function itself is not over-blocked), reverted immediately. **Final SQL sweep, every table any test this round could plausibly have touched, all byte-identical to the pre-test baseline captured before any testing began**: `character_sheet` (`hp_current`/`hp_max`/`conditions`/`has_inspiration`/`gold_cp` for all 3 real PCs — Hana 12/12/[]/false/900, Kael 12/12/[]/false/880, Visenya 10/10/[]/false/5400), `quest_progress` (all 4 real beats of `something-is-wrong-with-gerald`, still `not_started`/`null`), `session_notes` (still exactly 1 real row, `npc_relationships` still `{"oro":"ally"}`, `session_status` still `active`), `vendor_stock` (0 rows), `inventory` (22 rows), `field_guide_sessions` (1 row, 140 blocks). Zero console errors throughout every check, across both the original test tab and a fresh second tab used to escape a Browser-pane scroll-repaint glitch encountered mid-session (worked around via a tall-viewport, zero-scroll full-page capture — the same class of tooling limitation already on record in this file's history, not a real application bug, confirmed by every DOM-level check reading correct live values throughout). Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

**2026-08-19 (new session — Previously Completed Sessions audit: can a DM review what happened in a past session? Same investigate-then-report treatment as the Live Session/Combat/Notes audits below. Investigation only, no `dm.html` changes.) — read-only, `dm.html` unmodified.**

**Step 1 — confirmed current real state, tab by tab, not assumed from the prior 3 audits' own "already fixed" framing.**

Setup: mostly correct, one real gap found. The 5-entity picker chips (Locations/NPCs/Creatures/Quests/Items) stay genuinely visible and readable in audit mode — confirmed only their search/browse/remove controls hide (`.scc-picker-search`/`.scc-browse-btn`/`.scc-chip-remove`), the chips themselves are untouched by any audit-mode CSS rule, so a DM can see what was assigned. The session date gets a real readonly swap (`scc-session-date-display`, confirmed present in `sccLoadHistorySession()`). **Not previously flagged: Prep Notes has no such fallback.** `#scc-prep-notes` carries the bare `.scc-textarea` class, and `.scc-audit-mode .scc-textarea { display:none !important; }` hides it outright with zero readonly display — unlike the Notes tab's 5 recap fields, which got exactly this treatment in an earlier round but Setup's own Prep Notes field never did. A DM reviewing a past session today cannot read what they planned/expected going in.

Live Session (the Phase 3a/3b merged accordion tab) — **not fine, confirmed via a full code trace, not assumed working from the restructure's own commit message.** Two real, previously-unflagged problems, one severe:

1. **The 5 accordion sections (NPCs/Locations/Quests/Scenarios/Field Guide) are non-expandable in audit mode.** `.scc-audit-mode .scc-collapsible-toggle { pointer-events:none; }` was added for Setup's own prep-notes toggle — confirmed via grep that the only two places `.scc-collapsible-toggle` appears in static markup are that one Setup toggle and the accordion's 5 toggles (`scc-live-acc-toggle scc-collapsible-toggle`, shared class). Since `.scc-audit-mode` lands on `#form-session` (the accordion's real parent, confirmed via DOM structure — `#scc-panel-live` sits inside `#form-session` alongside Combat/Notes), all 5 toggles lose click responsiveness the moment a DM opens history. Confirmed via `sccLiveOpenSection()`/`sccLiveApplyAutoExpand()`: every section starts collapsed by default except Quests, which only force-opens if the viewed session has a real in-progress beat (`sccComputeActiveBeat()`) — no other section is ever programmatically opened by `sccLoadHistorySession()`. Net effect: a DM opening a past session's Live Session tab sees, at best, one auto-opened Quests section and four permanently-stuck-shut sections with no way to reach NPCs/Locations/Scenarios/Field Guide at all — the single richest tab in the app renders as almost nothing, the same failure shape the Notes/Combat/Field Guide audits already found and fixed elsewhere in this app, just never caught here.
2. **A real, more serious problem: the always-visible control strip and (whenever a section happens to be open) the accordion content underneath it stay fully live and write-enabled, with zero `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY` gating anywhere.** Grepped every write path this control strip and its accordion sections can reach: `sccLiveAdjustHp`/`sccLiveSetHp` (Party HP), `sccLiveAddCondition`/`sccLiveConfirmCondition`/`sccLiveRemoveCondition` (Conditions), `sccLiveToggleInspiration` (Inspiration), `sccCycleRelationship`/`sccCycleRelationship2` (NPC relationship badges), `sccSaveBeatStatus`/`sccCycleBeatStatus` (quest beat status) — confirmed none of the 10 check `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY`; only the 4 Tagged/Quick-Note write paths do (fixed in an earlier round). This is genuine data-corruption risk, not just a UX inconsistency, confirmed by reading each write target's real scoping: HP/Conditions/Inspiration write directly to `character_sheet`, which has never been session-scoped at all (same accepted last-write-wins risk class the Phase 2 audit already put on record for HP specifically) — so this is "a DM can silently change a player's real current HP while believing they're in a locked view," consistent with the Combat audit's own precedent finding for exactly this reason. But `sccCycleRelationship`'s own `sccSaveNpcRelationships()` writes keyed off `SCC_SESSION.session_number` — and `SCC_SESSION` is *never* repointed at the historical session by `sccLoadHistorySession()` (confirmed via the function's own code — same fact the Notes audit already established for a different write path) — so cycling a relationship badge while "viewing" session 1 actually PATCHes the *live current* session's `npc_relationships`, potentially against a completely different NPC roster than the live session has assigned. `sccSaveBeatStatus()` is worse still: confirmed via direct schema query that `quest_progress` has no session dimension in its own primary scoping at all — keyed by `quest_id`+`beat_order` only, with a single nullable `completed_in_session` integer as the sole session marker — so toggling a beat's status while auditing a past session mutates the real, global, current state of that quest, and (if marked completed) stamps `completed_in_session` with whichever session is *actually* live, not the one on screen. This is the most severe finding across all 4 tabs audited this session.

Combat — confirmed still correct as shipped (`.scc-combat-audit-note` swap-in present and correctly gated, `.scc-combat-builder`/`.scc-initiative-tracker` both hidden via the existing `!important` rules). No regression, no further gap found.

Notes — confirmed still correct as shipped (5-field readonly swap present, Tagged Notes rendered visible-but-readonly via `sccBuildTagNoteCard(n, SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY)`, all 4 write paths gated, Copy Post-Session Summary's trigger reachable per the immediately prior round). No regression.

**Step 2 — the 6 requested categories, real current state and real minimum for each.**

1. **Notes** — already solved by the prior 3 rounds. Confirmed, no further work.
2. **Combat encounters** — confirmed zero persistence exists anywhere (re-confirmed via a fresh `list_tables` query, still no `combat_encounters` table or equivalent). **Schema-overlap question, answered directly rather than deferred**: pre-staging (Setup preparing an encounter ahead of time) and after-the-fact history (what really happened) are genuinely the same underlying need, not two unrelated ones — they're two ends of one lifecycle on the same real-world entity ("the encounter that belonged to session N"): planned → run → resolved. A single schema addition (e.g. `session_notes.combat_encounters` jsonb, or a dedicated `combat_encounters` table keyed by session_number) with a lifecycle status field could hold the planned combatant list *and* get filled in with a resolved summary once `sccEndCombat()` runs, rather than building two separate, disconnected schemas for what's really one object's before/after state. This directly reuses the shape already floated in the Aug 12/17 2026 Session Setup and DM Tools UX/IA investigations (`{name, combatants:[{creature_id, quantity}], notes}`) — worth designing as one piece, not two, whenever it's built. Real minimum for "what happened" (explicitly not a full turn-by-turn replay): `sccEndCombat()`'s own Combat Summary popup already computes participants, total coins collected, and per-item loot awards in memory — capturing that exact computed object into one row at End Combat time (participant names/counts, outcome/coins/loot actually split, an optional one-line DM note) is a small addition riding on data the app already builds and currently just discards.
3. **Quest progression** — confirmed real and persisted, but NOT meaningfully session-scoped for historical review. Schema check confirmed: `quest_progress` is keyed by `(quest_id, beat_order)` only, with a single nullable `completed_in_session` integer as the sole session marker — no `in_progress_in_session`, no change log, no way to tell when a beat *became* in_progress, and if a beat is toggled back and forth across two different sessions, only the *most recent* completion's session number survives — earlier history is silently overwritten. So today you can tell "this beat is currently marked completed, and was last marked so in session N" — you cannot tell "this beat completed during session 1" as a durable historical fact once anything touches it again. Small fix if wanted: a real append-only log table (`quest_progress_log`: quest_id, beat_order, status, session_number, changed_at) rather than trying to retrofit more columns onto the single current-state row.
4. **Entity Interactions** — needs real definition; investigated rather than guessed at scope. Genuinely derivable today: which NPCs/Locations/Creatures/Items were *assigned/planned* for a session, via Setup's `session_notes.npc_ids`/`location_ids`/`creature_ids`/`item_ids_awarded` — this is real, already-fetchable data (and its readonly display in Setup was just confirmed working, item 1 above). NOT derivable at all: what actually *happened* with any of them — no record of whether an assigned NPC ever came up in play, no timestamp on when a relationship changed (only the current value, in a single overwritten JSON blob — `session_notes.npc_relationships` is plain `text`, not a log), no link between "this item was in `item_ids_awarded`" and an actual `inventory` row that resulted from it (the two are never connected at write time — confirmed via grep, nothing cross-references them). Realistic scope for this category without new tracking: a session-review page can honestly show "what was planned" (Setup's lists), not "what happened as a result" — the latter needs new schema, and is the same underlying gap the Combat and Quest-Progression findings above already describe from their own angles.
5. **Summary of Session** — confirmed, satisfies the ask as-is. `sccCopyPostSessionSummary()` already resolves the correct session (live or historical, via `#scc-session-number`), reads the 5 real recap fields plus a fresh, session-scoped fetch of Tagged Notes, and its trigger button was made reachable from a locked historical session in the immediately prior round. No further work needed.
6. **Anything else, own judgment** — XP/loot awarded per session is not trackable at all today, and it's the same root gap as item 2, not a separate one. Confirmed via schema + a full grep of `dm.html`: `quest_progress.xp_awarded`/`loot_awarded` (booleans) and `session_notes.inspiration_awarded` (jsonb) all exist in the real schema and are **never referenced anywhere in the app** — fully dead columns, presumably scaffolded early and never wired up. `inventory.dm_granted` (boolean) is real and used, but the table has no session-number column at all, so even a confirmed DM-granted item can't be tied back to which session it came from. `session_notes.session_summary` (text) is also a fully dead, unreferenced column. All of this — XP, loot, and item-award session-tagging — would be solved by the same combat-encounter-outcome schema recommended in item 2, since that's exactly the data `sccEndCombat()` already computes and currently throws away; this shouldn't be designed as a 4th separate tracking system.

**Step 3 — proposed shape, sized, same convention as the Live Session/Combat/Notes audits.**

Small, mostly quick wins on already-solved ground: (a) Prep Notes readonly display in Setup audit mode — literally the same pattern already shipped for the Notes tab's 5 fields, one new `-display` div + swap logic. (b) Fix the Live Session accordion's non-expandable toggles — either scope the `pointer-events:none` rule off `.scc-collapsible-toggle` generically and onto a narrower selector that doesn't also catch `scc-live-acc-toggle`, or force-open all 5 sections (not just Quests) whenever `sccLoadHistorySession()` runs, since there's no editing risk left once (c) below closes the write-path gap — either approach is a small, contained fix. (c) **Highest priority, not optional**: add `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY` guards to every Live Session write path that lacks one — `sccLiveAdjustHp`/`sccLiveSetHp`/`sccLiveAddCondition`/`sccLiveConfirmCondition`/`sccLiveRemoveCondition`/`sccLiveToggleInspiration`/`sccCycleRelationship`/`sccCycleRelationship2`/`sccSaveBeatStatus`/`sccCycleBeatStatus` — same one-line-per-function pattern already used for the 4 Notes write paths. This is a correctness fix, not a UX polish item — it closes a real, currently-live path to silently corrupting the *current* session's real HP/conditions/relationships/quest-progress data while a DM believes they're looking at a locked historical record. Recommend doing this before (b), since fixing accessibility to sections that are still write-unsafe would only make the corruption risk easier to trigger, not harder.

Medium: a `quest_progress_log` (or equivalent) so quest-beat history survives being touched twice — needed for item 3 to be genuinely reviewable, not urgent since nothing is actively wrong today (the current-state view just isn't historical), but blocks ever showing "this beat completed in session 1" as a durable fact.

Large, flagged not designed this round: the combined combat-encounter schema (plan+outcome, one lifecycle) that would resolve items 2, 4 (the item-award half), and 6 (XP/loot) all at once — genuinely one design decision serving three of the six requested categories, not three separate builds. Recommend this be scoped as its own dedicated session once the small/medium items above are handled, given it needs a real schema decision (jsonb-on-session_notes vs. a dedicated table) the same way the still-open "real encounter pre-staging" project from the Aug 12/17 investigations already flagged.

No `dm.html` changes this round — investigation and a written strategy only, confirmed via `git status` before pushing (only this file plus the pre-existing, unrelated `.DS_Store`).

**2026-08-18 (continuing the same session — Notes panel, Copy Post-Session Summary now pulls real Tagged Notes, the last item off the audit below) — `dm.html`.**
**Fetch, not cache.** `sccCopyPostSessionSummary()` previously only read the 5 textareas' DOM values, confirmed in the original audit as a real gap — a DM who captured anything via Quick Note got a summary silently missing it. Fixed with a dedicated `sccFetch('session_note_entries', '&session_number=eq.' + num + '...')` inside the function itself, deliberately not reusing `SCC_NOTE_ENTRIES` (the Notes tab's own render-time cache, which reflects whatever session that tab last happened to render, not necessarily the one this specific click was for) — matches the explicit instruction not to trust whatever happens to already be cached client-side. Grouped by category in `SCC_NOTE_CATEGORIES`'s own canonical order (the same order `sccRenderTagNoteFilters()` already renders them in), appended after the 5 existing sections; a category with zero real notes for the session is skipped outright rather than printed as an empty placeholder 7 times, since — unlike the 5 always-present textareas — these are dynamic, not fixed sections.
**A second real correctness bug found and fixed, beyond just "add the notes."** Reasoning through "the summary should pull whichever session is actually being viewed" surfaced that the *header* (`# Session N Summary`, `Date:`, `Premise:`) had the identical class of bug as the missing-notes gap, just unnoticed until now: it read `SCC_SESSION.session_number`/`.session_date`/`.premise` directly, and `SCC_SESSION` stays pointed at the *live* session throughout audit view by design — confirmed last round, the exact reason `sccRenderTaggedNotes()` needed a `sessionNumOverride` param at all rather than just reading `SCC_SESSION`. Triggered from history view, the old code would have shown the live session's number/date/premise as the header while the 5 textareas underneath it (correctly, via `sccPopulateSetupForm()`) showed the *historical* session's real recap text — a real, visible mismatch a DM would have had to notice and mentally correct for. Fixed by resolving the session number from `#scc-session-number` — the same hidden input `sccPreviousSessionRow()` already established as the reliable "whichever session is currently displayed, live or historical" source in this exact file, for exactly this kind of cross-cutting need — then looking up the matching row in the already-loaded `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` cache for the header fields specifically. No second fetch needed there (unlike Tagged Notes, which the ask explicitly required to be fresh) — `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` is already correctly kept in sync with real session data by the existing load paths, and nothing about clicking Copy itself could have gone and changed a session's own date/premise out from under it.
**A third bug, unrelated to the fetch logic, caught by verifying a copy-pasted line rather than trusting it.** The rewrite carried over the original `'Post-session summary copied ✓'` toast line verbatim. Checking it against `showDMToast()`'s real implementation (`toast.textContent = msg` — read directly, not assumed) confirmed that specific literal character was fine (`textContent` renders literal Unicode correctly, unlike `innerHTML`-only HTML entities), but the same check surfaced a **separate, already-broken pattern** sitting in 4 other `showDMToast()` calls in this file: `&#10003;`/`&#8594;` passed as HTML entities, which never decode through `textContent` and render as the literal, garbled text `&#10003;`/`&#8594;` to a DM instead of a checkmark/arrow. Confirmed live via a direct probe (`showDMToast('Test &#10003; entity', ...)` renders that exact unparsed string) before treating it as real rather than theoretical. Traced the origin of each: two (`sccAwardCoins`/`sccAwardCoinsAll`) were introduced 2 rounds earlier this same session, copied from a neighboring function's convention without ever visually verifying the result rendered correctly; the other two (`sccAwardItem`/`sccSummaryAwardItem`, identical `item.name + ' &#8594; ' + player.name + ' &#10003;'` pattern) predate this session entirely. Fixed all 5 (the 4 pre-existing plus this round's own line) to real JS unicode escapes (`✓`/`→`), which decode correctly regardless of `textContent` vs `innerHTML` — used the established Python exact-string-replace workaround for every one of them, since typing an escape sequence directly through the Edit tool risks it being re-normalized back into the literal character it's meant to avoid, a risk this file's own history has documented and hit before. **Investigated further, not fixed, flagged plainly**: a broader sweep for any `showDMToast()` call containing a literal emoji-range character found 9 more instances scattered through the file — these are not broken (literal Unicode renders fine via `textContent`), only non-compliant with this file's own C2 convention; left untouched as genuinely out of scope for a round about Copy Post-Session Summary's data correctness, not silently swept in.
**Verified live end-to-end, real Supabase writes throughout, both required cases.** Live session: filled all 5 textareas with real text and added 4 real Tagged Notes across 3 categories (DM-Only ×1, Quest-Related ×2, an untitled Unexpected Moment) through the actual "+ New Note" UI on the real, currently-active Session 1 — confirmed all 4 rows via a direct SQL read before testing the copy itself. Clicked the real Copy button (`navigator.clipboard.writeText` intercepted, since this sandboxed browser blocks real OS clipboard access — an established workaround used elsewhere in this project's own history) and confirmed the captured text matched exactly: correct header, all 5 sections in order, then "## Quick Notes — DM-Only" / "## Quick Notes — Quest-Related" (both notes correctly grouped under one shared heading, ordered most-recently-updated first, matching the real Notes tab's own list ordering) / "## Quick Notes — Unexpected Moment" (the untitled note rendering with no stray empty bold line, confirming the conditional title-line logic) — and confirmed Player Actions/Follow-Up/Loose Thread/Milestone are correctly absent from the output entirely, matching that Session 1 genuinely had zero real notes in those categories. Historical session: built a synthetic `session_number:999` pushed into `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` client-side only (`session_notes` never touched), paired with one real, temporary `session_note_entries` row so the fetch had genuine Supabase data to prove against — entered history view via the real `sccLoadHistorySession(999)`, confirmed directly that `SCC_SESSION.session_number` still read the live `1` while `#scc-session-number` correctly read `999` (the precise split this fix depends on, confirmed present and real, not assumed), then called the actual function — its own trigger button lives inside `.scc-setup-actions`, which stays correctly hidden in audit mode alongside Save Notes, so it isn't reachable via a real click there today (flagged, not changed, see Current Focus) — and confirmed the copied text correctly showed "Session 999," the real historical date/premise, the real historical 5-field recap text, and only the one real historical Tagged Note, with none of Session 1's 4 real notes leaking across. All 5 test notes deleted afterward; confirmed via a final SQL read that both `session_notes` and `session_note_entries` are back to their exact real baseline. **2026-08-18 (continuing the same session — Notes panel, first 4 fixes from the audit below, bundled per the audit's own recommendation since they're one coherent problem: `.scc-audit-mode` left a completed session's real notes with no read path anywhere in the app) — `dm.html`.**
**Scoped to exactly the 4 items named.** (1) Post-complete staleness — `sccCompleteSession()`'s completion callback now resets `SCC_NOTE_FILTER_CAT`/`SCC_NOTE_FILTER_Q` and the search box's DOM value, then calls `sccRenderTaggedNotes()`; that function's own existing `if (!num) {...'Save session setup first'...}` branch fires correctly once `SCC_SESSION` is null (set a few lines earlier in the same callback), so this needed no new empty-state logic, just the missing call. (2) Write guards — `sccLiveSaveNote()`/`sccAddTagNote()`/`sccSaveTagNoteField()`/`sccDeleteTagNote()` all gained the identical `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY` check `sccOpenQuickNoteModal()` already had (same message, same early-return shape) — closes the exact inconsistency the audit found: only 1 of 4 real write paths into `session_note_entries` checked it before this.
**(3) 5 recap fields — replicated an existing pattern rather than inventing one.** This file already has exactly one precedent for "show a locked field's real value instead of hiding it," built for the date field (`scc-audit-readonly-val`, a sibling `<div>` swapped in for the `<input>`). Added the identical shape for all 5 textareas — new `id="scc-note-*-display"` divs, shown/hidden by `sccLoadHistorySession()`/`sccLoadCurrentSession()` exactly where the date field's own swap already lives, right next to it. One real addition the date field never needed: the note fields hold genuine multi-paragraph text, and a plain block-level `<div>` collapses embedded newlines by default — new `.scc-audit-readonly-val--note` modifier (`white-space:pre-wrap`) added specifically for these 5, confirmed necessary and sufficient via a real 2-paragraph test string.
**(4) Tagged Notes — the larger half of this round.** Replaced `.scc-audit-mode .scc-tagnote-section { display:none !important; }` with `.scc-audit-mode .scc-tagnote-add-row { display:none !important; }` — the whole section (search, category filters, the note list itself) now stays visible and interactive for reading; only the literal add-a-note controls are hidden. Per-card behavior moved into `sccBuildTagNoteCard(n, readonly)`, a new second parameter: real HTML `readonly` on the title `<input>`/body `<textarea>` — deliberately not `disabled` (which also blocks focus and text selection, so a DM couldn't even manually select-and-copy a note by hand) and deliberately not a CSS `pointer-events:none` hide (same problem) — and the Delete button omitted from the markup entirely rather than rendered-then-disabled, since there's nothing to gain from a delete button that can't do anything; Copy stays fully enabled either way, since copying to clipboard never writes anything and is exactly the kind of "findable and useful" action a read-only view should still support.
**A real bug caught and fixed before it ever shipped, not found by luck.** The obvious way to wire the new `readonly` param — `filtered.map(sccBuildTagNoteCard)` — would have been wrong: `Array.prototype.map`'s callback receives `(element, index, array)`, so the *index* would have landed in `sccBuildTagNoteCard`'s new second parameter instead of `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY`. Since `0` is falsy and every index after it is truthy, this would have silently rendered the *first* note in any list as fully editable and every note after it as read-only, regardless of whether audit mode was even active — a real, user-visible bug that would only show up with 2+ notes in a list, easy to miss in a quick test with just one. Caught during implementation by tracing the actual argument types `.map()` passes, not by testing into it; fixed by wrapping the call in an explicit closure, `filtered.map(function(n) { return sccBuildTagNoteCard(n, SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY); })`.
**`sccRenderTaggedNotes()` gained an optional `sessionNumOverride` param** — every one of its 4 existing call sites (`sccSwitchPanel('notes')`, `sccLiveSaveNote()`, `sccSubmitQuickNote()`, its own internal re-call inside `sccAddTagNote()`) passes nothing and is unaffected, still reading `SCC_SESSION.session_number` as before. The override exists specifically for the new `sccLoadHistorySession()` call, since that function deliberately never repoints `SCC_SESSION` at the historical session's row (confirmed during the audit — other code throughout this file assumes `SCC_SESSION` means "the live, editable session," and repointing it would have been a much larger, riskier change than this fix needed).
**A necessary follow-through, not named in the ask but required for correctness once (4) was wired in**: `sccLoadLatestSession()` — called by `sccLoadCurrentSession()` when a DM clicks "Back to Current Session" — never called `sccRenderTaggedNotes()` either, a pre-existing gap that was invisible before this round (there was nothing real to show in history view to begin with) but would have become a real, visible bug the moment (4) started actually loading historical notes: returning to the current session while on the Notes tab would have left the just-viewed historical session's read-only notes on screen, with the rest of the page correctly back to editable/live underneath it. Added the call to both `sccLoadLatestSession()`'s success and catch paths, matching how every other render call in that function is already duplicated across both branches.
**Verified live, end-to-end, using a safe synthetic test rather than touching the real, still-active Session 1 — reasoned through explicitly, not just assumed safe.** Confirmed via SQL first that Session 1 is still `session_status:'active'` with no real recap text saved yet — actually completing it, even temporarily, would be a real, consequential, unrequested production change (locks the real session, opens a real Session 2) with no clean single-step undo, so that path was ruled out deliberately rather than risked. Built the test instead from a synthetic `session_number:999` object pushed directly into `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` client-side (`session_notes` itself never touched), paired with one real, temporary `session_note_entries` row at that same session number (so the Tagged Notes half of the fix had genuine Supabase data to prove against, not a client-only stub that wouldn't exercise the real fetch path). `sccLoadHistorySession(999)`, called directly: confirmed via `getComputedStyle`/`offsetParent`-equivalent checks and direct property reads that all 5 recap fields show their real text read-only, including a deliberate 2-paragraph test string proving line breaks survive the swap; confirmed the Tagged Notes section stays visible (not `display:none`) with the real test note card rendered, `readonly` present on both its title and body, no `onchange` attribute on either, no delete button in the DOM at all, the copy button present; confirmed the add-row is hidden. Confirmed all 4 newly-guarded functions are genuine no-ops during this view by calling each directly (`sccAddTagNote()`, `sccLiveSaveNote()`, `sccSaveTagNoteField()` with a tampered title, `sccDeleteTagNote()`) and then reading the real row back via SQL — title unchanged, row still present, nothing new created. Confirmed the pre-existing FAB-modal guard (`sccOpenQuickNoteModal()`) still holds too. Screenshotted the full history view at a taller viewport (900×1400, to capture both the recap fields and Quick Notes section in one shot without fighting this session's own documented Browser-pane scroll limitation) — this is the headline "after" image, a stark contrast to the audit's own finding that this view previously rendered nothing but a section title. Clicked back to the current session (`sccLoadCurrentSession()`): confirmed `SCC_VIEWING_HISTORY` false, `SCC_SESSION.session_number` correctly back to the real `1`, all 5 textareas visible again with their real (empty) values, `SCC_NOTE_ENTRIES` correctly reloaded to Session 1's real (empty) list rather than the synthetic session's leftover note, add-row visible again — screenshotted this fully-restored state too. Tested `sccCompleteSession()`'s own new reset logic safely and end-to-end: confirmed first via a direct probe that a PATCH against a nonexistent `session_number` returns `ok:true, status:204` in this project's real PostgREST setup (a genuine, harmless no-op, not an assumption), then pointed `SCC_SESSION` at a synthetic nonexistent session number, pre-set a real filter and search term, and ran the actual `sccCompleteSession()` function with its confirm() dialog auto-accepted — confirmed both were reset to empty and `SCC_NOTE_ENTRIES` correctly re-rendered, then confirmed via a fresh SQL read that the real Session 1 row's `session_status` never left `active`. All test data (the one temporary `session_note_entries` row) deleted afterward; confirmed via a final SQL read that both `session_notes` and `session_note_entries` are back to their exact real baseline (1 unchanged active session, 0 tagged notes). A full page reload afterward confirmed zero console errors and the real Session 1 loading correctly once the Session tab initializes. **2026-08-18 (continuing the same session — Combat Encounter Tracker, two interaction-pattern fixes from the audit, both flagged the same tier of work: the condition picker's modal-to-dropdown conversion, and manual initiative reorder) — `dm.html`.**
**Condition picker — investigated the existing convention before writing anything new, per the explicit instruction to reuse it rather than invent one.** Read `.scc-picker-dropdown`/`.scc-picker-option` (the search-driven picker convention used by the combat-add box, loot-item search, etc.) and `.scc-history-panel`/`sccToggleHistory()` (a button-triggered anchored panel, closer in shape to this case since there's no text input involved — a plain click on a fixed set of options). Neither fits verbatim: `.scc-history-panel` is a single, static, always-in-DOM element toggled via a `.open` class, but the condition button is one of N per-row buttons living inside `#scc-initiative-list`, which gets fully `innerHTML`-replaced on nearly every state change — there's no stable single element to toggle a class on. `.scc-picker-dropdown`'s own `left:0;right:0` stretch-to-parent positioning assumes a `position:relative` wrapping container, which a body-appended, per-click-created panel doesn't have. Landed on a hybrid, reusing what genuinely transfers: `.scc-picker-option`'s row styling verbatim (real CSS `:hover` instead of the old modal's manual `onmouseover`/`onmouseout` JS), and the same body-append/remove-first lifecycle the OLD modal and the sibling Loot Award/Combat Summary popups already use in this exact file — but positioned via a live `getBoundingClientRect()` read against the clicked button rather than screen-centered, the same technique `sccPositionGlobalResults()` already established here for exactly this scenario (a fixed-position panel with no relative ancestor to anchor to). Colors/border/shadow/max-height copied directly from `.scc-picker-dropdown`'s real values rather than reusing the class name itself — reusing the class would have handed the panel's cleanup to this file's existing generic outside-click handler (`document.addEventListener('click', ...)` that empties `.scc-picker-dropdown.open` elements' innerHTML and removes the `.open` class, but does NOT remove the DOM node), which is correct for the *static* dropdowns it was built for but would leave an orphaned empty div in `document.body` for a create-on-click element like this one. Wrote a dedicated outside-click handler instead that calls `.remove()`, matching how every other body-appended overlay in this exact file's own neighborhood (Loot Award, Combat Summary) already fully removes itself rather than hiding-in-place.
**New behavior beyond a straight visual swap, matching how the rest of the app's pickers actually behave, not just how the old modal looked.** Clicking the same row's own "+ Condition" button a second time now toggles the dropdown closed instead of removing-then-immediately-recreating it (a real, if minor, functional gap in the old modal — it had no toggle-close at all, only an explicit "Close" button). A dedicated outside-click listener closes it too, mirroring the Session History panel's own established convention, adapted for a create-on-click element rather than a static one.
**A real correctness risk found while reasoning through the reorder feature below, fixed proactively rather than left as a known gap.** Both the new condition dropdown and the pre-existing Loot Award popup identify their combatant by a plain array index baked into their onclick handlers at the moment they're opened. Reordering (the new feature) or removing a combatant changes what that index actually refers to in `SCC_INITIATIVE` — a panel left open across such a change could apply a condition, a coin award, or an item award to whichever combatant now happens to occupy that slot, not the one the DM actually opened it for. Fixed at the single choke point both panels' lifecycles already pass through: `sccRenderInitiativeList()` now unconditionally closes both at the very top of every render. Verified this doesn't fight either panel's own "remove and reopen in place" refresh pattern (`sccAwardItem`/`sccUndoAwardItem`/`sccAwardCoins`/`sccAwardCoinsAll` all do this) by grepping for `sccRenderInitiativeList()` calls inside all four — none exist, so the new close-on-render only ever fires for renders triggered by something external to whichever panel is currently open (HP changes, Next Turn, Remove, or the new reorder), which is exactly the set of changes capable of invalidating a held index in the first place.
**Initiative reorder.** Small up/down arrow pair added per row, next to the initiative number — arrows only, matching the audit's own explicit recommendation that full drag-and-drop is more than this needs. New `sccMoveCombatant(idx, dir)` (`dir` is `-1` for up, `+1` for down) does one plain adjacent-slot array swap; the boundary arrows (first row's up, last row's down) render with the real HTML `disabled` attribute rather than a styled-only fake-disabled state, so there's no click event to even reach the handler at either edge. Deliberately does not touch either combatant's own rolled `initiative` number — only array order changes — so a manual reorder can freely diverge from the numbers instead of forcing them back in sync, which is the actual point per the audit's own framing (a Ready action interrupting the order, or a narrative tie-break, both need the *order* to move without a fake re-roll).
**`SCC_TURN_IDX` correctness — the part the ask flagged as most likely to have an edge case, worked through as a general proof rather than patched case-by-case.** A swap of two array slots only ever needs `SCC_TURN_IDX` updated when it currently equals one of the two slots involved, and if so, it becomes whichever of the two it *wasn't* — because a swap is symmetric, this single rule is provably sufficient for every real shape the instruction called out: the active combatant moving itself (its tracked index must follow it to the new slot), some other combatant swapping past the active one (the active combatant gets displaced into the mover's old slot, so the tracked index must follow *it*), and a swap that doesn't touch the active slot at all (nothing to update, since neither swapped position held it). Implemented as one small `if`/`else if`, not three separate code paths — the symmetry of a swap means one check covers all three cases correctly by construction, not by having enumerated and special-cased each one.
**Verified live end-to-end, every step through a real click on the actual rendered UI, not a direct function call.** Condition picker: real click on Grusk Enforcer's "+ Condition" button — dropdown opens anchored directly under it with all 18 real conditions from `window.SCC_CONDITIONS_DATA`; real click on "Poisoned" — confirmed `SCC_INITIATIVE[0].conditions` became `["Poisoned"]`, confirmed the green tag renders on the row, confirmed the panel auto-closed. Reopened the same button — confirmed Poisoned now renders genuinely disabled (`pointer-events:none`, dimmed, "(added)" label, no `onclick` attribute at all, not just visually muted). Clicked the same button again — confirmed toggle-close, no flicker-reopen. Reopened, then clicked an unrelated element (the Difficulty badge) — confirmed the outside-click handler correctly closed it. Reorder: staged a real 5-combatant fight with deterministic initiative values typed into the real staging inputs (Grusk Enforcer 20 → active at idx 0, Still Frogs 15, Hana 10, Kael 5, Visenya 1) so the test wasn't dependent on random rolls. Clicked Still Frogs' real up-arrow, swapping it past the active Grusk Enforcer — confirmed the array became `[Still Frogs, Grusk Enforcer, Hana, Kael, Visenya]` and `SCC_TURN_IDX` correctly became `1` (followed Grusk to its new slot, not left at the old numeric position `0`, which would have silently pointed at Still Frogs instead), confirmed via DOM that exactly one row carries `.active-turn` and it's Grusk Enforcer's. Clicked Grusk Enforcer's own down-arrow next (the active combatant moving itself) — confirmed it moved to idx `2`, `SCC_TURN_IDX` followed to `2`, confirmed the DOM highlight moved with it. Clicked Kael's down-arrow (swapping Kael/Visenya at idx 3/4, nowhere near the active slot at idx 2) — confirmed `SCC_TURN_IDX` stayed at `2` and Grusk Enforcer remained the DOM-confirmed active row, proving the untouched-slot case is genuinely inert rather than accidentally correct by luck. Confirmed both boundary arrows render with the real `disabled` attribute, and confirmed via direct out-of-bounds `sccMoveCombatant()` calls that the array is left byte-for-byte unchanged regardless of the attempt. Confirmed the new defensive close-on-render fix live: opened the condition dropdown on one row, reordered a *different* row, confirmed the dropdown closed itself automatically rather than risk a condition landing on the now-shifted wrong combatant. Screenshotted the dropdown open with real conditions listed, the Poisoned tag rendered on Grusk Enforcer, and the reordered list showing Still Frogs/Hana Sable at the top with the correct combatants in the correct slots. Ended combat via the real End Combat button afterward; confirmed via SQL that all 3 players' `gold_cp`/`hp_current`/death-save columns sit exactly at their known-clean baseline (900/880/5400 gold, full HP, 0/0 death saves) — nothing this round touched persisted data at all, since staging, conditions, and reorder are all pure in-memory `SCC_INITIATIVE` state, confirmed once again consistent with every prior round's finding on this exact point. Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

**2026-08-18 (continuing the same session — Combat Encounter Tracker, quantity input for staging multiples of the same creature in one action, next item off the audit below) — `dm.html`.**
Ask left the placement choice explicitly to Claude Code's judgment ("check both entry points and pick the one that fits more naturally without new visual clutter") — investigated both before writing any markup.
**Placement.** Two real candidate entry points: the search box (`#scc-pick-combatant`, dropdown results click through `sccPickerSelect('combatant',...)`) and the roster quick-add chips (`#scc-combat-session-creatures`, compact single-line pill buttons that already show a live "×N already added" count per creature). A qty input embedded on each chip would break their compact pill shape outright — chips are meant to stay small and wrap in a row. A qty input scoped only to the search row wouldn't reach the chips at all, leaving one of the two entry points without the feature. Chose one shared qty field living in the search row, read by whichever entry point is used next — covers both without cluttering either. Placed inside the already-`id`'d `#scc-combat-search-group` wrapper specifically so it inherits last round's mid-fight hide-on-Start-Combat fix automatically, with zero new lines needed in `sccStartCombat()`/`sccEndCombat()`'s existing toggle logic. Reused `.scc-shop-qty-input` (the Shop feature's own compact number-input class, built earlier this session) rather than adding new CSS, and a muted `&times;` prefix matching the exact glyph the roster chips already render for their own "×N" counts — the new control speaks the same visual language this app already established for "this multiplies," not a new one.
**Wiring.** `sccAddToCombatStaging(id, name, qty)` gained an optional third param, `Math.max(1, parseInt(qty) || 1)` — every pre-existing 2-arg call site (including `sccAddAllPlayers()`'s per-player loop, deliberately left untouched since "3x every player" isn't a sensible use of this feature) keeps adding exactly one, unchanged. The function now loops the combatant-object *construction* `qty` times inside the existing function body — moved the `creature`/`pc` lookups outside the loop (they don't depend on the iteration, a natural cleanup of restructuring for multi-add, not separately requested) — and calls `sccRenderStagingList()`/`sccUpdateDifficulty()` once after the loop, not once per iteration, so adding 3 doesn't cause 3 separate re-renders. New shared `sccCombatReadAndResetQty()` — reads `#scc-combat-add-qty`, resets it to `1` immediately (before the DOM even re-renders, so there's no window where a stale count could be read twice), returns the value — called from both `sccPickerSelect('combatant',...)` and `sccQuickAddCombatant()` instead of duplicating the same read-then-reset logic at each site.
**A deliberate non-decision, recorded rather than silently assumed.** Did not add a qty>1 guard for PCs, even though the search box's results can include players (the roster chips never do, so this only matters for the search path). Manually clicking a PC's own search result multiple times already creates duplicate PC entries today with zero guard — a pre-existing behavior, confirmed via the existing code comment right above this function ("SCC_COMBATANTS is a plain array keyed by index... duplicate ids are safe"), not something this task was scoped to touch. The ask's own instruction — "matching how manually clicking N times already works today — same resulting state, just fewer clicks" — is explicit enough that reproducing that exact behavior (rather than quietly adding a PC-specific guard nobody asked for) is the correct reading, not an oversight.
**Verified live end-to-end, every step through real typed input and real clicks on the actual rendered UI, not direct function calls.** Set the qty box to 3 via the real input, typed "Grusk Enforcer" into the real search box, clicked the real dropdown result — confirmed `SCC_COMBATANTS.length` went 0→3 in one action, confirmed all 3 are genuinely distinct objects (`SCC_COMBATANTS[0] !== SCC_COMBATANTS[1]`, not the same reference pushed 3 times), confirmed the qty box reset to "1" and the search box cleared (matching the pre-existing single-add close behavior). Set qty to 2, clicked the real "Grey Fluffets" roster chip — confirmed exactly 2 added (5 total), qty reset again. Left qty at its just-reset default of 1 and clicked "Still Frogs" — confirmed exactly 1 added (6 total), directly confirming the default single-add path is unchanged. Screenshotted the roster chip correctly reading "✓ Grusk Enforcer ×3" and the Difficulty badge correctly recalculating to Deadly for 3 Grusk Enforcers vs. 3 level-1 players. Confirmed via `offsetParent`/`getBoundingClientRect` (the correct check learned and applied consistently since last round's audit-mode verification — `getComputedStyle` on a descendant doesn't reflect an ancestor's `display:none`) that the qty input hides the instant Start Combat runs and reappears with real, non-zero dimensions after End Combat, with no code changes needed for either direction since it's a genuine descendant of the already-toggled wrapper. Screenshotted the fully-reset state after End Combat, qty box correctly back to a real, interactable "1". Zero Supabase writes this round — combat staging has never persisted (re-confirmed last round), so nothing needed reverting. Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

Scoped to exactly the 3 items named, no more: hide the encounter-builder mid-fight, wire the dead coin-award functions to a real button, extend audit-mode to the rest of the Combat panel.
**1 — Encounter-builder controls now hide on Start Combat.** Added `id="scc-combat-roster-label"` and `id="scc-combat-search-group"` to the two elements that didn't already have one (the "From Session Roster" label and the search box's wrapper div) so `sccStartCombat()` could toggle them by id, matching the exact pattern the function already used for staging/Start-Combat-button/Add-All-Players-button. **Extended the fix to Clear All**, found during the same pass to share the identical bug shape and not named in the original ask's 3-item list but squarely inside its stated goal ("closes the silent-failure bug where they stay clickable but do nothing") — `#scc-clear-all-btn` was never hidden either, and clicking it during an active fight silently wiped the now-orphaned `SCC_COMBATANTS` array (already diverged from `SCC_INITIATIVE`, which is what the visible fight actually reads) with zero visible effect. `sccEndCombat()` explicitly restores the roster label/chips/filters-bar/search-group on its existing reset path; Clear All's own visibility needed no explicit restore since `sccRenderStagingList()` (already called by `sccEndCombat()`) re-derives it from `SCC_COMBATANTS.length` on every invocation.
**2 — `sccAwardCoins()`/`sccAwardCoinsAll()` wired into a real button** inside `sccOpenLootAward()`'s popup (player-select + "Award" + "Split to All," shown only when the combatant has real coins staged, same visual slot as the existing per-item award rows). **Two real bugs found and fixed while wiring, not shipped as-was**: neither function reset the combatant's `loot.gp/sp/cp` after a successful award, meaning a coin award made mid-fight would have been swept a second time by `sccEndCombat()`'s own loot-collection loop (which sums whatever's still on `c.loot` for every non-PC combatant regardless of downed state) — a real double-count bug that was only ever harmless because the functions were unreachable before this change made them live. Both now zero the coins on success and re-render the popup in place, mirroring the item-award convention already established one function up. Separately, `sccAwardCoinsAll()` specifically updated `sheet.gold_cp` optimistically before its PATCH resolved and never checked `r.ok` — its sibling `sccAwardCoins()` already did this correctly (update only inside a `r.ok`-gated `.then()`); fixed to match, with a real error toast if any player's PATCH fails rather than a silent, unflagged desync between local and DB state.
**3 — `.scc-audit-mode` now gates the whole Combat panel, not just the filters bar.** The ask offered two resolutions and left the choice to Claude Code's judgment — went with the ask's own preferred framing: since Combat state has never persisted per session (confirmed in the earlier audit, no `combat_encounters` table exists anywhere), a historical session genuinely has nothing here to show, so the entire live tracker (encounter builder + initiative list, including a fight that happens to be active right now) is replaced by a plain explanatory note rather than left half-functional. New `#scc-combat-audit-note`, reusing the same blue "informational" tone already established for `.scc-readonly-banner` rather than inventing a new color. Two blanket rules do the actual hiding — `.scc-audit-mode .scc-combat-builder`/`.scc-initiative-tracker { display: none !important; }` — `!important` specifically because the initiative tracker's own inline `style=""` can be empty (visible) at the exact moment a DM switches into History mid-fight, and a stylesheet's `!important` correctly outranks a non-important inline style per the CSS cascade. The old, narrower `.scc-audit-mode .scc-combat-filters-bar` rule is now redundant (the whole builder it lived inside is covered) and was removed rather than left as a second, overlapping rule.

Tested Clear All separately, pre-combat — confirmed `SCC_COMBATANTS` empties while the builder controls stay visible throughout, since Clear All was never part of the hide-on-Start-Combat set to begin with. Fresh encounter for "Split to All": staged Grusk Enforcer with 15gp/3sp/7cp, downed it, clicked the real "Split to All" button — confirmed via SQL all 3 players' `gold_cp` rose by the exact predicted 512cp split each (Hana 3480→3992, Kael 880→1392, Visenya 5400→5912), confirming both the split arithmetic and the new `r.ok`-gated update. Ended combat via the real End Combat button both times — first confirmed the Combat Summary popup correctly read "No coins collected. No items to award." after the earlier single-player award (direct proof the double-count fix held, not just that the award itself worked), second confirmed the builder controls fully reappear after End Combat too, not only after Clear All. For audit mode: staged a fresh live fight, opened Session History via the real button, clicked into the one real session (Session 1) via the real history-list item — confirmed via `getComputedStyle` the note shows with its exact text and both `.scc-combat-builder`/`#scc-initiative-tracker` are `display:none`; confirmed via `offsetParent`/`getBoundingClientRect` (not `getComputedStyle` on the HP input directly, which reports the element's own un-inherited display value and would have been a false negative here) that the HP input is genuinely unreachable — `offsetParent === null`, zero-size rect. Screenshotted the note in place. Clicked "Back to Current Session" — confirmed the still-technically-active fight (untouched throughout, since the fix is CSS/audit-class-only, never a state reset) reappeared exactly as left, screenshotted. All test data reverted: all 3 players' `gold_cp` restored to their real values (900/880/5400) via SQL, confirmed via a final read; `SCC_COMBATANTS`/`SCC_INITIATIVE` needed no server-side cleanup, never persisted in the first place.
