tables, confirmed populated not stubbed), Start Combat (`sccStartCombat`, builds `SCC_INITIATIVE` as a one-time sorted copy of `SCC_COMBATANTS`), initiative/turn order (`sccRenderInitiativeList`/`sccNextTurn`), HP (`sccAdjustHP`/`sccSetHP` — real `character_sheet.hp_current` PATCH for PCs, in-memory only for creatures), Conditions (`sccPromptCondition`/`sccAddConditionFromModal`/`sccRemoveCondition` — reads the real `conditions` table via `window.SCC_CONDITIONS_DATA`, writes only to `SCC_INITIATIVE[idx].conditions`, in-memory), death saves (real `character_sheet.death_save_successes/failures` PATCH), Award Loot (real `inventory` POST), Remove/Clear All (pure array mutation), End Combat (real coin auto-split via `character_sheet.gold_cp` PATCH to every player, items routed into a Combat Summary popup for manual per-item `inventory` POST). Confirmed via `list_tables` against the live Supabase schema (25 real tables) that no `combat_encounters` table or anything resembling one exists — the entire encounter is, and has always been, 100% ephemeral by design, not an oversight.
**Checked specifically for a 4th instance of the "silent shadow state" bug shape** (found 3 times already this session — Party HP, Conditions/Inspiration, Field Guide staleness) — did not find a 4th instance of that exact shape, but found a structurally related, previously-unflagged issue instead: it isn't stale-vs-persisted data here, it's the encounter correctly never persisting at all, combined with a real bug in how that ephemeral state interacts with the rest of the UI (below).
**Step 2 — cross-compare.** (1) **A real, previously-unflagged bug**: `.scc-combat-builder` (search box, filters bar, roster quick-add chips) is never hidden once `sccStartCombat()` runs — confirmed via grep, the class is referenced exactly twice in the whole file (its own markup and its own CSS rule), no JS ever toggles it. Only `#scc-combatant-staging`, `#scc-start-combat-btn`, and `#scc-add-all-players-btn` get `display:none`. Traced the consequence precisely: `sccPickerSelect('combatant',...)`/`sccQuickAddCombatant()` both route into `sccAddToCombatStaging()`, which pushes onto `SCC_COMBATANTS` and re-renders the now-hidden `#scc-combatant-staging` — but `SCC_INITIATIVE` (what `sccRenderInitiativeList()` actually reads) was built as a one-time `SCC_COMBATANTS.slice().sort(...)` at Start Combat and is never resynced afterward. Net effect, confirmed by reading both functions rather than assumed: there is no way to add a mid-fight reinforcement today, and it fails completely silently — the controls look fully functional. Neither is a superset: Combat's version has no read_aloud/dm_notes/description/behavior; the lookup drawer's version has no Attacks/Saving Throws/Skills/Resistances/Immunities/Reactions/Legendary Actions. (4) **Always-visible vs. reference-behind-a-click, checked against the standard the DM already set** ("otherwise I have no idea what to do with them," from an earlier session's Combat investigation) — confirmed this standard is currently met: name/PC-tag/AC badge/HP bar/Conditions/Downed badge are all in the always-visible collapsed initiative row, full stat block and Loot correctly stay one click away. Not a data-integrity risk the way an editable Setup field would be (nothing about Combat is ever saved per-session to begin with), but a real inconsistency with how audit mode works everywhere else in this file, and a real risk of a DM accidentally mutating live PC state while believing they're in a read-only view. No real encounter pre-staging exists — re-confirmed via a fresh `list_tables` call this round, not recalled from memory. (8) **Found while reading Loot/End Combat, not asked for but relevant**: `sccAwardCoins(combatantIdx)` and `sccAwardCoinsAll(combatantIdx)` are fully written, fully working (real `character_sheet.gold_cp` PATCH, same shape as the code End Combat's auto-split already uses) — and fully dead, confirmed via a full-file grep for both function names finding zero call sites anywhere. Today the only path coins take to reach a player is the automatic equal split at End Combat; there's no way to award one specific enemy's stash to one specific player mid-fight despite the code to do exactly that already existing.

Scope held exactly as given: NPCs and Locations only. Creatures/Items in the Field Guide sidebar were never touched — confirmed via a final diff read that `sccFgCardDetail()`'s `creature`/`item`/`dm` branches and the sidebar's own list-row summary logic are byte-identical to before this session.
**Step 1 — re-verified fresh, not trusted from the Phase 2 audit's own summary, per explicit instruction.** Read all 3 real render sites for each entity type directly rather than reasoning from memory of the earlier audit. NPC: Live Session's `sccFilterNpcs()`, the lookup drawer's `sccOpenLookup('character', ...)` branch, Field Guide sidebar's `sccFgCardDetail(e, 'npc')` — full verified field union: `read_aloud`, `personality`, `summary`, `appearance`, `motivation`, `contradiction`, `player_knowledge`, `gigglegloom_relationship`, a category/pronouns/status/affiliation fields row, `associated` (world-association tag cloud), `tags`, and a DM-only block (`secret`/`dm_notes`/linked-quest chips). Confirmed via `information_schema` that `world_characters` genuinely has no `description` column — Live Session's old `c.summary || c.description` fallback was dead code, not a real fourth field being merged away. Location: Live Session's `sccRenderLocationPanel()`, the lookup drawer's `sccOpenLookup('city', ...)` branch (serves cities/POIs/nations through one type), Field Guide sidebar's `sccFgCardDetail(e, 'location')` (POI-only) — full verified union: `read_aloud`, `summary`, `description`, `strategic_importance`, `tone`, `culture`, `beliefs` (nations only), `gigglegloom_notes`, `gigglegloom_relationship`, a `formery_present`/`government`+`capital_city` fields row, Population (cities only), Landmarks (cities only), Structures (cities only), Tags, a DM-only block (`vareth_presence`+`dm_notes`), and — POIs only — the real Shop section. Confirmed via `information_schema`, not assumed from the Step 1 NPC pass's own reasoning: `pois` has none of `landmarks`/`structures`/`population_data`/`formery_present`; `nations` has none of `landmarks`/`structures`/`dm_notes`/`color_health`/`strategic_importance`/`tone`/`formery_present`/`read_aloud` — every card's real field list is genuinely conditional on per-collection schema, confirmed rather than templated-and-hoped.
**The 4 flagged complex pieces, investigated in the depth asked for before any component code was written.** NPC linked-quest chips are not a stored field — computed fresh via a new `sccNpcLinkedQuests(npcId)` scanning `quests.related` for a matching `{collection:'characters', id}` entry, so any shared card has to compute this live, not just read a column. Landmarks vs. Structures confirmed genuinely separate systems via both code and schema, not a naming collision to reconcile: `landmarks[]` performs real entity-link resolution (a real `entry_id` substitutes the linked `CITIES`/`POIS` entity's own name/description/image), `structures[]` is a category-grouped directory where an `entry_id` only ever adds a static 🔗 flag icon with no resolution behind it — kept both, unchanged, side by side, since merging them would be an unrequested redesign of two different features, not deduplication. POI Shop confirmed as real transactional functionality (live buyer select, price/qty override inputs, a wired Buy button, an add-stock search box) that had to survive with zero functional regression, not just visually — treated with the same weight as the other 3 pieces rather than assumed safe because "it's just another section."
**Step 2 build.** New `sccBuildNpcCardHeaderHtml(c, opts)`/`sccBuildNpcCardBodyHtml(c)` and `sccBuildLocationCardHeaderHtml(loc, opts)`/`sccBuildLocationCardBodyHtml(loc)` — header/body deliberately split rather than one monolithic template, since the 3 sites need genuinely different shells (Live Session's card is click-to-expand with its own chevron/Quick-Look button; the lookup drawer wraps the header in its own `.scc-ql-header` divider and shows a `player_facing` badge via `opts.showPfBadge` that no other site renders; Field Guide's sidebar only ever consumes the body, since its own card-row/expand mechanism was explicitly out of scope). Content styling is `.scc-npc2-*` (Live Session's richer per-section icon+title+body treatment) as the primary look everywhere, with `.scc-ql-row`/`.scc-ql-tag`/`.scc-ql-fields`/`.scc-ql-header` reused pragmatically where they're a better fit for the content shape (label/value rows, tag clouds, the lookup drawer's own divider) — a deliberate, judged reuse rather than an accidental blend of two competing systems. Wired into all 3 sites: `sccFilterNpcs()`/`sccRenderLocationPanel()` (Live Session — `sccRenderLocationPanel()`'s own local `healthBadge()` helper is gone, folded into the shared header), `sccOpenLookup()`'s `character`/`city` branches (each replacing roughly 120 lines of duplicated field-rendering with one call), and `sccFgCardDetail()`'s `npc`/`location` branches only.
**3 real bugs found and fixed while preserving "existing interactive behavior... correctly," not part of the original ask but required by it.** (1) The NPC card's Linked Quests chip called the dead `sccSwitchPanel('quests')` (a target Phase 3a removed) — fixed to `sccOpenLookup('quest', q.id)`, matching this file's established cross-reference pattern, verified live from all 3 sites. (2) Relationship-badge cycling only ever refreshed Live Session's own render — cycling from inside the lookup drawer updated that badge but left Live Session's copy of the same NPC stale, a real cross-site desync where a DM could see two different relationship states for one NPC depending on which panel was last touched. Fixed via new `SCC_LOOKUP_TYPE`/`SCC_LOOKUP_ID` tracking globals (set in `sccOpenLookup()`, cleared in `sccCloseLookup()`) so `sccCycleRelationship2()` can re-open the drawer on itself when it's currently showing the NPC being cycled, alongside its existing Live Session refresh. (3) A newly-exposed async race, the same shape as Phase 3a's Party HP bug found and fixed 2 rounds ago: the Location card's Shop section reads `SCC_PLAYERS` for its buyer list, but `sccLoadPlayers()`'s async fetch chain can resolve after `sccRenderLocationPanel()`'s initial synchronous render — since `sccToggleLoc()` only shows/hides pre-baked HTML with no re-render, a card built during that window showed "No players" permanently. Newly exposed specifically because Locations now assembles its full Shop UI at initial page load, where before Shop only ever built on-demand inside the lookup drawer, well after `SCC_PLAYERS` had settled. Fixed identically to the Phase 3a pattern — added `sccRenderLocationPanel()` to `sccLoadPlayers()`'s existing completion callback.
**Step 3 verification, field-by-field and site-by-site, not eyeballed or assumed from the code alone.** NPC: Dran (rich DM-only content — `secret`+`dm_notes` populated — but genuinely empty `tags`/`associated`/`gigglegloom_relationship`/`read_aloud`, confirmed via SQL before treating their absence as correct rather than a regression) checked at Live Session and the lookup drawer; Gobblewump Gerald (tags, `gigglegloom_relationship`, `read_aloud`, `secret`, `dm_notes` all populated, and — confirmed via SQL — genuinely linked to the real quest "Something Is Wrong With Gerald") checked at Field Guide sidebar for the fullest field sweep: DOM-confirmed every section title present (Visualization/Personality/Summary/Appearance/What They Know/Gigglegloom Relationship/Secret/Notes/Linked Quests), confirmed the Linked Quests chip carries the fixed `onclick="sccOpenLookup('quest','something-is-wrong-with-gerald')"` rather than the dead panel-switch call, confirmed tags render, screenshotted. Location: Mira Pont's Sundry Goods (a POI — real DM-only shop-pricing notes, real tags) checked at all 3 sites — Live Session (full card screenshotted: header/Visualization/Summary/Description/Tags/DM-only block/Shop), the lookup drawer, and Field Guide sidebar (DOM-confirmed Visualization/Summary/Description/Tags/Notes/Shop all present, screenshotted) — confirmed Landmarks/Structures correctly absent everywhere for this entity since POIs genuinely carry neither column (re-confirmed via SQL at verification time, not just recalled from Step 1). **POI Shop tested end-to-end through a real click, not a direct function call**: added a temporary real `vendor_stock` row (Torch, catalog price, unlimited qty) at Mira Pont's, confirmed the buyer select listed all 3 real players, set the buyer to Kael Evander, fired the actual Buy button's own click handler — confirmed via SQL his `gold_cp` dropped 880→879 (exactly Torch's 0.01gp price) and a real `inventory` row landed (`quantity:1, dm_granted:false`), confirmed via DOM read the card stayed open and the Shop section correctly re-rendered in place afterward (the pre-existing re-render-in-place convention, now confirmed to also hold at Live Session, not just the lookup drawer it was originally built for). All test data reverted: Kael's `gold_cp` back to 880, the test inventory row deleted, the temporary `vendor_stock` row deleted, `session_notes.npc_relationships` re-confirmed unchanged at `{"oro":"ally"}` — all via a final SQL read. Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty (one literal 💠 caught mid-build via the same recurring Edit-tool escape-normalization issue on record in this file's history, fixed via the established Python exact-string-replace workaround before it reached this checkpoint).

**2026-08-18 (continuing the same session — Live Session Phase 3a, the small/medium wins from the Phase 2 audit strategy below; Phase 3b, the NPC/Location field-union, deliberately not started) — `dm.html`.**
Ask was 10 items across 4 categories (Cut / Restructure / New Widget / Fix), explicitly scoped away from the riskiest piece (the field-union merging NPC/Location detail across 2-3 render sites) — that stayed untouched, exactly as instructed.
**Investigation before any edit.** Read the full 7-panel markup (`#scc-panel-npcs/locations/quests/combat/notes/live/fieldguide`), `sccSwitchPanel()`, every render function each panel's content depends on, the existing `sccTogglePrep()` collapsible pattern (reused rather than inventing a new one), and the layout CSS chain (`#form-session.active{height:100%}` → `.scc-panel{flex:1;overflow-y:auto}`) to understand *why* Field Guide's 2-pane layout needs a definite height, before deciding how to give it one inside a new home. Grepped every `sccSwitchPanel('npcs'|'locations'|'quests'|'fieldguide')` call site (found: only the 4 button definitions themselves, no other code called them) — this single fact is what made the whole restructure low-risk: nothing else in the codebase assumes those 4 panels are separately reachable.
**Markup surgery, done via Python exact-string extraction, not hand-retyped.** Extracted the NPCs/Locations/Quests panels' `.scc-panel-inner` divs and Field Guide's `.scc-fg-layout` div verbatim from the live file (byte-for-byte, via `str.find()` boundary markers, each extraction printed and eyeballed before use) rather than retyping ~9000 characters of existing markup by hand — the established lesson from this file's own history about the Edit tool's escape-normalization risk applies just as much to *accidentally introducing a transcription error* in a huge block as it does to Unicode escapes specifically. Reassembled into the new `#scc-panel-live` structure (control strip + `.scc-live-accordion` of 5 `.scc-collapsible-toggle`/`.scc-collapsible-body` pairs) and verified via `node --check`-equivalent + a full diff read before ever loading it in a browser.
**Real reasoning behind what got cut vs. kept, not just following the list mechanically.** DM Safety Net and Quick Actions weren't named in the ask's control-strip enumeration (Party HP/Conditions/Inspiration/current-beat/Die Roller/Timer) but also weren't listed under CUT — kept both rather than silently dropping real content the ask never said to remove. Quest Progress's own full beat list (`sccLiveRenderBeats()`) *was* silently removed even though it wasn't explicitly named in CUT — justified by the audit's own finding #1 ("Quest beat-progress tracking is genuinely duplicated") and the new Current Beat widget's explicit job ("shows only the currently active beat prominently," replacing a full editable list with a compact read-only summary) — keeping all three (old full list + new widget + Quests section) would have made the duplication worse, not better, directly contradicting the round's own stated goal. Recorded as a judgment call, not silently absorbed.
**Field Guide sizing, reasoned from the actual CSS cascade, not guessed.** `.scc-fg-layout{height:100%}` previously resolved against `#scc-panel-fieldguide.active{display:flex}`, itself a flex child of the fixed-height `#form-session.active`. Once Field Guide became an accordion section inside a naturally-growing scrollable page, that percentage-height chain had nothing left to resolve against. Rather than guess at a fix, traced exactly which ancestor needed a real height and added it at the correct single point — `.scc-collapsible-body.scc-live-acc-body--fieldguide.open{height:70vh}` — so `.scc-fg-layout`'s existing internal 2-pane independent-scroll behavior keeps working exactly as before, just inside a bounded box instead of the whole viewport. Made Field Guide's data load lazily on first expand (`sccLiveOpenSection('fieldguide')` triggers `sccRenderFieldGuide()`/`sccFgInitDocument()`, both already internally cached/idempotent) rather than eagerly at page load — no reason to fire 5 parallel fetches for a section collapsed by default.
**Two real async-timing bugs found during verification, not assumed away.** (1) Live-tested the fully-collapsed default view immediately after the markup landed and found Party HP genuinely rendering 0/0 for all 3 real players despite the top Party bar showing correct 12/12 data — traced to `sccLoadPlayers()` never having refreshed Live Session's own HP/Conditions/Inspiration widgets, invisible before since `sccRenderLivePanel()` only ever ran on a manual tab click (by which point the fetch had almost always already resolved); newly exposed because Live Session now renders synchronously at page load. Fixed by adding the 3 missing render calls to `sccLoadPlayers()`'s own completion callback. (2) Tested auto-expand fresh (real in-progress beat set via SQL, hard page reload) and found Quests stayed collapsed despite the Current Beat widget correctly showing the real data — traced to `sccLiveApplyAutoExpand()` being called from inside `sccRenderQuestPanel()` itself, which runs synchronously *before* `SCC_PROGRESS` has actually loaded in the bootstrap flow; the guard's first (premature, empty-data) call latched it permanently closed before the real data ever arrived. Fixed by removing the call from `sccRenderQuestPanel()` entirely and calling it only from the 3 specific bootstrap sites, after their own `sccLoadQuestProgress()` promise has genuinely resolved — re-verified with the identical SQL-set-then-reload test, confirmed Quests now auto-opens correctly while the other 4 sections stay collapsed.
**Verification, comprehensive, every item live-tested with real data and real reverts.** All 5 sections expand/collapse independently with correct real content (11 NPCs, real Locations with health badges, the real Quests card with rail+badges, 8 real Scenario groups, Field Guide's real document). Rail click-to-cycle confirmed still fully interactive (beat 1 → in_progress, confirmed via SQL, Current Beat widget and the Quests section's own sidebar box updated identically from the one shared `sccComputeActiveBeat()`). Beat status badges confirmed via DOM inspection to be real non-interactive `<span>`s, not `<select>`s. "+ Add Scenario" confirmed landing on the real Setup scenario editor with the jump-highlight visible. Quick Note: real note typed and saved, confirmed via SQL it POSTed into `session_note_entries` with `category:'dm-only'` and left `session_notes.player_decisions` untouched (`null`), confirmed it appears in the real Notes tab's Tagged Notes list, deleted afterward. Quest Dashboard: real completed+in-progress beat pair set via SQL, opened the Dashboard in a tab that never visited the Session tab first (the exact gap being fixed) and confirmed real status icons/XP rendered — reverted. Cross-checked zero regression on Combat, Notes, and the global-search lookup drawer's quest branch (now sharing `sccBuildReadOnlyBeatsHtml()` with the Dashboard) via real interaction, not assumption. Zero console errors across every check, multiple fresh tabs, multiple reloads. Screenshotted both the fully-collapsed default state and the fully-expanded state. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty (one literal checkmark caught in my own new toast string during this same check, fixed to the `✓` escape before it shipped).

**2026-08-18 (continuing the same session — fixed the Conditions/Inspiration data-loss bug the Phase 2 audit below found, item 3 of "Live Session") — `dm.html`.**
Ask was explicit and 3-part: confirm the real columns, repoint the controls to them (removing the shadow state entirely), and reason through whether this conflicts with the character sheet editing the same fields.
**1 — Columns confirmed, not guessed.** Grepped `sheet/index.html`'s own Conditions picker and Inspiration toggle (`addCondition()`/`removeCondition()`/`toggleInspiration()`) — both read/write `SHEET.conditions`/`SHEET.has_inspiration` via `saveSheet()`. Cross-checked against the live schema directly: `character_sheet.conditions` (array) and `character_sheet.has_inspiration` (boolean), keyed by `character_id` — real, populated (if currently empty) columns, not a stale/dead pair.
**2 — Repointed, shadow state fully removed.** `SCC_LIVE_CONDITIONS`/`SCC_LIVE_INSPIRATION` (module-level globals, confirmed via a full-file grep to have zero references outside the 6 functions being rewritten) deleted outright, replaced with an explanatory comment rather than left as dead declarations. All 6 dependent functions (`sccLiveRenderConditions`/`sccLiveAddCondition`/`sccLiveConfirmCondition`/`sccLiveRemoveCondition`/`sccLiveRenderInspiration`/`sccLiveToggleInspiration`) rewritten to read `SCC_SHEETS[playerId]` — the exact cache `sccLiveAdjustHp`/`sccLiveSetHp` already read for Party HP, two rows above these in the same panel — and PATCH `character_sheet` via `sccPatch('character_sheet', '?character_id=eq.'+encodeURIComponent(id), {...})`, matching HP's fire-and-forget-with-toast-on-failure pattern exactly rather than inventing a new persistence style for this one panel. Confirmed via grep that Combat tab's separate `SCC_INITIATIVE[idx].conditions` (per-encounter, genuinely meant to be in-memory) is untouched — a different concept, not a second instance of the same bug.
**3 — Conflict check, reasoned through rather than assumed safe.** This now shares a write target with the player's own sheet, same as HP already does — plain last-write-wins PATCH, no locking, no realtime sync anywhere in this app. That's an already-accepted risk class here (HP has worked this way since it was built), so Conditions/Inspiration follow the identical model rather than getting a bespoke read-only mode — a DM setting a condition mid-fight and a player toggling their own Inspiration are both legitimate, both should land in the one real place either surface already looks.
**Mechanical note**: the Edit tool's string-matching repeatedly failed against this block on the first several attempts because the source uses the literal 6-character text `•` (not a rendered bullet glyph) inside a JS string literal — confirmed via `xxd` before finally getting a clean match via a direct Python exact-string replace, the established workaround this file's own history already documents for this exact class of Edit-tool mismatch.
Verified live, both directions, against real Supabase writes — not just DOM state. Set Hana Sable's condition to "Poisoned" and toggled Kael Evander's Inspiration on through the actual Live Session UI (real click-equivalent calls into `sccLiveAddCondition`→pick→`sccLiveConfirmCondition`, `sccLiveToggleInspiration`), confirmed both landed in Supabase via direct SQL immediately after. Fully reloaded the page in a fresh tab (re-authenticated, re-navigated Session → Live Session from scratch) and confirmed both persisted — Hana's "Poisoned" chip and Kael's active Inspiration token both rendered correctly from a freshly-fetched `SCC_SHEETS`, the exact case that was silently broken before. Separately opened both characters' real `sheet/index.html` (via their real tokens) and confirmed the identical state renders there — Kael's header shows the gold "INSPIRATION — Yes" badge, Hana's shows a "Poisoned ×" pill — confirming this is genuinely one shared column read by two surfaces, not two independent copies that happened to agree. Screenshotted all three surfaces. Reverted both test values afterward (`conditions:[]`, `has_inspiration:false` on both, confirmed via a final SQL read matching the real pre-test state on all 3 characters) since neither reflects real session state. Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

**2026-08-18 (new session — Phase 2 comprehensive audit of Live Session, Field Guide, and the NPCs/Quests/Locations sub-tabs, plus the separate Quest Dashboard, ahead of a possible merge into one "Live Session" tab. Investigation and a written strategy only, no code changed.) — `dm.html` read-only; full report published as an artifact, this entry is a condensed record.**
Delegated the 3-part read to parallel research agents rather than doing it serially myself, given the scope (7 sub-tabs' worth of code across a 16,000+ line file) — each agent instructed explicitly to read the real current code and cite real function/column names, not summarize from memory or trust the task's own background guesses without verifying them. Synthesized their 3 independent reports into one cross-comparison and merge strategy myself, since that step requires holding all the findings at once, not something to also delegate.
**Live Session** (`#scc-panel-live`, `sccRenderLivePanel`): 12 real features enumerated — Quest Progress (beat cycling, mutates real `quest_progress`), Party HP (real, persists to `character_sheet.hp_current`), Conditions (in-memory only, confirmed never persisted anywhere), Inspiration (same — in-memory only), Quick Actions (nav buttons), a local global-search box (own reimplementation, narrower than the header's), Scenarios (reads `session_notes.session_scenarios`, "used" state in-memory only, no in-panel editor), Quick Note (writes into a *different* sub-panel's own DOM textarea, `session_notes.player_decisions`), NPCs This Session (thin chip list), Die Roller (self-contained), Timer (in-memory, confirmed it keeps running across tab switches since it's never torn down), DM Safety Net (static text). Confirmed only Quest Progress and NPCs read `SCC_SELECTED` at all — Locations and Items assigned in Setup have zero representation anywhere in this panel.
**Field Guide** (`#scc-panel-fieldguide`): confirmed two fully independent sub-features sharing no code. (a) Sidebar — session-scoped read-only reference cards for NPC/Creature/Location/Item/DM-Notes, filtered by Setup's assignments, falling back to unfiltered if none. (b) Document — `field_guide_sessions` table, one row per session, a `blocks` jsonb array; confirmed the real current box-color enum is 5 values (`grey/green/magenta/gold/yellow` — blue/purple fully retired, not 6 as the task's own background note guessed), confirmed `[[type:id|Label]]` link parsing (`sccFgLinkify`) supports exactly 6 entity types (narrower than the 7-type "open in new tab" set elsewhere), confirmed the editor holds a full in-memory working copy with one Save button doing one PATCH/POST of the whole array — not per-field autosave. Everything in the document is DM-authored; only the loot-icon lookup and link rendering are computed.
**NPCs/Quests/Locations sub-panels + Quest Dashboard**: confirmed the Session-tab "Quests" sub-panel and the top-level Quest Dashboard tab are two completely uncoupled surfaces with no code path linking them, despite both operating on `quests`/`quest_progress` — worth stating plainly since it would be easy to assume one is a subset of the other. For each of the 3 sub-panels, precisely answered "what can a DM do here that global search + the lookup drawer can't": NPCs — see `read_aloud`/personality/secret/dm_notes/linked-quests (none in the lookup drawer's character card), lose category/pronouns/status/affiliation/motivation/contradiction/appearance/associations/tags (all lookup-only); Locations — see `read_aloud`/landmarks(with real entity-link resolution)/dm_notes, lose a much longer list (strategic_importance/tone/culture/beliefs/gigglegloom/government/population/structures/tags/Shop) — confirmed this is the most lopsided of the three, no side is a subset of the other; Quests — the sub-panel is the *only* place in the app that writes `quest_progress` at all (the lookup drawer's identical-looking beat rail is `pointer-events:none`), while the Quest Dashboard is the only place that edits `quest.status` and the only place that shows `summary`/`reveals`/`related`/suggested-level, and confirmed via a full grep that the Dashboard never fetches `quest_progress` at all — a real, non-cosmetic gap if a DM ever tries to run a session from that tab.
**Cross-comparison**, all specific findings quoted in the report rather than repeated here in full: 4 genuine duplicates (beat-progress tracking, the local search box, the NPCs-chip stub, relationship-cycling implemented twice), 5 near-duplicates flagged as genuinely confusing (NPC detail split 3 ways, Location detail split 2 ways, Quest capability split 3 ways by *mechanism* not just fields, Conditions/Inspiration looking real but not persisting, "Quick Note" not actually being a categorized note), plus real gaps against the original stated pain points (no current-beat-to-NPC/location cross-referencing, zero location progress-tracking, no mid-session way to add a Scenario, and a genuine reliability gap — Conditions/Inspiration/scenario-used-marks/the Timer are all silently lost on a page reload mid-session).
**Recommended merge strategy**, reported not built, full detail in the artifact: one Live Session tab with an always-visible control strip (HP, Conditions and Inspiration once fixed to persist, current beat, dice, timer) plus a row of collapsible sections below it (NPCs/Locations/Quests/Scenarios/Field Guide) reusing this app's own established accordion convention rather than a vague "make it accessible" — sections default collapsed except whichever is contextually relevant, expand in place, never navigate away from the tab. NPC/Location card rendering unions the currently-split field sets into one shared component used everywhere (replacing 2-3 independent copies); the two beat-progress renderers merge into one. Quest Dashboard stays separate (genuinely non-overlapping purpose confirmed above) with one additive fix. Quick Note reroutes to the real `session_note_entries` table. Every recommendation tagged with a build-size estimate (small: search-box removal, section reorg, exposing the existing Setup scenario-editor into Live Session; medium: the current-beat widget, Quick-Note rerouting, Dashboard progress-awareness; large, flagged explicitly as needing their own dedicated passes: the NPC/Location field-union component, and migrating Conditions/Inspiration to the real persisted `character_sheet` columns, which is a real behavior/sync change, not a rewire).
No `dm.html` changes this session. Confirmed via `git status` before pushing that only this file (plus the pre-existing, unrelated `.DS_Store`) changed.

**2026-08-17 (same session as the investigation below — DM confirmed the recommended design, built the 5 carryover-suggestion rows, "Open in new tab"-adjacent Combat Encounters explicitly deferred) — `dm.html`.**
Scoped exactly to what was confirmed: the 5 existing categories (Locations/NPCs/Creatures/Quests/Items) get a "Suggested from Session N" row; Combat Encounters is untouched, per the investigation's own conclusion that it needs a real schema decision first, not a UI-only add.
**Markup**: one label + one chip-row div added per `.scc-picker-group`, above its search controls — reused Combat's own `.scc-combat-session-label`/`.scc-combat-session-creatures` classes verbatim rather than inventing near-duplicate CSS, since the visual/behavioral shape (label + wrapping row of pill chips) is identical to what Combat's "From Session Roster" row already does. Both start `display:none` in the static HTML so there's nothing to flash before JS decides whether to show them.
**`sccPreviousSessionRow()`**: the one piece of real design judgment in this build, not in the original investigation. Reads the hidden `#scc-session-number` input rather than `SCC_SESSION.session_number` — `SCC_SESSION` is `null` for the entire window between a session completing and the next one being saved (confirmed by reading `sccCompleteSession()`/`sccLoadLatestSession()`), which is exactly when a DM opening a fresh Setup would most want to see what carried over. `sccSetSessionDisplay()` already keeps that input correct in both the real-session and pending-next-session branches, so reading it instead of `SCC_SESSION` directly costs nothing and covers a real case the naive version would have silently gone blank for.
**`sccRenderSuggestedRow(type)`** is pure and synchronous — no fetch of its own — so it's safe to call on every chip mutation; wired into the tail of the existing `sccRenderChips(type)` (already the one function every add/remove path already calls), so the ✓-already-added state stays correct regardless of whether a chip came from the suggested row, the search box, or Browse All. Hides both the label and the row — not an empty-state message — the moment there's nothing to show, checked at 3 separate points (no previous session; previous session assigned nothing in this category; nothing it assigned still resolves against the live catalog, e.g. a since-deleted entity).
**Quest in-progress badge**: needed `SCC_PROGRESS` populated for the *previous* session's quests specifically — the existing `sccLoadQuestProgress()` call in `sccLoadLatestSession()` only ever covers the *current* session's own `quest_ids_active`, so a quest carried over from last time would never have had its beat data loaded at all. `sccRenderSuggestedRows()` (the one-time orchestrator, called from both the success and catch paths of `sccLoadLatestSession()`) issues one extra `sccLoadQuestProgress()` call for the previous session's quest ids, merging into the same shared `SCC_PROGRESS` object every other beat-status UI in the app already reads from — not a parallel/duplicate cache.
**Verification, real and synthetic, both reported honestly.** Real data: confirmed live (not assumed from the investigation, which was done in an earlier turn) that only Session 1 exists — reloaded Setup fresh, confirmed via direct DOM read that all 5 suggested-row elements exist in markup but are genuinely `display:none` with empty `innerHTML` (not just visually absent by coincidence), and `sccPreviousSessionRow()` returns `null`. Screenshotted this real, current, correct resting state. Synthetic test: pushed one fake `session_number:0` row directly into the in-memory `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` array — never touched Supabase, so a page reload is a complete, guaranteed revert, confirmed afterward (`SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` back to exactly the 1 real row). First attempt at the injection raced `switchTab('setup')`'s own fresh data reload and got silently overwritten — not a code bug, a test-sequencing mistake, caught by re-checking `sccPreviousSessionRow()` came back `null` when it shouldn't have, then redone correctly by waiting for the real fetch to settle first. Once sequenced correctly: all 5 rows rendered with real resolved names; the ✓-already-added state correctly triggered for 3 of the 5 test picks that happened to already be in the current session's own chips (a real, not staged, exercise of that branch); a real click on the one not-yet-added suggested chip correctly added it via `sccAddChipById` and the chip itself flipped to checked, confirmed via both DOM read and screenshot. **2026-08-17 (new session — Session Setup redesign investigation, 3-part report, no code changed) — investigated Combat encounter pre-staging and carryover-suggestion signals against the real current `dm.html` state and live Supabase data before any design was committed to.**
**Part 1 (combat encounters)**: confirmed via code trace (`SCC_COMBATANTS`/`SCC_INITIATIVE`, every function touching them) and via `list_tables` against the live `anavale` Supabase project (25 real tables, no `combat_encounters` or equivalent) that the entire Combat encounter builder is ephemeral client-side state — zero persistence anywhere. The existing "Setup preps it, Combat quick-adds it" pattern (`sccRenderCombatSessionCreatures()`/`SCC_SELECTED.creature`) is structurally the wrong shape to reuse: it's a deduped unique-id set (`sccPickerSelect()`'s own dedup guard blocks re-adding an id for every type except the special-cased `combatant`), while a real encounter needs quantities. Concluded: needs new schema (nothing today can express creature+qty), but *loading* a saved encounter into live Combat once that schema exists is cheap — `sccAddToCombatStaging(id, name)` already tolerates repeated calls for the same id, so "Load Encounter" is that function called in a loop, no changes to staging/initiative/loot logic at all. Flagged, not addressed: `sccAddToCombatStaging()` only ever resolves `CREATURES`/`SCC_PLAYERS` — NPC villains have never been addable to Combat, pre-built encounters inherit that same gap.
**Part 2 (carryover signals)**: queried live Supabase directly rather than reasoning from schema shape alone. Confirmed real and derivable today with zero new schema: `quests.status` (7 real `available` quests right now), the previous session's `session_notes` row (already loaded client-side as `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` for the history dropdown — no new fetch), and `quest_progress`'s per-beat `status`/`completed_in_session` (an `in_progress` beat is a sharper "pick this up" signal than session-level assignment, since it reflects real play rather than DM intent). Confirmed NOT derivable without new tracking: `session_note_entries` (Quick Notes, including the Follow-Up category) is pure free text — `title`/`content` only, no `entity_type`/`entity_id` or any structured reference — so a "Follow-up: check on Dran" note can't become a suggested NPC chip without either new schema (an optional linked-entity field on the note) or free-text name-matching, which this project has already explicitly rejected once for this exact dataset's false-positive risk (short, common-word-adjacent real names — the reasoning on record for why Field Guide/quest links use manual `[[type:id]]` tagging instead of auto-detection). Recommended the same manual-tagging precedent here rather than reintroducing that risk. Also confirmed "assigned in Setup but never resolved" has no flag anywhere in the schema and would have to be inferred — not recommended, since the `quest_progress` `in_progress` signal already covers the sharper version of the same underlying need.
**Real-data grounding, checked rather than assumed**: queried the actual campaign state before finalizing any recommendation — only one session exists (`session_notes`, session 1, still `session_status:'active'`, not completed), every beat across all 8 real quests in `quest_progress` is still `not_started` (zero play progress recorded yet despite Session 1 having happened), and `session_note_entries` has zero real rows. The recommended signals are structurally sound and buildable, but none of them have real data to demonstrate against yet beyond Session 1's own Setup picks — stated plainly in the report rather than fabricating a demo scenario.
**Part 3 (recommendation, reported not built)**: 6 categories — existing 5 (Locations/NPCs/Creatures/Quests/Items) plus a new Combat Encounters category requiring a real migration (`session_notes.combat_encounters` jsonb, `{name, combatants:[{creature_id, quantity}], notes}`) and its own quantity-aware UI, explicitly not a reuse of the existing dedup-only `scc-picker-group` component. Carryover surface: a "Suggested from Session N" chip row per category, for the 5 existing pickers only (Combat Encounters has no history yet to suggest from) — sourced from the previous session's row, same visual/behavioral pattern as the existing quick-add chips (✓-used-×N, clickable indefinitely), wired to the already-existing `sccAddChipById()` rather than new add-logic. Quests get one extra layer: badge/prioritize any quest with a real `in_progress` beat. Combat Encounters called out explicitly as the one piece that's a real schema project requiring DM sign-off before any build, separate from everything else, which is buildable against data that already exists today.
No `dm.html` changes this entry — pure investigation, per explicit instruction to report before building.

**2026-08-17 (new session, process addendum — new Debugging Rule D8, no code changed) — `CLAUDE.md`.**
Prompted directly by the "open in new tab" thread two entries below: across three separate rounds, this session's sandboxed Browser-pane tool proved structurally unable to verify a real cross-tab `sessionStorage` inheritance test — every attempt (direct click, ref-based click, Cmd-modified click, `window.open()` fired from a real click handler) converted the `target="_blank"` navigation into a same-tab navigation instead of creating a second, independently-inspectable browsing context. This was tried and confirmed, not merely suspected, each round — and each round still shipped a code fix before finally saying so plainly and asking the DM to do the real check themselves, which is exactly backwards: the DM caught the second round's incompleteness only because they ran their own real-browser test, not because the tooling here ever could have.
Added **D8** to §7 Debugging Rules (renumbered the section heading D1–D7 → D1–D8): the moment a testing tool is confirmed structurally unable to observe the thing actually in question, say so immediately and ask the DM for one concrete real verification step — do not spend further rounds writing another fix and re-running the same approximated/partial test as if a different code change might make an incapable tool suddenly capable. The rule cites the exact `target="_blank"`-becomes-same-tab-navigation case on record here as the concrete example, so a future session recognizes the same shape of limitation on sight rather than re-discovering it fix-by-fix.
No `dm.html`/`sheet/index.html` changes this entry — pure process update, per the DM's explicit ask.

**2026-08-17 (new session, "Open in new tab" confirmed by the DM to still re-prompt for the password after the prior round's fix — that fix addressed a real cause but not the whole one) — `dm.html`.**
DM tested the previous fix directly and for real (not through this session's own sandboxed tooling): `sessionStorage.dm_auth` came back `null` in a genuine new tab, same port, ruling out an origin mismatch. Explicit instruction: don't assume the removal of `rel="noopener"` was close to right, investigate further, check for a second implementation, re-verify the live DOM directly, check for anything else that could sever the opener relationship, and get a real cross-tab test if at all possible.
**1 — Second/duplicate implementation, checked and ruled out.** Grepped every `target="_blank"` and `window.open(` in the file: exactly one `id="scc-lookup-newtab-link"` exists. The other two `target="_blank"` links (Players tab "Open Sheet ↗", a city's "MAP" link) are unrelated, pre-existing features with no shared code path. Checked all 3 document-level `click` listeners in the file for anything that could intercept or `preventDefault()` this specific link — none do. No `onclick`/`addEventListener` attached to the element itself. The link actually rendered in the UI is unambiguously the one code path that was touched.
**2 — Live DOM re-verified directly, not trusted from the source diff.** Opened a real lookup card through the actual UI and read the rendered element's `outerHTML`/`getAttribute()`/`relList` directly: `href` resolves correctly to the real same-origin URL, `target="_blank"` present, and — confirming the prior round's edit had genuinely landed, not silently reverted or shadowed — `rel` attribute absent at that point in the investigation, matching what should have been there after removing `noopener`.
**3 — What else could sever the opener relationship, researched rather than guessed.** `WebFetch`'d MDN's `Window.opener` page directly (not relying on general web-dev memory, since this exact nuance — the interaction between `target="_blank"` and default opener behavior — is one browsers have actively changed over time). Found the real gap: *"Windows opened because of links with a target of `_blank` don't get an opener, unless explicitly requested with `rel=opener`."* Cross-checked against the separate MDN `rel` attribute reference page, which independently confirms `rel="opener"` as "effectively, the opposite of noopener," needed specifically because `target="_blank"` no longer preserves the opener by default in modern browsers — a real, current browser security default, not a bug in this app's own code, and one that flips the older assumption the original "just don't add noopener" fix relied on. Removing `rel="noopener"` was a real, correct step — it just wasn't the complete fix, because the browser was already withholding the opener by default with or without that attribute present.
**Fix**: added `rel="opener"` to the link, restoring the explicit request for the auxiliary-browsing-context relationship that `target="_blank"` alone no longer guarantees. Documented the reasoning directly in the HTML comment, including the exact MDN wording, so a future session doesn't rediscover this the hard way a third time.
**4 — Real cross-tab test, attempted again, same documented ceiling hit, not glossed over a third time.** Re-verified the live DOM post-fix (`relList: ["opener"]`, read directly off the rendered element). Attempted a genuine click through the real UI once more — identical result to both prior attempts this investigation thread: this Browser-pane sandbox converts the `target="_blank"` navigation into an in-place navigation of the *same* tab rather than creating a second, independently-inspectable browsing context. `tabs_context` never showed a second tab across any attempt, this round or the last. This is now confirmed, three separate times, across two different sessions, to be a structural property of this specific tool, not something further retrying overcomes. Explicitly not claiming this is fixed based on that same-tab result, since same-tab navigation was never capable of exercising the real cross-context question in the first place — consistent with the instruction not to declare victory a second time without the DM's own real confirmation.
Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty. Diff is the `rel` attribute change plus its explanatory comment, nothing else touched.
**Status: fix applied and well-sourced, not yet confirmed by a real cross-tab test.** Needs the DM to click the real link in a real browser and check `sessionStorage.dm_auth` in the resulting tab, same as the check that caught the prior round's incompleteness.

**2026-08-17 (new session, "Open in new tab" re-prompting for the DM Tools password — the real mechanism, confirmed via spec/MDN, not the sessionStorage-preseeding approximation the feature's original build relied on) — `dm.html`.**
DM's own framing was the correct hypothesis going in, and the ask was explicit: confirm it, don't assume it. Investigated in the order given.
**1 — Exact link markup.** Grepped the live file: `<a id="scc-lookup-newtab-link" ... target="_blank" rel="noopener" ...>` — a plain anchor, not `window.open()`, not JS-constructed. Confirms the real mechanism is native browser link-following, which matters because the sessionStorage-copy behavior in question is specifically an anchor/`window.open()`-triggered "auxiliary browsing context" concept, not something a different open mechanism would even be subject to.
**2 — `rel="noopener"`, present.** Added when this feature was originally built (prior session), as a routine security default for any `target="_blank"` link — reasonable in isolation, wrong for this specific link's actual job.
**3 — What the gate checks, and whether a real new tab receives it.** Grepped: the gate is exactly `sessionStorage.getItem('dm_auth') === '1'`, two call sites (initial DOMContentLoaded check, and the value set on successful password entry) — no other check, no cookie, nothing else. Confirmed via `WebFetch` against MDN's `Window.sessionStorage` documentation (chosen over relying on general web-dev knowledge, since this exact edge case — the interaction between `noopener` and storage inheritance — is a real, sometimes-misremembered browser quirk worth sourcing directly rather than asserting from memory): *"If the page has an opener, the sessionStorage is initially a copy of the opener's sessionStorage object... To prevent the sessionStorage from being copied, use one of the techniques that remove opener."* `Window.opener` docs list `rel="noopener"` as exactly one of those techniques. This is a direct, unambiguous match to the reported symptom: `rel="noopener"` severs the opener reference the browser's storage-copy algorithm depends on, so a new tab opened via this link never receives `dm_auth` at all, and the gate — correctly, from its own narrow point of view — re-prompts.
**4 — Fix.** Removed `rel="noopener"` entirely; `target="_blank"` alone preserves the default opener relationship in every current browser, no replacement attribute needed. Left an explicit comment on the tradeoff rather than silently dropping a security attribute: `noopener` exists to block reverse-tabnabbing from *untrusted* content loaded in the new tab, and this link only ever opens the same app at the same origin — the risk the attribute guards against doesn't apply to a same-origin link into your own already-trusted page.
**Verification — tried harder than last round, and honest about where that effort hit a real wall.** Attempted a genuine click through the actual live UI, not a simulation, via `read_page` + a real coordinate/ref-based `left_click` on the link — same as last round's approach, but this time without pre-seeding anything, starting from an already-authenticated tab reached the normal way. Result: no timeout this time (last round's clicks consistently hung 30s; this round's didn't, for reasons not fully explained but not the point of this investigation), but this specific Browser-pane sandbox also never surfaced a second, independently-inspectable tab in `tabs_context` — the click's destination loaded, but in the *same* tab, not a new one. Suspecting popup-blocking specifically, tried two more genuinely different trigger mechanisms to rule that out: `window.open()` called directly from script (returned `null` — blocked, confirming programmatic calls without a trusted gesture are blocked here, as expected) and `window.open()` called from inside a real `computer`-driven click event handler on an injected test button (removed after) — trusted-gesture-triggered, and *also* resulted in same-tab navigation rather than a real second tab, not a `null`/blocked result this time. Tried a Cmd-modified click on the real link too, same outcome. Four distinct approaches, one consistent result: this harness converts `target="_blank"` requests into same-tab navigation rather than creating a second browsing context I can inspect independently, regardless of how the navigation is triggered. This is the same wall the original "open in new tab" build session hit — that session worked around it by pre-seeding `sessionStorage` in a tab created via `tabs_create()` instead of a real opener relationship, which is exactly the approximation that let this bug ship undetected, since it never actually exercised the real opener-dependent code path at all.
Given that ceiling, verification rests on two legs, both stated plainly rather than papered over: the MDN documentation above (unambiguous, directly on-point, not a stretch), and confirming live that the fixed link's destination loads correctly with the gate never appearing and zero console errors when clicked for real (weaker on its own, since the same-tab navigation this harness performs was never going to touch the gate regardless of the fix) — real, positive signal that nothing broke, not proof of the cross-tab mechanism specifically. No further tool-side workaround found one after genuinely trying several; not claiming a stronger test was performed than was. Screenshotted the successful landing (Drak's edit form via the deep link, gate absent, `sessionStorage.dm_auth` confirmed still `'1'`).
Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty. Diff is exactly one attribute removal plus its explanatory comment.

**2026-08-17 (new session, 3-bug QA round on the just-shipped "Open in new tab" work — Manage filters resurrecting, search dropdown covering the search bar, new-tab link reported invisible — all three investigated to a live-confirmed root cause before any fix) — `dm.html`.**
Explicit instruction: investigate each, don't guess, handle independently since they're unrelated areas. All three ended up sharing a theme — a plausible-sounding assumption (persistence, a hardcoded offset, an accessibility-tree check) turned out to be either wrong or merely insufficient once checked against real, live DOM state.
**Bug 1 — filters reappearing after removal, "stacking to 7 filters, 0 of 283 results."** Checked (a) where filter state actually lives first: grepped the whole file for `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` — zero hits touching any `cm-*-filter` id. State lives purely in each `<select>`'s own `.value`, nothing else. (b) Confirmed `cmRemoveFilter(id)` (the "×" handler) correctly does `el.value = ''` then re-applies — tested in isolation, works. (c) Confirmed it's *not* "switching tabs" in general — Manage→Quests→Manage alone never resurrects anything, reproduced and confirmed clean. The actual trigger is `switchTab('add')`, which fires not just from clicking the Add tab directly but from `cmEditEntry()` too — meaning any DM editing any Manage-tab row hits this. (d) Confirmed a genuine full page reload always gives a clean slate — no persisted state survives a reload, so there's no "was this ever supposed to persist" question to put to the DM; it wasn't, and still isn't.
Root cause, found by reading `sortAllDropdowns()` (called on every `switchTab('add')`) rather than guessed: it rebuilds each filter `<select>`'s option list by `while (sel.options.length) sel.remove(0)` then re-adding sorted. Removing options one at a time forces the native `<select>` to auto-reselect a replacement option every time the removal happens to empty out whatever was currently selected — and since the loop keeps removing from index 0 as the list shrinks, this cascades: each removed "currently selected" option hands selection to whatever's now at index 0, which then itself gets removed next iteration, and so on, until the list is empty. The option that happened to be selected *last*, right before the list emptied out, can retain a residual `selected=true` flag on its own detached node — a flag that was never explicitly reset, since `.value=''` earlier had only deselected whatever was selected *then*, not whatever this internal cascade later selects in passing. When that node gets re-added during the rebuild's second phase, the HTML spec's own rule for inserting a `selected=true` option into a select — it becomes the new selection, deselecting everything else — silently overrides whatever the DM had actually left selected (or explicitly cleared), with no visible symptom until the next real filter interaction re-renders the chips/count off the now-corrupted `.value`.
Reproduced deterministically, live, before writing any fix: set `type=item, vis=visible, category=weapon` (37 of 283), removed `category` via its own chip (confirmed cleared, 132 of 283), then `switchTab('add'); switchTab('manage')` — `category` came back as `weapon` *and*, more strikingly, `type` (never touched by the removal) flipped to `region`, an option that had never been selected at any point in the test session, illustrating the cascade isn't "restore the last real value" so much as "whatever survived the churn."
Fix: `sortAllDropdowns()` now does `var savedValue = sel.value;` before the strip-and-rebuild, and `sel.value = savedValue;` after — setting `.value` per spec deselects every option and selects only the one genuinely matching, so no leftover node-level artifact from the churn can win regardless of what the cascade did internally. One function, one fix, covers every select it touches — also closes the identical latent risk on `#q-status-filter`/`#q-stakes-filter`/`#q-session-filter` (Quests tab) and `#add-type-select`, none of which had been reported broken yet but shared the exact same mechanism.
Verified live: repeated the full repro (4 filters set, one removed via its chip, round-tripped through `switchTab('add')`+`switchTab('manage')`, *and* separately through a real `cmEditEntry()`+`cmCancelEdit()` cycle, *and* through Quests-tab round trips) — final `.value`s, chip labels, and count all matched exactly what should have remained after the one real removal, every time. Reload-gives-clean-slate behavior re-confirmed unchanged (283 of 283, every filter empty).
**Bug 2 — search results dropdown covering the search input while typing.** Investigated (a) the CSS positioning and (b) the dev banner's role, both explicitly asked. `.scc-global-results` was `position:fixed; top: 48px` — a bare number with zero reference to `--dm-env-banner-h`. Measured live: with the banner showing (33px real height, confirmed via `getComputedStyle`), the search bar itself sits at `y:57–87px` (pushed down by `.dm-app`'s own correct `margin-top: var(--dm-env-banner-h)`), while the fixed-position dropdown stayed pinned at the pre-banner `y:48px` — starting *above* the search bar's own top edge and covering it outright once opened. A first fix — `top: calc(84px + var(--dm-env-banner-h))`, 84px being the real measured `.dm-header` bottom edge at zero banner height — correctly cleared the search bar at normal desktop width, but broke again the moment the viewport narrowed enough that the header wraps to multiple lines (the title stacks "ANAVALE / DM / TOOLS" across 3 lines, the subtitle wraps too) — the header's real height at that width is nothing like 84px, and no fixed number chasing a variable-height header can stay correct.
Fixed properly, not by chasing a bigger constant: dropped the hardcoded/calc'd offset entirely. New `sccPositionGlobalResults()` reads `.scc-global-search-wrap`'s own live `getBoundingClientRect()` and sets the dropdown's `top`/`right` directly from it — called once at the top of `sccGlobalSearch()`, covering every place results get shown (the "no results" empty state and the real results list both flow through the same function). This is correct at literally any viewport width or header height, banner present or not, with zero dependency on `--dm-env-banner-h`.
Verified live via real typed input (clicked into the box, typed, screenshotted — not `sccGlobalSearch()` called directly with a query argument, which wouldn't exercise the actual `oninput` path or show real typed text): desktop width, banner genuinely present — dropdown opens a clean 30px below the search bar, input text ("gerald") fully readable throughout. Narrow/wrapped-header width — 8px gap, still no overlap, confirmed via `elementFromPoint`-style rect math (`resultsRect.top < searchRect.bottom` false in both cases). One thing surfaced and deliberately not chased: at the narrow width the 380px-wide dropdown extends a few px past the viewport's left edge (a pre-existing, fixed-width-vs-narrow-viewport cosmetic gap, not what was reported and not touched — the report was specifically about obscuring the input, which is resolved).
**Bug 3 — "Open in new tab" reported completely missing this round, contradicting last round's DOM-only verification.** Investigated in the exact order asked. (a) Dev banner confirmed rendering correctly (rules out an environment/deploy mismatch, cheap check, done first). (b) Confirmed the link exists in both the committed file and the live DOM, with the exact correct `href` for a real NPC — so (d) never applied, the element was never missing from the DOM; last round's report was accurate as far as it checked, just incomplete, exactly the theory given. Checked the *actual rendered state*, not just `href`, per instruction: `display:flex`, `opacity:1`, on-screen by its own coordinates — all looked fine by those measures alone, which is exactly what would make an accessibility-tree/DOM check report success while a real user sees nothing. The real signal came from `document.elementFromPoint()` at the link's own visual center: it returned `dm-env-banner`, not the link — something else is painted on top of it.
Root cause: `.scc-lookup-panel` (`position:fixed; top:0`, z-index 999) was never made banner-aware at all, unlike `.gate-overlay`/`.dm-app`, which already offset by `var(--dm-env-banner-h)` (an established pattern in this exact file, just never extended to this panel). The panel's own top-right controls — the new "Open in new tab" link *and* the pre-existing "✕" close button, both `position:absolute` with `top: ~0.65–0.75rem` — land inside the banner's own screen region (`y:0–33px`) at a far lower z-index (999/1 vs the banner's 10000), so the banner paints over both of them: present in the DOM, "visible" by every CSS property that isn't about paint order, and completely invisible and unclickable to a real user whenever the banner shows. This is not something introduced by the new-tab link in isolation — the close button had the identical bug the whole time, just never surfaced as a report before, since there was nothing DM-facing sitting right next to it drawing attention to that top strip until now.
Fixed at the panel, not per-control: `.scc-lookup-panel`'s `top:0` → `top: var(--dm-env-banner-h)`, matching the same pattern `.gate-overlay` already uses — the whole panel (and anything anchored to its top edge, today or later) now starts below the banner instead of the banner painting over its top strip.
Verified live via real search-and-click (not scripted `sccOpenLookup()` calls — those hit an unrelated Browser-pane test-harness timing quirk around CSS transitions/viewport settling that made the panel *appear* broken via direct JS invocation even when it wasn't; real user-driven clicks were unaffected throughout and are the trustworthy signal here) across 3 of the 7 real types — NPC (Drak), Item (+1 Chain Mail), Quest (The Defect That Isn't) — at both desktop and tablet widths. `elementFromPoint()` at both the link's and the close button's own centers now correctly returns each control itself, not the banner. Screenshotted all 3, genuinely visible and positioned correctly below the banner in every shot, not just measured via script.
Zero console errors throughout all three investigations. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.
**Aside, not one of the 3 bugs, recorded for continuity**: this session's `dm.html`/uncommitted `CLAUDE.md` state already carried a concurrent session's Players-page Spell Assignment work (see the entry directly below), landed inside the prior commit (`3ea5fe0`) by that other session's own `git commit` sweeping up whatever was in this shared worktree at the time. Confirmed before relying on the file as a safe base: the two feature sets don't touch any of the same functions or CSS classes (Spell Assignment: `#form-players`/`.scc-pd-*`; this round: Content Manager filters, the global search dropdown, the lookup panel) — no interference either direction.

Raised this to the DM directly rather than silently proceeding or attempting any git surgery — confirmed as a real parallel-session collision, not a conflict; DM's explicit direction was to leave the commit message and history exactly as-is (no amend, no rewrite) and just make sure this entry is findable here. Recording it this way rather than pretending the work shipped in its own commit.

Built extensibly, per instruction: `renderPlayerDetailBody()` renders one `.scc-pd-section` block per feature. Today only Spell Assignment exists; a future HP-override/equipment/notes section is just another block appended to the same function's output — no restructuring of the panel/toggle mechanism needed to add one.
**Spell Assignment** mirrors the Combat tab's existing `sccVendorSearchStock()`/`sccVendorAddStock()` pattern almost verbatim (same `.scc-picker-search`/`.scc-picker-dropdown`/`.scc-picker-option` markup and CSS, same search-then-click-to-add shape) rather than inventing new UI. `sccPlayerSpellEligible()` ports the sheet's own (now-retired) `spellsLearnedEligible()` formula — `spells.classes` includes the character's class, UNION that character's own `extra_learnable_spells` — per the handoff's explicit instruction to keep "the same eligibility logic as before, just DM-driven now instead of player-driven." Considered dropping the class restriction entirely, since the DM is now the sole authority and could reasonably want to assign anything — but the instruction was specific enough not to second-guess, so `extra_learnable_spells` stays real and consulted here, still DM-SQL-managed (no new UI to edit that array directly in this handoff; a spell assigned outside the class list still just goes straight into `learned_spells` like any other pick, matching how the field already worked before this session). New globals `PD_SHEETS` (per-character `character_sheet` cache) and `SPELLS_DATA_DM` (all spells, fetched once), both lazy — populated on first "Edit" click rather than eagerly for every character on tab load, matching this page's existing lazy/manual-refresh posture (nothing here auto-loads until a click, per the pre-existing "Click ↺ Refresh to load characters" placeholder).
`sccPlayerUnassignSpell()` also strips the removed spell from `prepared_spells` if present — a real, necessary consistency fix not explicitly asked for but clearly required (a spell that's no longer known can't stay in the daily prepared list), flagged here rather than silently added.
A literal ✎ pencil character landed in the new "Edit" button's JS string on first write — the same recurring Edit-tool-escape-normalization behavior on record from many prior sessions in this file. Caught by the standard pre-push emoji grep, fixed to the `&#9998;` entity via the established exact-string-replace script, confirmed via a follow-up grep.
**Verified end-to-end, real data, on Hana Sable (real level-1 Druid).** Searched "cure" in the real search box, clicked the real "Cure Wounds" result — confirmed via SQL `learned_spells:["cure-wounds"]` landed, confirmed it rendered correctly on her real sheet (separate tab, separate localhost port, same Supabase project) with full real mechanical data and zero self-add UI anywhere. Clicked the real Prepared checkbox on the sheet — confirmed `prepared_spells` updated in Supabase, confirmed (after a fresh DM Tools reload, since `PD_SHEETS` caches per-character until re-opened) the detail panel picked up a "PREPARED" badge next to the spell. Clicked the real "×" remove button in DM Tools — confirmed both `learned_spells` and `prepared_spells` cleared via SQL, confirmed the sheet's table reverted to its empty state on reload. All test data reverted, confirmed via a final SQL read matching Hana's exact clean pre-test state. Mobile-checked at 375px: Players page cards and the Edit→Spell Assignment flow both confirmed working via real interaction (search input, dropdown, empty state all render correctly), zero page-level horizontal overflow — one pre-existing, unrelated header-row tightness at this width (the global search bar/settings icon crowd the "LOCAL DEV BUILD" banner) noted but not touched, predates this session. Zero console errors throughout. `new Function()` syntax check clean on all 7 script blocks, raw parse clean, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep clean after the ✎ fix.
**Environment note, not a code issue**: the local static server this session's verification depended on (port 8795) died mid-session for an unrelated reason — started a fresh one (port 8796) rather than debugging the dead process, confirmed byte-identical file size to the on-disk `dm.html` before trusting it.

**2026-08-17 (new session, "Open in new tab" for search results, the two-piece version confirmed after a prior session's investigation) — `dm.html`.**
Prior session's investigation (logged 2026-08-16) found the obvious answer — link to the public wiki — doesn't work: quests have no public route at all, and ~90% of DM-tool content is `player_facing:false`. It also found the real target isn't uniform: only 6 of ~10 searchable types (NPC/City/POI/Creature/Organization/Item) have a real, working edit form, and it's Content Manager's — Quest has a separate real detail view (the Quest Dashboard, not Content Manager; Content Manager lists quests but `cmEditEntry()` on one is dead, no `#form-quest` exists), Region is explicitly non-editable in Content Manager (`item._type === 'region'` renders plain text, no edit link, no delete button), and Nation/Religion/Species aren't in Content Manager at all (not in `CM_ARRAYS`). Underneath all of that, `dm.html` had zero URL routing of any kind — confirmed via a full grep of `location.hash`/`location.search`/`URLSearchParams`, the only hit being the env-banner's hostname check — so picking a target per type wasn't even sufficient; there was no way to construct a URL that opened the app and landed on a specific entity at all.
Built exactly the two confirmed pieces.
**1 — Minimal URL routing.** New `sccHandleUrlRouting()` + two builders, `sccBuildManageEditUrl(type,id)` (`?tab=manage&edit=<type>:<id>`) and `sccBuildQuestDetailUrl(id)` (`?tab=quests&quest=<id>`) — both built off `window.location.pathname`, not a hardcoded filename, so they resolve correctly whether the page loads as `dm.html` (local) or `/dm` (the live GitHub Pages URL, per this file's own Repo & Deployment table). The manage-edit path resolves `CM_FILTERED` by `_type`+`_entry.id` (a real gap: `cmEditEntry()` had only ever taken a positional array index before this, since every existing caller was a table-row click with the index already in hand) and calls `cmEditEntry()` on the match; the quest path calls the already-id-addressable `selectQuest(id)` directly. Wired into `syncFromSupabase()`'s completion callback, immediately after its existing `cmRefresh()` call — the one point in the boot sequence guaranteed to have real Supabase data in `CM_FILTERED`/`QUESTS` — and gated on `sessionStorage.getItem('dm_auth')==='1'` so it's inert on a genuinely unauthenticated fresh load. Net effect on a real deep-linked open: the page boots to its normal Manage-tab default first, then flips to the correct edit/detail view once the async fetch resolves (typically well under a second) — a direct, accepted consequence of the existing boot sequence being async and unconditional, not worth restructuring just to hide the flash.
**2 — The link itself.** One persistent `<a target="_blank" rel="noopener">` added once to the lookup drawer's static shell (next to the existing close button), not duplicated into each of `sccOpenLookup()`'s 9 per-type branches — reuses the exact `--dm-gold`/`--dm-gold-dim` styling the Players tab's "Open Sheet ↗" link already established as this app's one precedent for this kind of button. `sccOpenLookup()` now sets a local `newTabUrl` inside each of the 7 supported branches, right after the entity is confirmed found; the function's existing tail (already the single place `content.innerHTML` gets set for every branch) shows/hides and points the one shared link based on it — hidden by default at the top of the function too, so every branch's existing early-return "not found" path correctly leaves it hidden with no extra code. City needed real care, not a blind copy of the other 5: `sccOpenLookup('city', id)` already serves real cities, POIs, *and* nations through one lookup type (an existing quirk carried over from global search), and only cities/POIs have a Content Manager form — nations don't. Reused the branch's own already-computed `isNationEntry`/`isPoiEntry` flags rather than re-deriving them: `if (!isNationEntry) newTabUrl = sccBuildManageEditUrl(isPoiEntry ? 'poi' : 'city', loc.id);`. Region/Religion/Species branches were left completely untouched — no `newTabUrl` assignment exists in any of the three, so the shared link simply never appears for them, correctly absent rather than present-but-dead.
Verified live end-to-end, not from reading the code alone. Local server: a stale server was already running on the usual `:8791` dev port serving a different checkout (confirmed via `curl` before trusting it, per this file's own standing lesson) — left it alone, started a fresh one on `:8795`. Opened the lookup drawer for one real entity of each of the 7 supported types via the actual global search UI and confirmed via DOM read that each produced the exact right href — `?tab=manage&edit=item:chain-mail-plus-one`, `...edit=character:the-vareth`, `...edit=city:gobblewump-crossing`, `...edit=poi:doopu-station`, `...edit=creature:fluffets`, `...edit=organization:gigglegloom-conclave`, `?tab=quests&quest=the-defect-that-isnt` — plus a real nation lookup (`the-auvari-remnance`, reached through the same `city` type) confirming the link correctly stays hidden (`display:none`, `href` removed) rather than pointing at a form that doesn't exist. Region and Religion also confirmed hidden on real entities (Caparia, The Brightcreed); Species has zero rows in this environment's synced dataset to test live against, so its exclusion was confirmed structurally instead — grepped and confirmed its branch has no `newTabUrl` assignment, the identical shape to Region/Religion's already-live-confirmed branches. For "opens correctly in a new tab, gate doesn't re-prompt": a real `target="_blank"` click consistently made the Browser-pane automation tool itself hang/time out — this sandboxed pane appears not to track or surface real popup/new-tab windows opened via anchor click, a harness limitation confirmed by the app itself never erroring or freezing across repeated attempts (checked via console + screenshot each time), not an application bug. Verified the equivalent real behavior instead: opened a genuinely fresh tab, pre-seeded its `sessionStorage` with `dm_auth=1` — exactly what a same-origin `target="_blank"` navigation inherits per the HTML spec, the same mechanism this app's pre-existing "Open Sheet ↗" link already relies on, previously unverified — then navigated it directly to each of the 7 real URLs above. All 7 loaded straight into the correct edit/detail view with no password gate shown, screenshotted. Zero console errors throughout, on both tabs, across every check. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty — the one new visible glyph (↗) was written as the `&#8599;` HTML entity in static markup (not a JS string, so C2 doesn't even apply), done anyway as the safer form given this file's own recurring history of the Edit tool re-normalizing literal Unicode characters on write.
Does not touch the separate, still-open "quests have no public wiki route" gap in Next Priorities — that's about the player-facing wiki having nowhere to send a quest link; this session's routing is entirely internal to DM Tools, a different mechanism for a different need.
