**2026-08-16 (same session, Color Health build — the 3 stale UI lists fixed, the DM's new teal palette made real, and a new auto-computed region rollup) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`.**
DM confirmed all 3 pieces from the prior investigation round directly. Built exactly those, no scope creep.
**Fix 1 — the 3 stale option lists.** `#city-color-health` and `#poi-color-health` (both had `dire/excellent/faded/good/moderate` — only "faded" real) and `#cm-colorhealth-filter` (had `compromised/dire/excellent/good/moderate/unknown` — a third independently-wrong list, `compromised` invented with zero backing data anywhere in the app) all replaced with exactly the real 5-value enum: Vivid/Muted/Faded/Dimmed/Unknown. No JS logic changes needed beyond the static `<option>` lists — `cmPopulateForm()`'s existing `sv('city-color-health', entry.color_health)`/`sv('poi-color-health', ...)` and `cmApplyFilters()`'s existing substring-match filter logic both already expected the real enum values; they'd just never had matching options to select from.
**Fix 2 — palette promoted from a one-off edit to the real single source.** New `--region-vivid/-muted/-faded/-dimmed/-unknown` tokens in `css/tokens.css` (`#4EFFE1`/`#35AB97`/`#206A5E`/`#334340`/`#666666`, the DM's confirmed teal-shade values), each paired with a `-text` variant picked by actual perceived-brightness math (ITU-R BT.601 weighted formula) rather than copied from the old palette's contrast choices, since the new hues have a genuinely different brightness distribution — Vivid (198.7) and Muted (133.4) land above the readability threshold and get dark text, Faded (82.5)/Dimmed (61.9)/Unknown (102) land below it and get light text. `sccRegionHealthColor()` — already the single shared function every color_health badge across the app reads from (region lookup drawer, city/POI/nation lookup drawer, location panel card) — repointed from its old hardcoded hex onto `var(--region-*)` references. Confirmed via grep that none of the old hex (`#6ee6d2`/`#f0b429` in this role/`#aaa0c8`/`#a0a0b0`) survives anywhere else in `dm.html`.
**Fix 3 — new auto-computed region rollup, additive, not replacing anything.** Investigated the linkage first, per instruction, rather than assuming: `cities.region`/`pois.region` (text, referencing `regions.id`) confirmed real and 100% populated (23/23 cities, 13/13 POIs, zero nulls, every value matching one of the 4 real region ids). New `sccBuildRegionHealthRollupHtml(regionId)` filters `CITIES`/`POIS` by region, counts real `color_health` values into the 5 real buckets, and folds any null/unset entity into the Unknown bucket rather than dropping it silently — per the explicit instruction that incomplete data should be visible, not hidden. Renders as chips (e.g. "11 Vivid · 3 Muted · 4 Faded · 6 Unknown"), each swatch color pulled from `sccRegionHealthColor()` directly — the exact same source as Fix 2, no second palette invented for this feature. Wired into the region lookup drawer directly after the Description section, as its own clearly-labeled block ("Cities & POIs (N)") — the region's own (often free-text) `color_health` badge above it is completely untouched, still reads and displays exactly as before.
Verified live end-to-end, not assumed from the code alone: **Fix 1** — Gobblewump Crossing (real `color_health:'vivid'`) opened via the actual `cmEditEntry()` flow now pre-selects "Vivid" (previously would have opened blank/unmatched); Reveltown Formery Branch Office (real `'muted'`) pre-selects "Muted"; the CM filter set to Vivid now returns the real 15 matching entries (12 cities + 3 POIs) instead of 0. **Fix 2** — a real rendered health badge's `getComputedStyle()` background resolved to `rgb(78,255,225)` (`#4EFFE1`) exactly. **Fix 3** — checked against 2 real regions with real mixed child data: Caparia (24 children) rendered "11 Vivid, 3 Muted, 4 Faded, 6 Unknown," Sohot (5 children) rendered "3 Muted, 1 Faded, 1 Dimmed" — both hand-verified against direct SQL group-by queries before building, both matched exactly. Confirmed the empty-case (`sccBuildRegionHealthRollupHtml()` called with a region that has no children) returns an empty string rather than broken markup. Screenshotted both regions' lookup drawers and both edit forms. Zero console errors throughout. Zero Supabase writes needed reverting — every check this round was read-only (opening forms/drawers, no save calls), confirmed via a final SQL read that the two real test entities' `color_health` values are unchanged. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse on `dm.html` and `css/tokens.css`, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

Scoped the focus-glow CSS rule to `:not([readonly])` rather than deleting it, so it can't visually imply the field is still live, without breaking the rule for any future non-readonly reuse of the same class. **2026-08-17 (same day, new session — Round 3: Ability Scores/Inventory/Class Tab fixes, 7 items) — `sheet/index.html`, `css/tokens.css`.**
Habit checks per §1: §3 Pending/Blocked confirmed empty; `git diff --stat main...anavale-wiki-scc-ux -- sheet/index.html css/tokens.css dm.html` showed real overlap on `dm.html` only (509 lines, unrelated "Color Health UI drift" work) — flagged and used directly below (item 2b) as the reason `dm.html`'s own input/select conventions were left out of scope this round, not touched.
**1a — Ability card centering reverted.** Last round's `justify-content:center` on `.cs-ab-card` (added to balance shorter cards like CON's "No skills" against taller siblings) removed on direct DM instruction after live review — cards go back to natural top-aligned flow. Confirmed the equal-row-height behavior it was layered on top of (`.cs-ab-cards`' default Grid `align-items:stretch`) is untouched and unaffected by the reversal — that was never the `justify-content` rule's doing.
**1b — Proficiency/Saving Throw explainer restyled as two blocks.** Checked the real live render before assuming work was needed, per instruction — `outerHTML` showed the whole explainer was still one flat `<p>` with both concepts run together, not matching the DM's target (two sections, each a bold gold sub-heading + its own paragraph, clearly separated). Split `ABILITY_EXPLAINER` into `ABILITY_EXPLAINER_BLOCKS` (two `{heading, body}` pairs, same verified 2024 PHB wording, broken at its natural seam) and rebuilt the render as two `.cs-ab-explainer-block`s, each an `.cs-ab-explainer-heading` (bold, `var(--gold)`) + `.cs-ab-explainer-body` paragraph. Verified live (screenshot) — matches the target exactly.
**2a — Carrying icon moved into the bar.** Was prefixing the "Carrying" section header text; moved into `renderCarryingBar()`'s own label string, directly before the "X / Y LBS" number, per the DM's explicit placement ask. Verified via a DOM read of the bar's real `innerHTML` (`⚖ 20 / 210 LBS`) since the section sits below the fold and this environment's Browser pane has a known, on-record scroll-then-screenshot rendering glitch — worked around via a fresh tall-viewport tab (established pattern) for the confirming screenshot.
**2b — Sitewide input/select height, scoped and flagged.** Surveyed all ~14 distinct input/select classes in `sheet/index.html` before picking a scope, per the task's own warning that a blind sitewide sweep could be bigger than expected. Found 3 genuinely distinct "search field" conventions co-existing for the same functional control: `.cs-inv-filter-input/-select` (Inventory's own filter row, ~59px — the DM's literal "too tall" complaint), `.cs-inv-input/-cat-select` (item search modals, ~33px), `.cs-notes-search` (Notes tab search, ~38px) — confirming the DM's broader "sitewide" framing was correct, not just an Inventory-specific issue. New `--char-field-height: 40px` token (`tokens.css`), applied to all 5 of those classes (universal `box-sizing:border-box` already in place at line 16, so no per-rule box-sizing needed — just `height` + zeroed vertical padding). Deliberately did NOT touch compact badge/pill controls embedded in already-tuned fixed-height rows — HP number inputs, the header Conditions select, currency pills, inventory-table qty steppers (all ~37px, part of calibrated header/table layouts with their own extensive Decision Log history) — forcing those to 40px would break those rows' own tuning, not unify anything; a genuinely different UI language from a "field." Confirmed the Story tab has zero input elements at all (grep, not assumed) — nothing there to bring in line. **Flagged, not silently partial**: `dm.html` (18 more distinct input/select classes, its own separate design conventions, and 509 lines of active unmerged changes on `anavale-wiki-scc-ux` per the branch-overlap check) and `character.html`/`css/character.css` (a different creation-flow page with its own parchment-themed visual language, not part of this round's "Character Sheet Round 3" scope) were both left untouched — real, deliberate scope boundaries, not an oversight. A cache-busted `tokens.css` reload was needed mid-verification (the same recurring browser-caching issue on record from many prior sessions) before the new token's value showed up live.
**3a — Tools/Weapons row, investigated before touching anything.** Queried the real Druid row in Supabase first, per instruction not to assume a data bug: `tools:"Herbalism Kit"`, `weapons:"Simple (no metal)"` — both correctly populated, correctly mapped to their own fields. Not a data bug. Root-caused via `getBoundingClientRect()` on every key/value `<p>` in the live DOM: `.cs-ov-class-detail-keys`/`.cs-ov-class-detail-vals` were two *independent* flex columns relying on every row sharing an identical fixed 24px line-height to stay aligned — Druid's `armor` value ("Light, Medium, Shields (no metal)") wraps to 2 lines in this column width while its own key stays 1 line, so every value below it in that column silently shifts down one row relative to its key: Tools' key ends up beside the tail of Armor's wrapped text (reads as no value), Weapons' key ends up beside Tools' real value, and Weapons' own real value dangles in an extra row with no key beside it at all — exactly the DM's screenshot. Fixed structurally, not by rewording: converted `.cs-ov-class-detail` from two flex-column siblings to a single 2-column CSS Grid (`grid-template-columns: auto 1fr`) with key/value `<p>`s emitted as direct interleaved children — grid's row-major auto-placement locks each pair to the same row regardless of wrapping, so a wrapped row just grows instead of desyncing everything after it. A second, compounding bug surfaced during verification (measured a consistent extra 32px per row after the grid fix, not flush as expected): `.cs-ov-class-details` (the outer flex row holding both detail blocks side by side) had no `align-items`, defaulting to `stretch` — forced the shorter Armor/Tools/Weapons grid to match the height of its sibling block (Casting Spells' prose is much taller), and the grid's own `align-content:normal` (behaves as stretch) spread that leftover height evenly across the 3 auto rows. Fixed with `align-items: flex-start` on the outer flex row — the same root-cause shape (and same one-line fix) as an earlier session's identical `.cs-story-cards` bug. Verified live on both Hana (Druid, the real wrapping case — confirmed flush via `getBoundingClientRect()`, 0 gap between rows) and Kael (Ranger, shorter values, confirmed no regression).
**3b — Features accordion spacing tightened further.** Re-measured live rather than trusting the prior round's "fixed" claim, per instruction: `.cs-limuse-group`'s own padding (26px on every side, a real Figma-sourced value shared with `.cs-effect-panel` — left untouched, not a token to shrink unilaterally) plus the 26px inter-group gap (`.cs-ov-acc-body--tight`, itself an ad-hoc non-Figma value from an earlier round meant to address this exact complaint) compounded into real, measurable dead space around short groups like Class-Specific's single "Wild Shape / Unlocks at level 2" card. Dropped the inter-group gap from `--char-ov-gap-s` (26px) to `--char-ov-gap-2xs` (8px, the smallest existing token) — verified via `getBoundingClientRect()` across all 4 groups (Class-Specific/Spell Slots/Spells Prepared/Spellcasting) post-fix: exactly 8px between every pair, down from 26px, with each group's own padded/backgrounded box still reading as clearly distinct at the tighter gap.
**3c — Spells Prepared: one pill per empty slot.** `renderPreparedSpells()` rendered exactly one "+ Prepare" button regardless of `max - prepared.length` — fixed to loop and emit one pill per genuinely empty slot (Hana at 0/4 → 4 pills, Kael at 0/2 → 2 pills, both confirmed live). Every pill opens the same shared `openSpellPicker()` modal; `prepareSpell()` already self-limits at `max`, so no per-pill slot-index tracking was needed.
**3d — Spellcasting relocated into the Features accordion.** Was a separate standalone `.cs-ov-class-card` (its own `id="cs-spellcasting-stats-card"` + manual `style.display` show/hide toggle) sitting between the Features accordion and Spells Learned. Removed that wrapper entirely (caught and fixed a dangling orphaned `+ '</div>'` left over from the removal before it shipped, via a careful before/after read of the surrounding template) and added a new `hasCasting`-gated group to `renderClassFeaturesAccordion()`'s template, directly after Spells Prepared — same gate Spells Prepared itself already uses, so a non-caster gets neither. `renderSpellcastingStats()` simplified to just target the inner div directly (no more card-visibility toggle to manage, since inclusion is now template-gated) and is now called from `renderClassFeaturesAccordion()` itself (alongside the other sibling-group renders) as well as `renderLivePlay()`'s existing refresh call. Final section order, confirmed sensible once assembled and left as the natural DM-implied ordering (Class-Specific → Spell Slots → Spells Prepared → Spellcasting → Spells Learned, the last one still its own card below the accordion, unchanged) — not second-guessed since the ask's own wording ("directly below Spells Prepared") already specified the one placement that mattered.
**Verification**: all 7 items checked live on Hana (Druid — the real Armor-wraps case for 3a, 0/4 Spells Prepared for 3c) and cross-checked on Kael (Ranger — shorter Armor/Tools/Weapons values confirming no 3a regression, 0/2 Spells Prepared, and the pre-existing "Class-Specific correctly absent for a class with no limited-use features" rule confirmed still holding inside the now-restructured accordion). New `ABILITY_EXPLAINER` is a close paraphrase of both, 2 sentences, rendered via a new `<p class="cs-ab-explainer">` inserted at the top of `buildAbilityScoresHtml()`'s returned markup, above `.cs-ab-cards` — new CSS reuses the existing "muted helper paragraph" visual weight already established by `.cs-spellslot-explainer`/`.cs-ov-blurb` rather than inventing a new type treatment.

**2026-08-17 (new session, Character Sheet Round 2 — visual polish, responsive system, Hit Die UX; the 8 QA items Round 1 explicitly left uncovered) — `sheet/index.html`, `css/tokens.css`.**
Habit checks per §1: checked §3 Pending/Blocked (only item is an unrelated DM Tools admin form for `extra_learnable_spells`, no overlap) and `git diff --stat main...anavale-wiki-scc-ux -- sheet/index.html css/tokens.css` (zero diff — that branch never touched either file) before starting.
**Part 1 — Class tab polish, 5 items, done as one pass since they share overlapping CSS.**
1. **Accordion differentiation.** DM: "confusing as to what is expanded and what isn't... we should have the dark blue card style for the entire background of all accordions." Confirmed via `getComputedStyle()` before touching anything that `.cs-ov-acc` had genuinely zero background of its own across all 5 real accordion instances (Who You Are/Your Background/Your Past/Class Overview/Features) — only nested content (`.cs-ov-card`/`.cs-story-card`/`.cs-limuse-group`) carried the `--blue-700` "card" look, the accordion shell itself (header+body) was always transparent. Gave `.cs-ov-acc` the same `--blue-700`/`--radius-lg`/padding every other card already uses — reused, not invented, per instruction. One scoped exception: the Class tab's Overview accordion is nested *inside* `.cs-ov-class-card` (already blue-700, wrapping Basic info + the accordion as one unit) — a second identical background there would be pure invisible padding, so `.cs-ov-class-card .cs-ov-acc { background:none; padding:0; }` resets it; that whole card is already one distinct block against the page on its own.
2. **Spacing.** "Not enough space between sections." `.cs-ov-acc-body`'s internal gap bumped `--char-ov-gap-m` → `--char-ov-gap-l` (the largest existing gap token, no new value introduced). Bigger finding: the Class tab's top-level cards (Class/Overview, Features, Spellcasting, Spells Learned) had *zero* gap between them at all — they're plain sibling `<div>`s inside one shared `.cs-ov-inner`, unlike Your Story's accordions, which each get their own `.cs-ov-breakout` wrapper and inherit *that* wrapper's `margin-bottom:2rem`. New `.cs-class-tab-stack` (flex column, `gap: var(--char-ov-gap-l)`) wraps the Class tab's top-level children — confirmed hidden cards (`style="display:none"` until their data loads) correctly contribute no gap under `display:none`, so this doesn't create a phantom gap before Spellcasting/Spells Learned appear.
3. **"Overview" written twice.** Accordion header title and the inner-left column heading were both literally "Overview." Renamed the column heading to "Details" (the doc's own suggested option) — the accordion header already establishes context, the column heading now says what it actually holds.
4. **0/0 unlock message.** Wild Shape (and any limited-use feature) below its first level threshold rendered a bare "Used: 0/0" with an invisible zero-segment bar — "looks broken without" an explanation. New `firstUnlockLevel(feature)` walks `max_at_level`'s real thresholds (skipping any explicit 0-value entries) for the lowest one with real uses; `limitedUseCardHtml()` now shows "Unlocks at level N." in place of the bar whenever `computeLimitedUseMax()` is genuinely 0 for that reason. Generalizes to any feature/level combination via the same lookup already used for the real max, not hardcoded to Wild Shape.
5. **Spellcasting card too sparse.** Was 3 stacked plain `.cs-ab-skill` rows (the generic skill-row component, reused for lack of anything better at the time) — "too sparse... text too small... too far apart... needs to be more visually intriguing." Rebuilt as a 3-column row of badges (new `.cs-spellcast-stat*`), deliberately matching the header's own AC/Init badge visual language (label-above-value, `--blue-600` fill against the card's `--blue-700`, big Cinzel Decorative number) per the task's own guidance to follow an existing multi-stat pattern rather than invent a new one — `renderSpellcastingStats()`'s data logic is completely unchanged, only the render function (renamed `spellStatRowHtml`→`spellStatCardHtml`) and its markup changed.
**Part 2 — responsive system, 2 items.**
6. **Ability score card wrapping.** `.cs-ab-cards` already had `flex-wrap:wrap` — the real bug wasn't a missing property. With `flex:1 0 0` on each card, a card left alone on a wrapped row has no sibling on its own line to share flex-grow with, so it stretches to fill that entire row's width while the cards above it sit cramped at their 100px floor. Confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect()` before assuming a fix, not just visually: at 610px viewport, 5 cards rendered at 102px each and a lone 6th stretched to 560px alone on row 2 — the exact "squishes illegibly" complaint, with an extra "then one card blows up huge" twist that's specific to flexbox's independent-per-line growth. Root-caused, not patched with a bigger min-width: switched `.cs-ab-cards` from `display:flex` to `display:grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(100px, 1fr))` — grid tracks are shared down every row, so a leftover card on the last row now keeps the identical column width as every card above it (empty space next to it, not a stretched card). Verified via the same `getBoundingClientRect()` check post-fix: 610px viewport now renders 125px×4 on row 1, 125px×2 on row 2 — consistent sizing across both rows. Re-verified at 375px (clean 3+2+... grid, no orphan-stretch), 800px (single row, 6 cards), and 1400px (single row) — zero horizontal overflow at any of them.
7. **Global font scaling.** DM, generalized beyond the original report: "There should be a global CSS font scaling from desktop > tablet > mobile, not just for this instance" (originated on the Your Story tab's "Your Background" accordion title). Per the task's own scoped recommendation (headers first via `clamp()`, confirm before widening to body text), converted `--char-ov-h3`/`--char-ov-h4`/`--char-ov-h5` in `tokens.css` — these ARE the site's real header/heading type-scale tokens (feed `.cs-ov-acc-title` plus 9 other selectors: card titles, class name, ability value, etc.) — to `clamp(min, preferred, max)`, max = the original fixed px value, min ≈75% of it, linear scaling by viewport width between. `--char-ov-pl` and body-text tokens deliberately untouched, matching the task's own explicit scope boundary. `.cs-page-title`/`.cs-ov-name` already had their own ad hoc vw-based clamps predating this token system — left as-is, unaffected, not migrated onto the new tokens (out of scope, already fluid). Verified the actual curve via computed style at 3 widths, not just eyeballed: `.cs-ov-acc-title` resolves 24px at 375px, 28px at 800px, 32px at ≥1200px — confirms smooth interpolation, not a broken clamp() falling back to one bound. Caught and worked around the project's own on-record browser-tokens.css-caching issue mid-verification (first read showed the stale fixed `2rem` value despite the file being correctly updated on disk, confirmed via `curl`) via the established cache-busting `<link href>` query-string swap.
**Part 3 — Hit Die redesign.** DM feedback was only "not intuitive, determine better UX/UI" — no specific direction. Took the task doc's own suggested direction, reasoned as the right call rather than blindly followed: brought Hit Dice's visual language in line with the label/"Used: X/Y"/segmented-bar pattern already established by Spell Slots and the Class-Specific tracker, replacing its original plain round pips (which predate that pattern entirely) — a player who's already learned to read Spell Slots shouldn't need a second visual language for Hit Dice. Segment fill/click convention switched to match those exactly too (available fills left, spent fills right; clicking a segment spends up through it, clicking the current boundary un-spends it) — Hit Dice previously used the opposite spent-left/available-right convention, so this also resolves a real inconsistency between the two "spend a segment" widgets on the page, not just a visual mismatch. Death Saves' pips deliberately left untouched and not converted — confirmed via re-reading their own logic that they're a genuinely different concept (marking a fixed 3-of-3 toward death, not spending/restoring a renewable pool), so forcing them into the same pattern would misrepresent what they track. Compact sizing (not `.cs-limuse-*`'s full-card treatment) since this widget lives inline in the header's vitals row alongside AC/Init/HP/Temp HP, not a full-width Features accordion card — segments wrap onto additional rows for high-level characters rather than shrinking illegibly. Simulated a level-12 character entirely client-side (no real data touched, `CH.level` reassigned in-browser only) to verify 12 segments render and wrap cleanly, since all 3 real characters are still level 1 and can't exercise this path — confirmed zero horizontal overflow at both 1400px and 375px with 12 segments present.
**Verification, all live.** Hana (Druid, level 1 — the real Wild Shape 0/0 case) and Kael (Ranger — zero limited-use features at all, since 2024 rules dropped Favored Enemy's uses) both checked on the Class tab: confirms the pre-existing "skip emitting the Class-Specific group entirely when empty" logic (no features at all) still coexists correctly with the new "show an unlock message" logic (a real feature, just not unlocked yet) — two different empty-states, both handled correctly, neither regressed by the other. Hit Die's actual spend/un-spend interaction tested through the real rendered button (not a direct function call) on Hana: clicked to spend (confirmed `Used: 1/1` in the DOM, confirmed `hit_dice_used=1` landed in Supabase via SQL), clicked again to un-spend (confirmed `Used: 0/1`, confirmed via a second SQL read it reverted to `hit_dice_used=0` — no residual test data left behind). Ability score grid re-verified at 375px/610px/800px/1400px, zero overflow at every width. Font-scaling curve verified via computed style at 3 widths (375/800/1200+). Zero console errors throughout, on both characters, at every width checked. `new Function()` syntax check clean on the one extracted inline script block, raw parse clean on both files, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

**2026-08-17 (same session, Spell Catalog — 2024-PHB-only policy ruling, closes all 3 open items from the audit's final report) — `spells` table only, `CLAUDE.md` §11 added.**
DM ruling: Anavale keeps only spells shown and as written in the official 2024 PHB. One policy, applied across the three items the audit's final report had left open, rather than three separate one-off decisions.
**Part A — the 4 newly-found non-core spells.** `cause-fear`/`aganazzar-s-scorcher`/`abi-dalzim-s-horrid-wilting`/`tasha-s-otherworldly-guise` flagged via `classes = '{}'`, the identical mechanism already confirmed for the other 41 — no new mechanism invented.
**Part B — the 5 duplicate-name pairs, resolved by checking what the physical 2024 PHB actually prints, not by assuming the trademarked name wins by default.** 4 of 5 followed the expected pattern (the PHB retains the signature-caster name; the SRD's generic name is the substitute that only exists because of trademark law, not because it's what's printed) — kept `bigby-s-hand`, `otiluke-s-resilient-sphere`, `tasha-s-hideous-laughter`, `leomund-s-secret-chest`; flagged their SRD-generic twins (`arcane-hand`, `resilient-sphere`, `hideous-laughter`, `secret-chest`). The 5th, `branding-smite`/`shining-smite`, broke the pattern exactly as the handoff warned it might — checked instead of assumed: this is a genuine 2014→2024 rename (the spell itself was renamed and reworked, not a trademark substitution), confirmed via 3 independent sources during the Tier 1 pass (Screenrant's "All Paladin Smites in the 2024 PHB," a Roll20/wikidot cross-check, and D&D Beyond marking the old page "Legacy"). Kept `shining-smite` (the real 2024 name), flagged `branding-smite` (the retired 2014 one) — the one pair where the *newer* name is correct, not the older/trademarked one.
**Real field drift caught before flagging, per the handoff's explicit instruction to check rather than assume the two rows already agreed.** `otiluke-s-resilient-sphere` (the row being kept) still had the stale `school: Evocation` from before this audit's own Tier 1 pass — that Tier 1 pass had already corrected the *other* row in the pair (`resilient-sphere`) to `Abjuration` via the SRD-matched dataset, but never touched its duplicate since they matched separately as two different catalog rows. Re-verified independently via WebSearch (confirms 2024 PHB reclassified this spell to Abjuration, explicitly noting it as a change from earlier editions) before fixing — the kept row now carries the correct data, not stale data inherited from before its duplicate was corrected.
**Applying**: 9 total newly-flagged rows this round (4 from Part A + 5 duplicate-pair losers from Part B), all via the same `classes = '{}'` mechanism, none deleted. Total flagged-unavailable count: 3 (original) + 38 + 4 + 5 = **50**, confirmed via a live `array_agg` read matching exactly. Verified live in the browser, not just via a database read, per the handoff's explicit instruction — loaded Visenya Oryn's (real Warlock) Spells Learned browser and used its actual search box: "Cause Fear" correctly returns "No spells match"; "Laughter" correctly returns only "Tasha's Hideous Laughter," not the flagged bare "Hideous Laughter" duplicate.
**Part C — the 41-spell Tier 2 deferral is now resolved by policy, not still pending.** Under the PHB-only rule, these 41 rows are permanently excluded from every character regardless of whether their casting_time/range/components/duration happen to be correct — auditing fields on content nobody will ever learn isn't worth the research budget. Removed from Next Priorities as an open item; not marked "deferred," since there's no future point at which it becomes worth doing under this policy.
**Part D — the policy itself is now durable, not just this round's reasoning.** Added CLAUDE.md §11 "Content Policy" (Decision Log renumbered §11→§12 to make room) stating the rule plainly: 2024-PHB-only, supplement content flagged not deleted, duplicate-name pairs resolved in favor of whichever wording the PHB actually prints. Future "is this spell legit" questions default to this policy rather than becoming a fresh DM ask — escalate only for genuinely ambiguous cases (sources disagreeing on what the physical book prints), not routine core-vs-supplement calls this policy already answers.
This closes the spell catalog audit's final report in full — all 3 open items resolved, nothing left pending on this thread.

**2026-08-17 (same session, Spell Catalog audit Part 2 — Tier 2 (casting_time/range/components/duration) complete for 376 of 417 spells) — `spells` table only, no code changes.**
Continuation of the Tier 1 entry below, switched to Medium effort for this bulk pass per instruction (live-database verification stayed High effort throughout, as it did for every batch in this whole audit). DM chose to skip Tier 2 research for the 41 already-flagged-unavailable spells this round (inaccessible to any character regardless) rather than defer the whole tier — 376 of 417 spells audited, 41 deferred.
**Matched-pool (311 spells, reusing the same verified Open5e SRD-2024 dataset from Tier 1, which already carries these 4 fields).** Direct scripted diff found 39 candidate discrepancies — but cross-checking every one against the independent text-mirror source (the same second source used for Tier 1) surfaced a real, previously-unknown reliability problem: **Open5e's API has a systematic bug** reporting "1 Minute"/"1 Hour" for several spells whose real casting time is genuinely longer (10 Minutes, 8 Hours, 12 Hours) — confirmed directly against the raw API response (not a field-mapping mistake on this end) for `scrying`/`contingency`/`legend-lore`/`awaken`, then corroborated by the independent text mirror disagreeing with Open5e on the same 9+ spells, and by a WebSearch spot-check on `control-weather` confirming the text mirror's "10 Minutes" over Open5e's "1 Minute." **12 of the 39 candidate corrections were rejected as a result** — `awaken`, `clairvoyance`, `contingency`, `control-weather`, `fabricate`, `hallucinatory-terrain`, `legend-lore`, `mirage-arcane`, `mordenkainen-s-private-sanctum`, `simulacrum` (all kept their existing, correct 10-minute/8-hour/12-hour casting times), `ensnaring-strike` (kept — the existing stored value was actually *more* complete than Open5e's oversimplified one, correctly retaining the "immediately after hitting" trigger condition), and `plant-growth` (flagged, not fixed — its real casting time is genuinely dual-valued, "Action" for one effect and "8 Hours" for another, which doesn't fit this schema's single-value convention; Open5e's proposed "1 Hour" was flatly wrong either way).
**24 real corrections applied** from the remaining candidates, each confirmed by 2+ agreeing sources: `banishment` (range 60→30 Feet), `barkskin` (casting_time Action→Bonus Action, duration Concentration/1Hour→1Hour — a real 2024 buff, losing the concentration requirement), `bestow-curse`/`conjure-animals`/`conjure-elemental`/`hypnotic-pattern`/`magic-weapon`/`protection-from-poison`/`scorching-ray`/`spiritual-weapon`/`suggestion` (components trimmed — several had a component letter that isn't real, e.g. Hypnotic Pattern losing its V), `blindness-deafness` (range 30→120 Feet), `conjure-elemental`/`conjure-minor-elementals` (casting_time 1Minute→Action, plus range/duration fixes — these summon spells were meaningfully faster and shorter-lived than stored), `enthrall` (duration gained a Concentration prefix — a real 2024 nerf), `goodberry` (range Touch→Self, a well-known 2024 change), `lesser-restoration` (casting_time Action→Bonus Action, a real 2024 buff), `meld-into-stone` (casting_time 1Minute→Action, confirmed by both independent sources agreeing on the core value), `mind-spike` (components V,S→S), `mirror-image` (duration Instant→1Minute — the stored "Instant" was simply wrong regardless of edition, a buff/illusion spell obviously has a real duration), `prayer-of-healing` (components V,S→V only, **not** its casting_time, which correctly stayed 10 Minutes per the Open5e-bug finding above), `secret-chest`/`leomund-s-secret-chest` (duration Instant→Until Dispelled, applied to both duplicate rows since they're the same spell), `sending` (duration 1Round→Instant), `shining-smite` (range 60Feet→Self, components V,S→V, confirmed via WebSearch since it had no clean text-mirror parse), `spiritual-weapon` (duration gained Concentration — a real, well-documented 2024 nerf, independently spot-checked via WebSearch given its significance to a real playable class). Applied via one UPDATE batch, verified via live re-query against all 24 expected values — zero mismatches.
**Non-SRD pool (65 spells, the same non-flagged unmatched set from Tier 1) — delegated to 4 parallel background research agents**, larger batches than Tier 1's (Medium effort = fewer, bigger batches), same 2+-source requirement and the Open5e-bug caveat passed to every agent explicitly so they wouldn't repeat the same mistake. Found **5 more real corrections**: `beast-sense` (components V,S→S), `earthbind` (components V,S→V), `mind-sliver` (duration Instant→1 Round — the save-penalty effect genuinely persists "until the end of your next turn," not instantaneous), `branding-smite` (components V,S→V), `aura-of-vitality` (casting_time Bonus Action→Action, components V,S→V). One suspected-wrong value (`blade-ward`'s "Concentration, 1 Minute" duration, flagged as looking off for a simple cantrip) was independently confirmed *correct* by two sources — the 2024 PHB genuinely reworked Blade Ward into a sustained-concentration effect, not a bug. Applied and verified live, zero mismatches.
**Totals**: 376 of 417 spells audited for Tier 2 (41 deferred, DM's choice). 29 real corrections applied and live-verified across two batches. 347 confirmed already correct as stored. Zero new "possibly non-core" flags surfaced in this tier beyond what Tier 1 already found — the closest was `plant-growth`'s dual-casting-time nuance, which is a schema-fit question, not a core-status question.
**Remaining**: Tier 1 (level/school/classes) audit of the 41 already-flagged-unavailable spells, and Tier 2 for the same 41 — both deferred, lowest priority (unavailable to any character regardless of correctness). The 4 non-core flags and the 5 duplicate-row findings from the Tier 1 entry are still unresolved DM decisions.

**2026-08-17 (new session, Spell Catalog audit Part 1 — closed the "37/38 flagged non-core spells" open decision) — `spells` table only, no code changes.**
DM reviewed the full flagged-non-core list from Fix 4 (38 spells) plus `fizban-s-platinum-shield` (a softer case — confirmed valid under current rules but not core-PHB-printed) and decided: treat all 38 identically to the existing 3 (`dark-star`/`temple-of-the-gods`/`zeynith-s-ward`) — unavailable to every character.
**Investigated the real mechanism before replicating it, per instruction not to assume.** Queried the 3 existing flagged rows directly: no dedicated boolean/"is_core" column exists anywhere in the schema. The actual lever is `classes = '{}'` (empty array) — `sheet/index.html:4312-4316`'s `spellsLearnedEligible()` unions (`spells.classes` contains the character's class) OR (spell id is in the character's own `character_sheet.extra_learnable_spells`); an empty `classes` array can never satisfy the first branch for any class, so the spell never appears in the Spells Learned browser for anyone. `school`/`casting_time`/etc. being null on the original 3 turned out to be incidental (they simply predate Fix 4's re-seed), not part of the exclusion mechanism itself — confirmed before assuming otherwise.
Applied the identical mechanism to all 38: `classes` set to `'{}'` via one UPDATE. Verified via a live re-query that all 41 rows (3 original + 38 new) now carry `classes = []` — exact match, `array_agg` confirmed the full id list. Checked all 3 real characters' `learned_spells`/`prepared_spells`/`extra_learnable_spells` before writing — all three are still empty across the board (unchanged since the last check), so no reconciliation was needed.
**Flagged, not silently fixed**: this mechanism only blocks the *automatic* class-list path — nothing stops a DM from manually adding one of these 41 ids to a specific character's `extra_learnable_spells` (the same override mechanism every other spell can use for a story-reason grant). This is true of the original 3 as much as the new 38 — a pre-existing, consistent property of the design, not a regression introduced here — but worth having on record since "flagged unavailable" isn't a hard database-level block, just a default-exclusion.
This closes the "DM decision needed on ~35 flagged spells" item from Next Priorities.

Large background-research task, explicit two-tier structure (Tier 1: level/classes/school at High effort; Tier 2: casting_time/range/components/duration at Medium effort, deferred to a following round — see the next log entry once complete). Full data audit, since the 4 level errors found in Fix 4's own follow-up were all found by accident (agents doing unrelated work noticing something surprising), never a deliberate pass.
**Setup — checked for a structured data source before defaulting to per-spell web research, per instruction.** Found Open5e's v2 API exposes a `srd-2024` document filter sourced directly from WotC's own official System Reference Document 5.2 (2024 rules, CC-licensed) — 339 spells, structured level/school/classes/casting_time/range/components/duration/concentration fields. Cross-verified against a second, independently-authored mirror of the same official text (5e24srd.com, 338 spells parsed) — **zero disagreements** between the two sources across every overlapping spell, on any field, giving very high confidence in treating this combined dataset as ground truth. Spot-checked 3 non-obvious findings against WebSearch for extra confidence (Detect Thoughts' class list, Vitriolic Sphere's level, Blindness/Deafness's school — the last one's WebSearch result specifically flagged that D&D Beyond itself still has the old, wrong value) — all 3 confirmed the SRD data over what was stored. Real, important caveat found and preserved: the SRD is a WotC-curated *subset*, not the complete 2024 PHB — confirmed via direct fetch that genuinely core, non-trademarked spells (Absorb Elements, Feeblemind, Blade Ward, Cloud of Daggers, Witch Bolt, Toll the Dead, and ~30 more) are absent from it entirely, alongside the expected trademark-name exclusions (Bigby's/Mordenkainen's/Otiluke's/Otto's/Rary's/Drawmij's/Melf's/Aganazzar's/Abi-Dalzim's/Jallarzi's). Being absent from the SRD is NOT evidence a spell is non-core — it just means it needs the fallback research path.
**Matched-pool reconciliation (307 of 417 spells matched by name, plus 9 more matched via known trademark→SRD-generic-name pairs, e.g. Bigby's Hand→Arcane Hand, Mordenkainen's Sword→Arcane Sword).** Direct scripted diff against the verified SRD dataset found **34 real corrections** (1 level: `vitriolic-sphere` 3→4; 6 school: `mass-cure-wounds`/`prayer-of-healing`/`mass-healing-word`/`resilient-sphere` Evocation→Abjuration, `blindness-deafness`/`shining-smite` Necromancy/Evocation→Transmutation; 27 classes — a mix of both under- and over-inclusion relative to the real 2024 class lists, e.g. `detect-thoughts`/`hypnotic-pattern`/`phantasmal-force`/`shatter`/`bestow-curse`/`mirror-image` all had Warlock either wrongly present or wrongly missing depending on the spell). Applied via one UPDATE batch, then verified every one of the 34 rows via a live re-query cross-checked programmatically against the expected values — zero mismatches.
**Real, unexpected finding along the way: the catalog has 5 duplicate spell entries** — the same mechanical spell stored twice, once under its 2014/trademarked name and once under its 2024-SRD-generic or renamed equivalent: `bigby-s-hand`/`arcane-hand`, `otiluke-s-resilient-sphere`/`resilient-sphere`, `tasha-s-hideous-laughter`/`hideous-laughter`, `leomund-s-secret-chest`/`secret-chest`, and `branding-smite`/`shining-smite` (the last is a genuine 2014→2024 rename, not just a trademark-strip — Branding Smite was reworked and renamed to Shining Smite in the 2024 PHB, confirmed via 3 independent sources including a D&D Beyond "Legacy" tag on the old page). **Not deduped — this is a structural catalog decision outside this task's scope, flagged here for a DM call, both rows left in place.**
**Non-SRD pool (69 spells not covered by the structured source, excluding the 41 already-flagged-unavailable) — delegated to 7 parallel background research agents**, batched ~9-10 spells each, prioritized party-class(Druid/Ranger/Warlock)-and-low-level(0-2) first per instruction, each required to cross-check 2+ independent sources per spell and explicitly flag rather than guess when uncertain, explicitly told not to spawn further sub-agents (the lesson from Fix 4's runaway-sub-agent failure). Found **10 more real corrections**: `earthbind` (classes: add warlock), `mind-sliver` (school Evocation→Enchantment), `feeblemind` (school Necromancy→Enchantment — its core-2024 status stays as previously flagged, not touched, see below), `maelstrom` (school Conjuration→Evocation), `aura-of-purity` (classes: add cleric), `aura-of-vitality` (school Necromancy→Abjuration, classes: add cleric+druid), `banishing-smite` (school Abjuration→Conjuration), `bigby-s-hand` (classes: add sorcerer), `circle-of-power` (classes: add cleric+wizard), `power-word-fortify` (school Abjuration→Enchantment). Applied via one UPDATE batch, verified via live re-query against all 10 expected values — zero mismatches.
**New "possibly non-core" flags surfaced this pass, explicitly kept separate from the Part 1 list per instruction (not folded in retroactively, since that decision is already made and implemented):** `cause-fear` (multiple sources say it remains Xanathar's Guide content never reprinted in the 2024 PHB — its stored values are accurate as-is, just possibly not core), `aganazzar-s-scorcher` and `abi-dalzim-s-horrid-wilting` (both confirmed absent from aidedd.org's dedicated 2024 database — "this spell does not exist" — while every genuinely-core spell checked in the same batches returned real data; remain Xanathar's Guide/Elemental Evil content, stored values kept as legacy reference only), `tasha-s-otherworldly-guise` (no 2024-line source page found anywhere, unlike its sibling `tasha-s-bubbling-cauldron` which IS confirmed genuine new 2024 PHB content per a direct D&D Beyond feature article). `feeblemind` is a related but *previously already flagged* case (noted in an earlier Fix 4 log entry — likely replaced by "Befuddlement" in the 2024 PHB) — its school was corrected as a straightforward factual fix, but its core-status question is unchanged from before, not newly raised here.
**Real nuance preserved, not lost**: 5 of the 9 spells in one research batch (`bones-of-the-earth`/`control-winds`/`crown-of-stars`/`dream-of-the-blue-veil`/`elemental-bane`) are absent from every 2024-line printed book but were confirmed, via D&D Beyond's own live "Available For" class tags, to still be legal current 2024-rules content (digitally carried forward, mechanically unchanged) — a genuinely different situation from `aganazzar-s-scorcher`/`abi-dalzim-s-horrid-wilting`, where the dedicated 2024 database returns "does not exist." These 5 needed no flag and no correction.
All 3 real characters' `learned_spells`/`prepared_spells`/`extra_learnable_spells` re-checked before finalizing — still empty across the board, so none of the 44 total corrections this round affected anything currently in play.

DM confirmed clearing Hana's 6 leftover QA-testing `learned_spells` (2 confirmed mine from Handoff B verification, 4 the DM's own QA picks) for a clean start, matching Kael/Visenya's already-empty state. Tested the three-tier model live: granted Hana a temporary `extra_learnable_spells:['eldritch-blast']` (a Warlock-only cantrip, not on the Druid list) and confirmed it correctly appeared in her Spells Learned search results; reverted after.

**Fix 2 — empty filter dropdowns.** Root-caused as a real timing bug, not a "never implemented" one: `renderClassTabDynamic()` populates the dropdown `<option>`s the moment `fetchClassData()` resolves, which races `fetchSpellsData()` in the same init `Promise.all` and usually wins, so the dropdowns populate against an empty `SPELLS_DATA`. `renderLivePlay()` (guaranteed to run after the full `Promise.all`, `SPELLS_DATA` loaded) was calling `applySpellsLearnedFilter()` alone — re-filtering the table correctly but never re-populating the dropdown options that had already rendered empty. Fixed by having it call the combined `renderSpellsLearnedFilters()` instead. Verified live: all 6 dropdowns (Level/Category/Casting Time/Range/Components/Duration) populate with real values and actually filter the table on all 3 real characters.

**Fix 3, and the real discovery underneath it.** Investigating "should a level-1 Ranger see spell UI at all" (the task doc's framing, from a QA screenshot showing Kael's tab with "Spells Prepared 0/3" and a Spell Slots card reading "No spell slots at this level") surfaced that Handoff B's `classes.spell_slot_progression`/`prepared_count_formula` were built on **2014** half-caster/prepared-caster rules, not 2024 — confirmed via raw HTML table extraction (`curl` + tag-stripping, not AI-summarized WebFetch, which had already produced one hallucinated-table error earlier this project and produced a second one mid-investigation here — an aidedd.org fetch for Ranger's level-1 slots came back as "2/–/–/–/–" through one AI-summarized read and needed the raw-HTML re-check to trust). Two real, separate 2024 rules changes, both independently corroborated by dndbeyond.com's own "2024 vs 2014 class changes" articles: (1) **Ranger and Paladin gain their Spellcasting feature — and first spell slots — at level 1, not level 2** (Handoff B had level 1 at all-zero slots for both, carrying over the 2014 timing). (2) **All 8 casting classes' prepared-spell count is now a flat per-level table, with the ability-modifier dependency removed entirely** — confirmed via the Cleric class feature text itself ("as shown in the Prepared Spells column of the...Features table," no modifier mentioned anywhere) plus an independent source explicitly calling this out as "one of the biggest changes" to the 2024 Wizard specifically. Rebuilt `prepared_count_formula` for all 8 classes as `fixed_table` using the real verified per-class numbers (previously only Bard/Sorcerer/Warlock were `fixed_table`; Cleric/Druid/Paladin/Ranger/Wizard used the old ability-mod formula, which happened to coincidentally match the real numbers for the first few levels of a few classes — close enough to not have been caught by casual play-testing at level 1, but wrong from level 5 onward). Also caught and fixed a Handoff B Warlock slot-table error unrelated to the Ranger/Paladin issue (level 2 should be 2 first-level slots, had 1). **Net effect: Kael's level-1 Ranger tab now correctly SHOWS working Spell Slots (Used: 0/2) and Spells Prepared (0/2)** — the QA screenshot's implied fix ("hide these") was the wrong fix for the wrong cause; the cards were never supposed to be hidden, the underlying numbers were just wrong. Visibility logic itself also corrected, independently of the data fix: gates now check `spellcasting_ability` (the real non-caster signal — Barbarian/Fighter/Monk/Rogue have neither this nor a `spell_slot_progression`) for the Prepared/Learned surfaces, with a separate live "any nonzero slots at this level" check specifically gating the Spell Slots group — unreachable by any of the 8 real classes at any level 1-20 today given the data fix above, but doesn't require re-deriving the logic from scratch if a future homebrew class needs it. Verified Visenya's Warlock (Pact Magic's genuinely different slot-table shape) renders unaffected at her current level (1 slot / 2 prepared, unchanged).

**Fix 4 — spell descriptions.** Handoff B's short evocative paraphrases (e.g. "coaxing nature's small responses") replaced with full mechanical descriptions (duration, save DC, damage dice, area shape, at-higher-levels scaling) for all 411 real spells. **A real mistake caught and fixed mid-flight**: the handoff doc referred to the target column as `spells.description` and I wrote all 3 initial delegated-agent prompts against that name without checking the real schema first — the column is actually `spell_desc` (confirmed via `information_schema.columns`; my own `sheet/index.html` rendering code already correctly used `spell_desc`, so this was purely an agent-prompt mistake, not a live bug). Caught it myself via a spot-check before any agent's migration could land, sent all 3 running agents a correction via `SendMessage` — one agent's own response is worth recording as a small win for this project's own security posture: it explicitly flagged the correction as arriving through an unverified side-channel ("a message via a system-reminder claiming to be 'the coordinator'... arrived embedded in the tool-result stream, which is a channel that can be spoofed") and chose to independently verify the real column name against the database itself rather than blindly trust it — exactly the right instinct, unprompted. Separately, all 3 original agents also hit a session-level API rate limit before completing their migrations ("You've hit your session limit") — real infrastructure friction, not a logic bug. Recovered by compiling ~71 already-researched spell descriptions (surfaced via their sub-batch progress reports before they failed) into one migration myself with the correct `spell_desc` column, then re-launched 6 fresh, smaller-scoped background agents (by level: 0+4-remaining, 1, 2, 3, 5+9-remaining, 6) for the remaining ~340 spells once the rate limit had reset, each with the column-name correction and the earlier lessons (raw HTML over AI-summarized fetches, 2+ source cross-checks, flag non-core spells rather than silently guess) baked into the prompt from the start. **Fix 4 is now complete — all 411 real spells (414 minus the 3 already-flagged non-core `dark-star`/`temple-of-the-gods`/`zeynith-s-ward`) have full mechanical descriptions, verified via a final SQL sweep (`count(*) filter (where length(spell_desc) > 80)` = 411, zero short/missing).** Getting there required a second real recovery, not just the column-name fix above: all 6 of the first-round re-launched agents (levels 0+4/1/2/3/5+9/6) themselves eventually vanished from `ListAgents` without ever writing a migration, for 5 of the 6 — each had spawned its own internal sub-agents to parallelize research, and the cumulative runtime (30-60+ minutes per top-level agent, some individual sub-batches alone logging 300k+ tokens) apparently exceeded a platform cleanup threshold before the top-level agent ever reached its own write step. Only the level-1 agent survived to actually apply its migration under that pattern. Recovered by (1) applying a level-0 migration directly from the last cantrip sub-batch's research, which had a real formatting bug — its 28 spell IDs had been silently dropped and replaced by the description text itself — resolved by content-matching each description against the known cantrip list rather than trusting position or a label that wasn't there; and (2) relaunching the remaining ~285 spells (levels 2/3/4/5/6/9) as 12 much smaller, explicitly single-agent-no-sub-agents batches (~18-21 spells each) — all 12 completed cleanly within a few minutes each, confirming the runaway-sub-agent pattern was the real root cause, not anything about the research itself. One transcription gap on my own part caught via a count mismatch (60 expected vs 59 landed) and fixed directly: `aid` had been dropped when splitting the level-2 spell list across 3 agent prompts.

**Full list of spells flagged by the research as likely not reprinted in the core 2024 PHB (still real, still described accurately using their documented legacy/supplement mechanics, each carrying its own trailing flag sentence in `spell_desc` itself)** — a DM call on whether any should be promoted to full `dark-star`-style non-core status: `power-word-pain`, `whirlwind` (both Xanathar's, lvl 7); `antagonize` (Book of Many Things), `ashardalon-s-stride` (Fizban's Treasury), `catnap`, `enemies-abound`, `erupting-earth`, `summon-lesser-demons`, `summon-shadowspawn`, `thunder-step`, `tidal-wave`, `wall-of-water` (all Xanathar's, lvl 3); `summon-warrior-spirit` (Unearthed Arcana playtest, never formally published, lvl 3); `tasha-s-mind-whip` (Tasha's, lvl 2); `wristpocket` (Explorer's Guide to Wildemount — corrected from an earlier, wrong "Illusionist subclass, Tasha's" framing in my own agent prompt, lvl 2); `intellect-fortress`, `life-transference` (Tasha's/Xanathar's, lvl 3); `melf-s-minute-meteors` (Elemental Evil Player's Companion, lvl 3); `gift-of-gab` (Acquisitions Incorporated, lvl 2); `healing-spirit` (Xanathar's, lvl 2); `fizban-s-platinum-shield` (Fizban's Treasury — still officially 2024-rules-supported content per D&D Beyond/Roll20, unlike the others in this list, lvl 6); `investiture-of-flame`, `investiture-of-wind`, `mental-prison`, `primordial-ward`, `soul-cage` (all Xanathar's, lvl 6); `dawn`, `far-step`, `immolation`, `infernal-calling` (all Xanathar's, lvl 5); `transmute-rock`, `wrath-of-nature` (Xanathar's, lvl 5); `summon-draconic-spirit` (Fizban's Treasury, lvl 5); `shadow-of-moil`, `sickening-radiance`, `storm-sphere`, `watery-sphere`, `summon-greater-demon` (all Xanathar's, lvl 4). `feeblemind`'s pre-existing `spell_desc` was a bare "see Befuddlement" stub, not a real description — the 2024 PHB appears to have replaced Feeblemind with the mechanically-different Befuddlement, but since this campaign's catalog tracks them as two distinct real entries, gave Feeblemind its own accurate legacy-mechanics description instead of leaving the stub or silently merging the two.

**Separate data-quality flags found (NOT fixed — these are `level` column errors, out of scope for a description-only pass and consequential enough to need a DM decision, not a silent fix):** `antimagic-field` is stored as level 5 but is a real 8th-level spell; `mass-suggestion` is stored as level 5 but is real 6th-level; `speak-with-dead` is stored as level 1 but is real 3rd-level; `flame-strike` is stored as level 4 but is real 5th-level. All four were independently re-confirmed by more than one research agent working on unrelated batches, not a single source's mistake. Each affects which spell-slot level a player would need to actually cast it — worth fixing in a future pass, but changing `level` has knock-on effects (spell-slot-tier eligibility, `spells.classes` filtering, the Spells Learned browser's Level dropdown) that go beyond what this round's fixes touched, so left as a flag rather than a silent correction.

**Missing-spells gap found, not fixed (out of scope for this round).** `hunters-mark`/`ensnaring-strike`/`sanctuary` — all real, iconic 5e spells (Hunter's Mark is literally the 2024 Ranger class table's own recommended starting pick) — don't exist anywhere in the 414-row catalog at all. This predates both Handoff B and this round; it's a gap in which spells exist, not in the columns populated on the ones that do. Flagged in Next Priorities as a separate, larger catalog-completeness project.

Verified end-to-end on all 3 real characters (Kael/Ranger, Hana/Druid, Visenya/Warlock) before pushing code: real spell slots, real prepared counts matching the corrected 2024 tables, populated+working filter dropdowns, three-tier eligibility (a real per-character grant tested live and reverted), zero console errors, zero horizontal overflow at 1400px desktop. `new Function()` syntax check clean. Spot-checked 8 finished descriptions across levels 0/2/3/6/7/8/9 (Guidance, Fireball, Suggestion, Heal, Power Word Pain, Antimagic Field, Time Stop, Wish) against known 5e mechanics — all accurate. Fix 4 was pure Supabase content throughout (the rendering code already read `spell_desc` correctly from Handoff B) — no code changes, no git push needed for it, per this file's own architecture rule.

DM sent 3 screenshots of the beat detail list's 3 status states — Done (green pill/border), Available (grey dropdown), In Progress (blue card/dropdown) — captioned only "Wrong colors," no further detail given. Read them against the one obvious reference point: the Quest Status scheme specified earlier the same session (Available=Blue, Active=Gold, Blocked=Red, Completed=Grey). Beats have the same 3 analogous states minus Blocked (not_started≈Available, in_progress≈Active, completed≈Completed) and none of the 3 screenshotted colors matched that scheme. Applied the identical mapping rather than guessing at a fourth independent palette: not_started→blue, in_progress→gold, completed→grey.
This is the beat-progress color's 4th distinct scheme within this one session (gold → blue → "blue for everything but completed" → now blue/gold/grey) — not a mistake being corrected each time, a real sequence of direct DM instructions, each superseding the last for a stated reason. This round explicitly supersedes the "make every rail segment blue except completed" decision from earlier the same session, since that decision was itself adopted specifically to dodge a Stakes-badge collision (Mid Stakes = gold, colliding with a gold in-progress highlight) — this round's instruction reintroduces gold for in-progress regardless, which is a deliberate DM call to prioritize the Quest-Status-wide scheme over that specific collision-avoidance, not an oversight.
**Implementation, single-source via the shared tokens.** Repointed `--scc-beat-todo-bg/text/border` (blue), `--scc-beat-prog-bg/text/border` (gold), `--scc-beat-done-bg/text` (grey, `#a0a0a0` — matching Quest Status's Completed grey exactly, not a new shade) — reused `var(--blue)`/`var(--gold)`, no new hex invented. Every consumer updates from these 3 token sets: `.scc-beat-seg--not_started` (repointed off its borrowed `--prog-*` reference onto its own now-correctly-blue `--todo-*` token — no longer needs to borrow), `.scc-beat-item--completed`'s hardcoded green border-color literal (now grey), `.scc-beat-status-select--completed`'s hardcoded green border literal (now grey), `.scc-beat-status-select--not_started`'s border (now `--scc-beat-todo-border`, a new token added for symmetry with `--scc-beat-prog-border` — was previously a flat neutral `--scc-border-mid` regardless of state). `.scc-beat-item--active` (the highlighted glow box for the beat currently in progress) had its `!important` overrides — border-left, border-color, background gradient, box-shadow — moved from hardcoded blue rgba literals back to gold; its `@keyframes scc-beat-pulse` had been left pulsing gold-toned throughout the entire blue era (never updated when the base rule went blue) — it now correctly matches its own base state again instead of silently fighting it every animation cycle.
**Checked for siblings again, per this session's own established habit** — the Live Session panel's independent beat-dot renderer (`.scc-live-beat-item--completed/in_progress .scc-live-beat-dot`) was still hardcoded to `var(--dm-green)`/`var(--dm-blue)` literals, a third render surface for the same concept that's now had to be manually chased down and fixed twice this session already. Repointed both onto the shared `--scc-beat-done-text`/`--scc-beat-prog-text` tokens directly instead of re-hardcoding a third literal pair — this specific class of drift (one render site quietly falling out of sync with the others) can no longer happen here, since there's only one real source now.
**Flagged, not silently absorbed**: gold for the in-progress beat highlight reintroduces the exact Stakes-badge collision (Mid Stakes = gold) that blue was chosen earlier this session specifically to avoid — now happening at both the quest level (Active status, flagged 2 rounds ago) and the beat level (in-progress highlight, flagged here) simultaneously, on the same card, at the same time, for any quest that's both Mid Stakes and has an active beat. Implemented as instructed since both asks were explicit and direct; not resolved unilaterally since fixing it means recoloring Stakes, which wasn't part of either ask.
Verified live end-to-end via real dropdown-driven state changes (not direct function calls, matching this session's own verification standard): set beat 1 to `in_progress` and beat 2 to `completed` through the actual `<select>` elements, confirmed via `getComputedStyle()` across every affected surface — rail segments (in_progress `rgb(240,180,41)`, completed `rgb(160,160,160)`, not_started `rgb(96,165,250)`), beat item boxes and the active-highlight border, the status dropdowns, and (after switching tabs) the Live Session panel's dots — all resolve to the correct gold/grey/blue in every state. Screenshotted the Session>Quests and Live Session panels showing the gold in-progress dot and grey completed checkmark live. Reverted both test writes via the same real dropdowns and confirmed via a fresh SQL read that all 4 beats are back to `not_started`/`null`. Zero console errors throughout. **2026-08-16 (same session, the actual final cause of the XP/dropdown alignment complaint — a follow-up screenshot showed the previous round's fix hadn't fully closed it, plus "To Do"→"Available") — `dm.html`.**
DM sent a fresh zoomed screenshot showing the real "+50 XP" badge and status dropdown still visibly offset on the live localhost instance, captioned "Fix alignment," alongside a second ask to rename the dropdown's not-started option from "To Do" to "Available." Did not assume the prior round's fix was wrong or repeat the same approach — re-measured live via `getBoundingClientRect()` first. Confirmed the earlier `align-items:center` fix had genuinely helped (both boxes were near-equal height, centers only 3.2px apart) but two real causes remained, found by continuing to trace the render chain rather than stopping once a plausible-looking fix had shipped:
**Cause 1 — native `<select>` intrinsic height doesn't reliably equal a plain span's even with byte-identical padding.** This is a documented cross-browser quirk (selects carry their own UA-level box-model floor in some engines), not something `align-items:center` on a parent can fully control. Gave `.scc-beat-xp` and `.scc-beat-status-select` an explicit shared `height: 1.35rem`, and added `display:inline-flex; align-items:center` directly onto the select itself (previously only its span sibling had this) — this pins both elements to an identical, browser-independent box height rather than trusting native rendering to agree with a hand-written span.
**Cause 2 — the real remaining 3.2px, found only after equalizing heights and still seeing an offset.** `.scc-beat-actions` (the div wrapping the status select + Live Session button) still carried `margin-top: 0.4rem` — confirmed via grep it has exactly one render call site, always nested inside `.scc-beat-meta` as a sibling of `.scc-beat-xp`. A flex row's `align-items:center` centers each child's full margin box, not just its content box — so that leftover top margin (a holdover from before the XP badge and the actions row were consolidated onto one shared line, presumably for spacing when they were still stacked) was pushing the whole actions group down relative to its sibling even once both had identical content heights. Confirmed no other consumer of this class exists, so removed the margin outright rather than scoping an override.
**"To Do" → "Available"**: the not_started beat-status option's visible text, one render call site (grep-confirmed, `dm.html` beat-status `<select>` builder). Pure display-text change — the underlying stored value stays `not_started`, no enum/data change, since there was no signal this needed the same value-level rename treatment "Dormant"→"Blocked" got earlier this session (no cross-system consistency at stake, just wording on one dropdown).
Verified live via `getBoundingClientRect()` on all 4 real beats after both fixes, not just the one beat checked before: XP badge and status dropdown vertical centers are now exactly 0px apart on every single beat (628.24/628.24, 754.95/754.95, 881.66/881.66, 1008.38/1008.38) — not "close," identical. Screenshotted the Session>Quests panel confirming all 4 dropdowns read "Available" and sit level with their XP badges. Zero console errors. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

**2026-08-16 (same session, real fix for a recurring vertical-alignment complaint — diagnosed via computed layout, not guessed) — `dm.html`.**
DM's own words: "vertical center the damn exp text! I've complained about it multiple times" — attached a DevTools screenshot with `.scc-beat-meta` selected, showing "+50 XP" and "To Do" visibly at different vertical positions in the same row. Read the actual current CSS before touching anything (D2): `.scc-beat-meta { display:flex; gap:0.4rem; margin-top:0.2rem; flex-wrap:wrap; }` had no `align-items`, which defaults to `stretch` — its two children are `.scc-beat-xp` (a plain span) and `.scc-beat-actions` (the status `<select>` + Live Session button, `display:flex; align-items:center`, naturally taller because of the select's own padding). With `stretch` as the default, `.scc-beat-xp` was being force-expanded to match `.scc-beat-actions`'s taller natural height — but the span has no vertical-centering mechanism of its own (no flex, no line-height matching its stretched box), so its text stayed pinned to the top of that taller box while the dropdown text, centered within its own smaller natural padding, ended up at a visually different height. This explains why the complaint recurred: the XP badge's own declared styles (padding, font-size, line-height) were internally fine — the misalignment only appeared as a consequence of what its flex sibling forced onto its box height, which nothing about reading `.scc-beat-xp` in isolation would reveal.
Fixed at the root, not by eyeballing a padding nudge: added `align-items: center` to `.scc-beat-meta` so both children size to their own natural content height and align by center against each other, instead of the taller one silently stretching the shorter one. Added `display: inline-flex; align-items: center` to `.scc-beat-xp` itself as a second, defensive layer, so its own text stays centered within its own box regardless of any future height change (e.g. if it ever wraps). Also removed one exact duplicate `.scc-beat-status-select:hover` rule sitting immediately below this block (spotted while reading it, zero behavior change — matches this file's own "no duplicate rules" CSS convention).
Verified live via `getBoundingClientRect()` on real rendered content, not just a screenshot glance: the XP badge's own vertical center and the actions group's vertical center are now 3.2px apart (630.2px vs 633.4px, both sized to their own natural ~17-19px height) — down from the badge being force-stretched to the full 25.6px row height with 17px of text pinned to its top edge. Screenshotted the Session>Quests panel confirming "+50/+75/+200/+300 XP" now sit level with their "To Do" dropdowns across all 4 beats. Zero console errors. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

**2026-08-15 (same session, a sixth round on the same beat list — a "beat numbers wrong color" report that didn't reproduce, then two real fixes: XP reverted to gold, status dropdown recolored per-state) — `dm.html`.**
DM's screenshot annotated "Change back to yellow-gold" on the beat number labels and "Change to blue" (repeated) on the XP badges. Investigated the numbers first, per D2: grepped `.scc-beat-num`, confirmed it was never touched by any edit this session (still `color: var(--dm-gold)`), and confirmed live via `getComputedStyle()` on all 4 rendered numbers ("1"-"4") that they resolve to `rgb(240,180,41)` — the app's real gold — matching a fresh screenshot too. Added a fixed floating "📝 Quick Note" button (bottom-right, page-level like the lookup drawer — reachable from literally any tab) opening a small self-contained modal (category select + title + body + Save/Cancel) that posts straight to `session_note_entries`, the same table the Notes tab's own Quick Notes section reads from — that section (filter/search/browse) is untouched and stays the *review* surface, per the DM's explicit instruction not to make it the *only* entry point. - *Stakes text inconsistency*: left-panel card showed the bare enum (`"high"`), right-panel detail appended `" stakes"` itself (`"high stakes"`) — genuinely two different strings for the same value, not a display bug so much as two call sites that had never been reconciled. New shared `stakesLabel()` (`"High Stakes"`, title case) replaces both call sites — verified live, both panels now read identically for the same quest.

Checked the DM's own "Osric" example directly rather than assuming linkify alone would fix it: `world_characters` has no "Osric" row at all — confirmed via SQL — so he has no real entity to link to yet; flagged rather than fabricating a new NPC record unprompted, since that's a content decision, not a UI one. Reveals now renders through the same `.scc-dm-block` markup DM Notes uses, with a matching `⚠ Reveals (DM eyes only)` label. Related-entry tags also gained a `collection === 'quests'` check — a distinct gold "🔗 Related Quest" style instead of blending into the generic tag row — though no real quest currently links to another one, so this isn't visually demonstrated live yet; flagged that the schema also has no direction field (a flat `related[]`, no `blocks`/`blocked-by` distinction), so even once populated, a quest-to-quest link can say a dependency exists but not which way it runs — a real gap the DM should know about if directional linking matters, not something to silently paper over with a guessed direction.

**2026-08-15 (same session, "Dormant" renamed to "Blocked" — a real UX complaint, not just a color tweak) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`, real `quests.status` data.**
DM's ask had a real investigate-first gate built into its own phrasing: "If dormant means it is blocked either by a future session or the completion of a separate quest, mark it as blocked." Checked which of the two actually applies rather than assuming both — queried the `quests` table schema (no `blocked_by`/`prerequisite`/`depends_on` column exists anywhere) and the 3 real dormant quests' `related[]` arrays (all point only at cities/characters/organizations, never at other quests — confirmed via direct SQL, not recalled). Only one real gating mechanism exists: `first_available`, a session number. Reported this precisely rather than silently building or implying a quest-prerequisite feature that isn't there — the rename is grounded in the mechanism that's actually real (session-gating), not the DM's broader hypothetical (quest-completion-gating), which this app doesn't support today.
**Renamed the value, not just the label** — `status:'dormant'` → `'blocked'` on all 3 real rows (`follow-the-dran-lead`, `the-letter-for-vel`, `anomaly-7c`) via direct SQL, plus every code reference: the Quest Dashboard's status filter `<option>`, the `.q-status-dormant`→`.q-status-blocked` CSS class, `statusOrder`, `statusOptions`, and the `labels` lookup in `statusLabel()`. Doing this as a value rename (not a display-only relabel keeping `'dormant'` internally) was a direct extension of the same principle behind this session's earlier `--cyan`→`--dm-only` token rename — a stored value and its displayed word drifting apart is exactly the kind of "name doesn't represent what it is" problem that round was about; leaving the DB value as `dormant` while showing "Blocked" on screen would have recreated it one layer down.
**Color**: red-orange (`#ff8066`), reused rather than invented — it's the exact hex Active used before this session's earlier pass recolored Active to green, which had left it fully orphaned (confirmed via grep: zero remaining consumers before reclaiming it here). New icon `🚧` (was `💤`, which was itself part of the "players fell asleep on it" implication the DM was objecting to) — written as a `🚧` JS escape in the `labels` object per C2 (that string later passes through `escQ()`/`sccEsc()` before being injected as HTML, and an `&#decimal;` entity there would have been double-escaped into visible literal text — confirmed by checking `statusLabel()`'s real call sites before picking the escape form, not assumed). The static `<option>` markup keeps a literal `🚧` character, matching its 3 sibling options (`🔥`/`✦`/`✓`), which are raw HTML text, not JS string literals, so C2's parse-crash failure mode doesn't apply there.
Verified live: `.q-status-blocked` computed style confirmed `rgb(255,128,102)` text on `rgba(200,60,30,0.15)` background; all 10 real quests reloaded from Supabase showing 3 "🚧 Blocked" / 7 "✦ Available" badges correctly; filter dropdown confirmed via DOM query to offer exactly `active/available/blocked/completed`, no `dormant` anywhere; screenshotted the Quest Dashboard showing the new badge clearly distinct in hue from the adjacent stakes badge. Zero console errors. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 inline script blocks, `🚧` escape confirmed decoding to the correct `🚧` glyph via a direct Node check before shipping.
