Confirmed by cross-referencing `the-letter-for-vel`'s own DM notes, on record from an earlier session, calling it a "future-session plant... do not develop this in Session 1." Making Completed plain grey created a real collision risk with Dormant's own already-grey/muted tone — resolved by keeping Dormant's existing cooler blue-grey tint (`rgba(120,120,140,...)`) distinct from Completed's new plain neutral grey (`rgba(160,160,160,...)` / `#a0a0a0`, no blue tint) — the two are close but distinguishable on close inspection, and each status already carries its own icon (🔥/✦/💤/✓) as the primary differentiator regardless, matching this session's established "structural distinction over hue-hunting" approach.
**Field Guide box colors — the biggest single change, a real data migration, not just new hex.** Renamed every semantic key to match its actual rendered color (previously `cyan`→rendered-magenta and `orange`→rendered-terracotta were exactly the kind of misnomer the DM was objecting to for `--cyan`) — this was a deliberate generalization of the DM's specific `--cyan` complaint to the same principle applied consistently, not scope creep: a DM editing a box via the dropdown was seeing a semantic label that didn't match what the box actually rendered as. Migrated all 48 real boxes in the one real Session 1 document via a single ordered jsonb update (`with ordinality`, order-preserving, verified `jsonb_array_length` unchanged at 140 blocks before/after): `cyan`→`magenta` (18 rows, DM Only), `orange`→`gold` (8 rows, Loot & Shop), `pink`→`yellow` (7 rows, Comedy Beats), `blue`→`grey` (9 rows, Lore — dropped entirely per the DM's "does not need a color, it is confusing," rendered plain/neutral now like the structural grey boxes). `purple` removed outright with no migration needed — confirmed via SQL it was already at 0 real usage, freed for the DM's stated reservation ("religion and deities," matching NPC Relationship's existing Deity=purple). `SCC_FG_BOX_COLORS`/`SCC_FG_BOX_COLOR_CLASS` simplified from 7 semantic keys with one confusing remap (`cyan`→CSS class `'yellow'`) down to 5 keys with a clean 1:1 identity mapping (`grey/green/magenta/gold/yellow`) — the block editor's color `<select>` now shows exactly what a box will render as, nothing indirect. Comedy's new yellow (`#facc15`) and Loot's new gold (`var(--gold)`, `#f0b429`) are deliberately different-enough hex to stay visually distinct from each other, confirmed via computed style live (`rgb(250,204,21)` vs `rgb(240,180,41)`). The loot/shop subtype-icon logic (drink/food/general/weapons/armor/magic, built 2 rounds ago) keyed off the literal string `'orange'` in 3 places — updated to `'gold'` alongside the rename, re-verified live that Mira Pont's real "AVAILABLE TO PURCHASE" box still shows its 🪙 coin icon correctly post-migration. The in-document legend paragraph ("How To Use This Guide," real DM-authored content, not app chrome) updated to match: "Green boxes = read aloud to players. Magenta boxes = DM only. Yellow boxes = comedy beats. Gold boxes = loot and shop." — the Blue/Lore line removed entirely since that color no longer exists.
**NPC Relationship.** Neutral swapped from blue to grey (`#9a9ab0`), Ally swapped from green to blue (`#60a5fa`) — Caution/Hostile/Deity untouched (gold/red/purple). Verified live via a real 5-click cycle through the actual badge (not a direct function call) on a real NPC (Dran) — confirmed Ally renders `rgb(96,165,250)`, cycled back to Neutral, confirmed via SQL the temporary `dran:'neutral'` write landed and then removed it via a scoped jsonb key-delete, leaving `npc_relationships` back to exactly `{"oro":"deity"}` — the real, intended QA-reference state from 3 rounds ago, untouched.
**`--cyan` renamed to `--dm-only` (plus `-dim`/`-border`), the DM's explicit, unambiguous instruction** — "do not use variable names that do not accurately represent what it is supposed to be." This token has now been cyan, then teal, then magenta, while its name stayed `--cyan` through all three — a name tied to a color's history instead of its meaning goes stale every time the color changes, exactly the failure the DM was pointing at. Renamed across every consumer: `tokens.css` (the definition itself, plus `--scc-dm-only-text: var(--cyan)`), and 15 usage sites across `dm.html` (NPC-card DM-only block, Field Guide DM-only box — now `.scc-fg-box--magenta`, Tagged Notes DM-Only badge, and 5 duplicate `player_facing` DM-Only badge ternaries across character/organization/region/religion/species Quick Look cards) — done via an exact global string replace (`--cyan-dim`→`--dm-only-dim`, `--cyan-border`→`--dm-only-border`, then bare `--cyan`→`--dm-only`, in that order to avoid partial-match corruption), confirmed via grep that zero `--cyan` references remain anywhere in either file.
**Duplicates resolved, matching the `sccConditionColor()` pattern from 2 rounds ago — the DM's "if there are duplicates resolve them" applied to two systems this touched anyway.** NPC relationship colors: the session-roster-card and lookup-drawer-card render paths each had their own identical hardcoded `REL_BG` object (confirmed via grep, genuinely byte-identical, drifted zero times only by luck) — both replaced with calls to one new shared `sccRelColor(rel)`. Region/location "color_health" badges: found a third copy beyond the two already known — the location panel's own `healthBadge()` helper, the city/POI/nation lookup-drawer branch, and the region lookup-drawer branch all had the identical 4-tier if/else color ladder — consolidated into one shared `sccRegionHealthColor(colorHealth)`, each call site's own quirks (the panel helper's `'UNKNOWN'` fallback label vs. the drawer branches' empty-string fallback) preserved exactly via a thin wrapper at each call site rather than baked into the shared function.
**Dead code removed, per the DM's explicit "if there is dead code remove it," not silently left as debt.** 20 unused `--scc-rel-*` tokens in tokens.css (defined, matched the pre-change colors, never actually consumed by anything — confirmed via grep before deleting, not assumed) — superseded by the new `sccRelColor()` JS function, which is now the single real source. 3 unused `--currency-copper/silver/gold-coin` tokens (confirmed zero consumers). The unused `SCC_REL_ICONS` object — confirmed via grep it was defined and never referenced anywhere, which also happened to be a latent, previously-unnoticed C2 violation (4 literal emoji circle characters directly in a JS object literal) that simply never crashed because the object was never actually reached at runtime; removed rather than fixed, since fixing dead code just to keep it dead isn't worth it. The fully-dead `.scc-flag`/`.scc-flag--vareth`/`.scc-flag--formery` CSS (flagged as dead in an earlier session's color audit but never actually removed until now) — re-confirmed via a fresh grep that the base class has zero HTML/JS consumers before deleting the whole rule set, not trusting the earlier flag from memory.
Verified live end-to-end, not just via computed-style spot checks: full Field Guide document reload showing 48 boxes distributed exactly as migrated (grey 10, magenta 18, green 5, gold 8, yellow 7 — matches the SQL migration counts precisely), the legend paragraph rendering the new color names, the block editor's color dropdown showing exactly the 5 new option values with no stale ones; Quest Dashboard screenshotted showing all 10 real quests with correct stakes/status badge colors (7 available/blue, 3 dormant/muted-grey, stakes green/gold/red matching each quest's real value) and the Stakes filter dropdown confirmed via DOM query to no longer have a Campaign option; NPC relationship cycle tested end-to-end through the real UI as described above. Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 7 real inline script blocks (of 17 total `<script>` tags — 10 are external `src=` includes, correctly excluded), diff-scoped literal-emoji grep caught one hit in my own new CSS comment (not a JS string, not a C2 violation, but fixed anyway to keep the check trivially clean) and came back empty after.

**2026-08-15 (same session, consolidation feasibility call + the 5 fixes it produced) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`.**
Ask had an explicit gate: investigate whether the color ledger's findings shared a fixable root cause before patching them one at a time, since this exact bug shape (one meaning, multiple independent hardcoded sources, silently drifting) had already caused repeat work across several rounds tonight — the DM-Only red/teal splits earlier this session were all instances of it.
**Step 1 — investigated, concluded consolidation is real and cheap for all 3 systems named.** None required a data-model change or touched anything risky; each already had (or could trivially get) one real shared source, and the fix was just repointing consumers onto it:
- **Conditions**: confirmed `COND_DATA` (Live Session panel) and `sccConditionsTableLookup` were both declared *inside* `sccLiveRenderConditions()` — function-local, not shared — while `COND_COLORS` (combat initiative tracker) was a fully separate hardcoded object with no fallback at all. The real fix already existed and was already fetched once at boot: `window.SCC_CONDITIONS_DATA`, populated from the real `conditions` table in `sccInit()`. Recommended: delete both local objects, add one small top-level function every surface calls.
- **Read Aloud**: only 3 real CSS declarations carry the color (Field Guide document box, Field Guide sidebar block, Quick Look card) — recommended one small set of shared CSS custom properties, the cheapest possible fix.
- **DM-Only**: a shared `--scc-dm-only-*` token set already exists and is already used by 6 call sites via `.scc-dm-block` — the 3 newly-found stale surfaces just needed repointing onto it, no new tokens required.
Recommended doing the consolidation rather than patching each instance, specifically because "patch the instance" is what had already happened three times this session and hadn't held.
**Step 2, item 1 — conditions.** New top-level `sccConditionColor(name)` (reads `window.SCC_CONDITIONS_DATA`, falls back to a generic purple only for a genuinely unrecognized name) replaces both `COND_DATA` and `COND_COLORS` outright — not layered on top of them. `sccLiveRenderConditions()` and `sccRenderInitiativeList()` both now call the one function. Verified live: staged a real creature, started combat, force-set its conditions to `['Frightened','Grappled','Muted']` (the 2 worst core-condition mismatches plus one of the 3 previously-uncovered custom conditions) — all three now render `rgb(248,113,113)`/`rgb(217,119,6)`/`rgb(196,201,212)`, exactly matching the real Supabase table, confirmed via `getComputedStyle()` and screenshot.
**Step 2, item 2 — Read Aloud.** New tokens `--scc-read-aloud-bg/border/text` (`rgba(134,239,172,0.07)` / `rgba(134,239,172,0.5)` / `#4ade80` — the Field Guide's existing values, picked as canonical since it's the more prominent of the 3 surfaces) added to tokens.css. `.scc-fg-box--green`, `.scc-fg-read-aloud`, and `.scc-ra-block`/`.scc-ra-label` (the actual bug — was gold) all repointed onto them; the tiny 0.06-vs-0.07/0.4-vs-0.5 opacity drift between the Field Guide's two own surfaces was also resolved as a side effect of true consolidation (both now literally the same token, not just close). Verified live: opened a real creature's Quick Look card with `read_aloud` content, confirmed `getComputedStyle()` reads `rgb(74,222,128)` (green) on both background and label, screenshotted.
**Step 2, item 3 — Manage tab hidden badge.** `.cm-vis-hidden` moved from `--dm-vis-hidden-bg/text` (still `rgba(200,60,30,...)` / `#ff8066`, the pre-recolor red — never touched by any earlier pass since Manage tab uses its own token namespace) to the shared `--scc-dm-only-bg/text`. Left the now-unused `--dm-vis-hidden-*` tokens defined in tokens.css rather than deleting them, matching this file's established practice for superseded-but-harmless tokens. Verified live: a real DM-Only quest row in the Manage list now shows `rgba(45,212,191,0.15)` background / `rgb(45,212,191)` text, screenshotted next to an unaffected green "Visible" badge for contrast.
**Step 2, item 4 — Field Guide sidebar DM Notes.** `.scc-fg-dm-block`/`.scc-fg-dm-label` moved off a hardcoded amber (`rgba(251,191,36,...)`) onto the shared tokens, and gained the same full-border-plus-left-accent shape `.scc-dm-block` already has elsewhere (was background-only, no real border). Verified live on a real NPC (The Vareth, has real `dm_notes`) via `sccFgToggleCard()`: background/border/label all confirmed `rgba(45,212,191,...)`/`rgb(45,212,191)`, screenshotted.
**Step 2, item 5 — Quest DM Notes, two colors depending on tab.** Root cause: 3 real render sites for a quest's `dm_notes` existed — Session > Quests panel card and the global search Quick Look card both already used the shared `.scc-dm-block` (correctly teal), but the Quest Dashboard's own detail view used a completely separate, never-migrated class, `.quest-dm-notes` (gold background/border, muted-grey body text, no border-left accent). Rather than repoint that class's tokens and leave 2 classes doing the same job, swapped the Quest Dashboard's markup to the literal same `.scc-dm-block`/`.scc-dm-label`/`.scc-dm-text` structure the other 2 sites use (matching their exact label text, `⚠ DM Notes`, and dropping the now-redundant separate `quest-section-label` heading, since `.scc-dm-block` already self-labels — the same pattern already used by every other `.scc-dm-block` call site) — then deleted the orphaned `.quest-dm-notes` CSS rule entirely rather than leaving a stale class sitting in the stylesheet that a future edit could mistake for still-live. Verified live: same quest ("The Missing Shipment") checked via `renderQuestDetail()` on the Quest Dashboard — confirmed `.quest-dm-notes` no longer exists anywhere in the DOM, `.scc-dm-block` renders with the correct teal background, screenshotted.
Zero console errors throughout. Net diff: 69 lines removed, 48 added — real consolidation, not a like-for-like patch. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 17 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty. No Supabase writes this round (every fix was pure CSS/JS rendering logic) — confirmed via a final SQL read that `session_notes.npc_relationships` is unchanged from the prior round.

**2026-08-15 (same session, building the 5 items confirmed from the investigation round below) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`.**
All 5 decisions were pre-confirmed by the DM (specific approach given for each), so this was a build session, not an investigate-then-report one. Order followed the ask; item 6 (relationship auto-save) called out as highest priority, built third but verified most rigorously.
**Item 3 — Field Guide DM-Only background hex.** `.scc-fg-box--yellow`'s `background` was `rgba(34,211,238,0.07)` — a hardcoded literal matching the *old* pre-recolor cyan (`#22d3ee`), never updated when `border-left`/label color were repointed to the new teal token weeks ago. Folded into the same edit as item 4 below since both touch this exact rule.
**Item 4 — DM-Only structural differentiator.** Confirmed last round: teal (H172°) and green (H142°) are only ~30° apart, and every other hue in the palette is already claimed by something else — approved going with a structural fix over a new hue, applied consistently across all 3 real DM-Only surfaces so none of them drift out of sync with each other again:
- **Field Guide box** (`.scc-fg-box--yellow`): background now `var(--cyan-dim)` (15%, correctly the teal token — fixes item 3 in the same stroke), a real full `border` added in addition to the existing `border-left` (was left-accent-only, the weaker pattern the original recommendation specifically called out), label font-size bumped 0.65rem→0.8rem, and a new `::before` pseudo-element renders a genuinely large (1.4rem) warning glyph as a corner badge — done as a separate CSS-only element rather than resizing the icon inside the label text, since that text is DM-authored Supabase data (`"💠 DM ONLY — ..."`) and can't be selectively resized from the app side.
- **NPC-card block** (`.scc-npc2-dm-block`, Dran's card and every other NPC's): background changed from a flat neutral `#0b0e16` to `var(--cyan-dim)` (was relying on its already-full border alone, no actual color fill); the ⚠ icon in the title — previously plain inline text at the same size as the rest of the line — wrapped in a new `.scc-npc2-dm-icon` span sized to 2.1rem, at both real render call sites (NPC branch and location branch). Drive-by fix while touching these exact two lines: one used a literal ⚠ character in the JS string (the other already used `&#9888;`) — normalized both to the entity, matching this file's own C2 convention, via the established Python exact-replace script rather than risking the Edit tool re-normalizing an escape on write.
- **Lookup-drawer block** (`.scc-dm-block`, shared by item/quest/creature/organization DM notes — 6 real call sites): bumped the shared `--scc-dm-only-bg` token from `rgba(45,212,191,0.06)` to `0.15`, matching the other two surfaces' new fill strength in one place; bumped `.scc-dm-label`'s font-size 0.62rem→0.75rem for the same "louder icon+label" effect used elsewhere, without needing to touch any of the 6 call sites individually.
Verified live via `getComputedStyle()` + screenshots on all 3 surfaces: Field Guide's real "EXACT RECOVERY SEQUENCE" box (bg `rgba(45,212,191,0.15)`, full border, `::before` content confirmed literally `"⚠"` at 22.4px), Dran's real NPC card (bg `rgba(45,212,191,0.15)`, icon span confirmed 33.6px vs. the title's own 1.4rem/22.4px base), and a real quest's DM Notes block via `sccOpenLookup()` (bg `rgba(45,212,191,0.15)`, label `"⚠ DM Notes"` at the new size). Screenshotted the Field Guide document showing clear visual separation between green Read-Aloud boxes (unchanged, left-accent-only) and the now-fuller-bordered, heavier-filled, corner-badged DM-Only boxes.
**Item 5 — Loot/shop icon discoverability.** Two changes, both scoped exactly to the confirmed ask (explicitly not a general editor search feature). (1) Renamed `#scc-fg-edit-toggle` from bare "Edit" to "Edit Document" (all 4 places its text gets set — initial markup, toggle-on, toggle-off, save-success — updated consistently), with a `title` tooltip pointing at the new per-box alternative. (2) New per-box "✎ Edit Box" link (`.scc-fg-box-edit-jump`) rendered on every box regardless of color, via a new `sccFgJumpToBlockEdit(idx)` — required threading a block's array index through `sccFgRenderBlock()`/`sccFgRenderDoc()` (previously only took the block object, not its position) and adding a stable `id="scc-fg-editor-row-{idx}"` to each editor row so the jump has something concrete to scroll to. The jump function: enters edit mode if not already in it, finds which of the 15 `h1`-grouped sections contains the target block via `sccFgGroupEditBlocks()`, force-expands that one group, re-renders, then scrolls to and applies a 2.5s gold-glow highlight (`.scc-fg-editor-row--jump-highlight`) to the exact row — and for orange boxes specifically, also locates and `.focus()`es the subtype `<select>` within that row (matched by its `onchange` attribute containing `'subtype'`, not by a fragile position assumption). Verified live end-to-end on Mira Pont's real "AVAILABLE TO PURCHASE" box: one real click on its new "Edit Box" link correctly entered edit mode, expanded the right section (confirmed via `document.activeElement` and the row's `id`), applied the highlight class, and left the real subtype `<select>` (`onchange="sccFgEditSetField(95,'subtype',...)"`) genuinely focused — confirmed via DOM state, not assumed from the code alone. Also screenshotted the read-view showing "Edit Document" in the toolbar and a real "✎ Edit Box" link visible on the grey "HOW TO USE THIS GUIDE" box.
**Item 6 — relationship badges auto-save (highest priority, real data-loss bug).** Root cause from last round confirmed and fixed: `sccCycleRelationship()`/`sccCycleRelationship2()` only ever mutated the in-memory `SCC_NPC_RELATIONSHIPS` object; the value only reached Supabase if the DM separately visited Setup and clicked "Save Setup." New `sccSaveNpcRelationships()` — same PATCH-if-existing/POST-if-new branch `sccSaveSetup()` already uses (`SCC_SESSION` can legitimately be `null` right after a session completes, confirmed via the code that resets it), silent on success matching `sccSaveTagNoteField()`'s established convention, a toast only on failure. **First version saved on every click directly and immediately exposed a real race condition in testing**, not a hypothetical one: 4 rapid clicks meant to cycle Oro to "deity" instead landed on "hostile" in the database — confirmed via SQL — because firing one independent network request per click let responses resolve out of order, and whichever happened to arrive *last* won regardless of which was *sent* last. Since rapid repeated clicking is exactly how this badge is meant to be used (cycling through 5 states to reach the one you want), this wasn't an edge case, it was the primary use case. Fixed by debouncing the actual save to 400ms after the last click via a single `SCC_REL_SAVE_TIMER`, so a rapid cycling burst produces exactly one network request carrying the final state, eliminating the race entirely rather than just making it less likely. Verified live, twice: first the broken version (4 rapid clicks → DB showed "hostile", confirmed via direct SQL, not assumed), then the fixed version (same 4 rapid clicks → DB showed "caution", the mathematically correct result starting from "hostile"'s prior test-polluted state) — then 2 more individually-spaced clicks to reach "deity" cleanly, confirmed via SQL immediately after and again after a full page reload in a fresh tab (Oro's badge rendered "DEITY" in purple `rgb(192,132,252)` on load, sourced from Supabase, not re-cycled). Per instruction, sanity-checked beyond just Oro: cycled Dran and Mira Pont's relationships too (both → "ally"), confirmed via SQL all three NPCs' values coexist correctly in the same `npc_relationships` JSON object without clobbering each other — then reverted Dran's and Mira Pont's test values via a scoped `jsonb - key` SQL update (kept Oro's "deity", since that's the real, intended end state this whole thread has been working toward, not test pollution).
**Item 7 — AC in the default combat row.** `sccAddToCombatStaging()` now also captures `ac: creature ? creature.ac : null` on the combatant object (mirroring the existing `cr` field's exact same conditional shape). `sccRenderStagingList()`'s collapsed row renders a new `<span class="scc-staging-ac">AC {n}</span>` right after the CR badge, omitted entirely when null — matching the CR badge's own established omit-when-absent pattern, no placeholder. Deeper stats (skills, saves, resistances, spells, etc.) deliberately left behind the "Stats" toggle, per explicit instruction. Verified live: staged Grusk Enforcer (real AC data) and Fluffets (no AC data) side by side — collapsed row read exactly "Grusk Enforcer · Creature · CR 0.5 · AC 11 · DEX +0" for the former, and correctly "Fluffets · Creature · DEX +0" (no AC badge at all) for the latter, screenshotted.
Zero console errors throughout, across every live check. Supabase confirmed clean afterward — final state is `session_notes.npc_relationships = {"oro":"deity"}` and nothing else, verified via a direct SQL read after all test data cleanup. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 17 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

**2026-08-15 (new session, 2 confirmed builds directly + 5 fresh investigations reported before fixing) — `dm.html`.**
Ask had two pre-confirmed decisions to build directly, and 5 items to re-investigate from scratch (explicit instruction not to trust either of the last two rounds' "verified correct" checks) and report before touching any code.
**Build 1 — Tagged Notes conceptual fix.** The DM's report ("I do not know what it is, what it is for, and how to use it") was upstream of the interaction-flow bugs fixed the last two rounds — nothing on the page ever explained why a second, different note-capture system exists directly below the 5 structured recap boxes (Player Decisions/Unexpected Moments/Loose Threads/Follow-Up/Milestone). Added a new `.scc-tagnote-compare` strip (gold left-border, matching the app's existing "important aside" visual language) between the notes-grid and the renamed section, with text that names both systems explicitly and contrasts them by *when* each is used, not just what each is: "the 5 fields above are for your end-of-session write-up — fill them out once, usually after the table wraps. Quick Notes below is for jotting something down the instant it happens, mid-session — tag it as you go, then filter or search to find it fast afterward." Renamed the section itself from "Tagged Notes" to "Quick Notes" (DM's "use judgment" option, taken — "Quick" signals the in-the-moment framing before the DM even reads the hint) — kept every internal id/class as `scc-tagnote-*` since renaming those would be pure churn with real regression risk for zero user-visible benefit. Rewrote the empty state: previously a single line ("No tagged notes yet — add one above") regardless of cause; now branches on *why* the list is empty — a real filtered-to-nothing state (notes exist, none match the current filter/search) still shows the plain "No notes match.", but a genuinely-zero-notes-ever state shows "Here's what a finished one looks like:" followed by a new `sccBuildExampleTagNoteCard()` — reuses the real card's exact markup for visual fidelity, but every field is a genuinely `disabled` input/textarea (not just styled to look inert), wrapped in a dashed-border, 72%-opacity card carrying a bold gold "EXAMPLE" flag badge, so it can't be mistaken for or accidentally edited as live data. Verified live: the comparison strip and renamed section both render correctly positioned; toggled `SCC_NOTE_ENTRIES`/`SCC_NOTE_FILTER_CAT` directly to reproduce both empty-state branches — confirmed the filtered-to-nothing case still shows the plain message with no example card, and the genuinely-empty case shows the labeled example with `disabled:true` on both its title and body confirmed via DOM read, not just visual glance. Screenshotted both. No Supabase writes in this build (pure client-side rendering change) — nothing to revert.
**Build 2 — Suggested Level input removed.** Per explicit DM confirmation, not a recommendation this time. Removed the `<input type="number" id="q-level-...">`, its label, its status span, the now-fully-dead `setQuestSuggestedLevel()` function (direct `fetch` PATCH to `quests.suggested_level`) in its entirety, and the now-dead `quest.suggested_level` gold badge in the meta row (kept showing a value from a field that can no longer be set would have been confusing, not backward-compatible). The read-only computed range + reasoning (`sccSuggestedLevelRange()`, built last round) is now the *only* Suggested Level display, rendered via a plain `<span>` — no id, no onchange, nothing to click. `quests.suggested_level` itself is left in the schema, untouched and unused (all 10 real quests already `null` there, confirmed last round) — matches this file's own established pattern of leaving a harmless deprecated column/token in place rather than migrating it out unprompted. Verified live: `quests-detail`'s rendered HTML for a real quest contains zero occurrences of `q-level-input` anywhere, and the visible line reads exactly "Suggested Level: lvl 1-2 (session 1, low stakes)" with no interactive element. Screenshotted.
**Investigation 1 — DM-Only color, re-tested fresh per explicit instruction not to trust memory of the last fix.** Checked Dran's actual NPC card and a real Field Guide DM-Only box *separately*, live, via `getComputedStyle()` plus screenshots for both — not assumed correct from either of the last two rounds' claims. Dran's card: genuinely, fully teal — border `rgb(45,212,191)`, title `rgb(45,212,191)`, all 36 internal "SECRET"/"NOTES" label elements `rgb(45,212,191)`. No bug. The Field Guide's own DM-Only boxes (`.scc-fg-box--yellow`, e.g. Beat 4's "EXACT RECOVERY SEQUENCE" box, block index 47 in the real Session 1 document): border-left and label text are correctly teal, **but the box's background fill is a real, confirmed bug** — `background: rgba(34,211,238,0.07)` is hardcoded to the *old*, pre-recolor cyan hex (`#22d3ee`) directly in the CSS rule (`dm.html` ~line 6194), never updated when `--cyan`/`--cyan-border` were repointed to the new teal (`#2dd4bf`) in an earlier round — only the border/label properties in that same rule were ever switched to reference the token; the background property was left as a hardcoded literal. Confirmed via direct grep of the CSS source, not just the computed style. This is a real, previously-undetected bug, reported here rather than fixed, per instruction — a one-line CSS fix (`background: var(--cyan-dim)` or equivalent) once confirmed.
**Investigation 2 — DM-Only vs Read-Aloud distinguishability, a real design problem independent of investigation 1.** Computed both hues from their real rendered colors: DM-Only teal `rgb(45,212,191)` ≈ H172°, Read-Aloud green `rgb(74,222,128)` ≈ H142° — only ~30° apart on the color wheel, both similarly saturated and similarly bright. That's genuinely close, not an exaggerated DM complaint — 30° is well within the range that's hard to distinguish at a fast glance, under imperfect table lighting, or for colorblind users (teal/green compression is a known issue for red-green colorblindness specifically). Checked whether a dramatically-further hue is actually available before recommending one: it isn't — blue is neutral-relationship, red is hostile/danger/errors (and was literally DM-Only's own pre-fix color, ruled out on both history and semantic grounds), purple is deity/high-stakes, pink is comedy, orange is loot, gold is the brand accent — every remaining hue family is already claimed by an existing meaning in this app, so picking a new one risks trading one collision for another. Recommended instead: keep the established teal hue, but make DM-Only stop depending on hue discrimination at all — a solid/heavier background fill (not the current subtle left-border-only accent) plus a significantly larger, bolder warning icon at the start of the label. Not built — reported per instruction, this is the single highest-stakes color in the app and shouldn't be repainted without confirmation.
**Investigation 3 — loot/shop subtype icon "not working," walked cold from a real click path, not direct function calls.** Reproduced a genuine DM-eye-view: navigated to the Field Guide panel, found the actual entry point (a small, plain "Edit" button sitting next to the session selector — confirmed via `document.body.innerText` that the string "block editor" appears nowhere in the real UI; that was my own descriptive shorthand in the prior report, not anything a DM would ever read on screen), clicked it via a real `.click()` call on the actual button element, and confirmed the resulting editor groups the document's 140 blocks into 15 collapsible sections with no search and no visual flag on which rows are orange/shop boxes — a DM would have to already know (or guess) which of 15 sections holds the box they want, expand it, and scan manually. The subtype dropdown itself, once found, has no visible label beyond its own default option text ("Icon: coin (default)") — nothing external to it explains what it's for. Confirmed the underlying feature genuinely works correctly (re-tested end-to-end via the same real click path used last round) — this is a pure discoverability gap, not a functional bug, exactly as suspected going in. Recommended: a visible one-line hint near the "Edit" button describing what editing enables (including shop icons), and/or a clearer label directly on the subtype select itself. Not built — reported per instruction.
**Investigation 4 — Oro's deity badge "still blue," found the real root cause, not a render bug.** First re-confirmed via direct SQL that `session_notes.npc_relationships` is still `{}` (empty) for the one real session — exactly as it was found last round, meaning nothing has ever actually been persisted as "deity" for any NPC, Oro included. Rather than stop at "the data isn't set" and guess why, traced the actual click path: `sccCycleRelationship()`/`sccCycleRelationship2()` (the badge's onclick handler) only mutate the in-memory `SCC_NPC_RELATIONSHIPS` object and call `sccRenderNpcPanel()` — there is no Supabase write anywhere in either function. The value only ever reaches the database if the DM separately navigates to the Setup tab and clicks "Save Setup" (confirmed `sccSaveSetup()`'s payload does include `npc_relationships: JSON.stringify(SCC_NPC_RELATIONSHIPS)` — so persistence is technically possible, just not connected to the badge-click interaction at all). Reproduced live end-to-end: added Oro to a session roster, clicked his relationship badge 4 times (neutral→ally→caution→hostile→deity), confirmed via DOM read the badge correctly rendered "DEITY" in purple (`rgb(192,132,252)`) at that moment — the render code itself has no bug. Then did a full page reload with no save in between, and confirmed via both `SCC_NPC_RELATIONSHIPS` (back to `{}`) and a fresh SQL read (`npc_relationships` still `{}` in the DB) that the change was completely lost. This is a real, structural inconsistency — nearly everything else in this app (loot assignment, tagged notes, quest status, HP) auto-saves on its own action; relationship cycling is the one interaction that silently doesn't, with no visual cue that it hasn't. Very likely exactly what happened to the DM. Not fixed — reported per instruction, recommending the badge click itself trigger a save (matching every other interaction's pattern) rather than requiring a trip to a different tab.
**Investigation 5 — Combat stats, AC "not showing," confirmed real but different from what was assumed.** Checked whether AC is actually missing from the Stats panel's own output for a creature with real data: it isn't — live-clicked the real "Stats" toggle button (not a direct function call) on a staged Grusk Enforcer and confirmed the panel correctly renders "AC 11 (leather armor)" among the other populated fields. No data or rendering bug inside the panel. The real gap is one level up: the *collapsed* staging row (what's visible before any click, i.e. what a DM glances at mid-combat) shows only Name/Type/CR/DEX-mod — confirmed via a direct DOM read of the row's `textContent` with the panel still closed — no AC, no HP, nothing else, and the "Stats" toggle needed to see any of it sits in a small row of icon-only-adjacent buttons easy to miss under real-table pressure. Recommending core stats — AC at minimum, per the DM's own explicit callout — render in the always-visible collapsed row instead of requiring a click, given the stated real-table use case ("otherwise I have no idea what to do with them"). Not built — reported per instruction.
Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 17 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty. No Supabase writes needed cleanup — both investigation and build work this round were either read-only checks or pure client-side state (SCC_NOTE_ENTRIES, SCC_NPC_RELATIONSHIPS, SCC_COMBATANTS), confirmed via a final fresh-reload SQL check that nothing test-related landed in the database.

**2026-08-15 (same session, building the 5 items approved from the investigation round below) — `dm.html`.**
Ask named exactly 5 approved items from the prior report — 4 real builds plus one log-only reference. All 4 builds verified live via real interaction, screenshotted, then any test data reverted.
**1 — Loot/shop subtype icons.** Added `SCC_FG_LOOT_SUBTYPES` (drink/food/general/weapons/armor/magic, each with a label + one HTML-entity icon: 🍺 `&#127866;`, 🍽 `&#127869;`, 🛎 `&#128718;`, ⚔ `&#9876;`, 🛡 `&#128737;`, 🔮 `&#128302;` — codepoints double-checked via `python3 -c "ord(ch)"` before writing, not eyeballed). Block editor's box row (`sccFgRenderEditRow`) gains a second `<select>` immediately after the existing color select, shown only when the box's color is `orange` — reuses `sccFgEditSetField(idx,'subtype',value)`, the same generic setter every other field already uses, no new plumbing. `sccFgRenderBlock()`'s box case now looks up the block's `subtype` in `SCC_FG_LOOT_SUBTYPES` and uses that icon; anything without a recognized subtype (every one of the real, already-migrated orange boxes, which predate this field entirely) falls back to the original universal coin exactly as before — a deliberately backward-compatible default, not a behavior change for existing content. Verified live: opened the real editor, found all 8 real orange boxes, set Mira Pont's "AVAILABLE TO PURCHASE" box (session 1, block index 95) to General Store via the actual dropdown, called the real `sccFgSaveDocument()`, confirmed via direct SQL the row now carries `"subtype":"general"`, then confirmed the *read* view (not just the editor) renders `🛎 AVAILABLE TO PURCHASE` — screenshotted. Reverted via a scoped `jsonb_set(...) - 'subtype'` SQL call afterward (test pick, not a real DM categorization decision), confirmed block 95 back to its pre-test shape and `jsonb_array_length(blocks)` still 140.
**2 — Roll Initiative copy.** `#scc-start-combat-btn`'s label changed from "Start Combat — Roll Initiative" to "Start Combat — Sort by Initiative", plus a new `title` attribute: "Uses any initiative you've already typed as-is; rolls a d20+DEX for anyone left blank, then sorts the full list." `sccStartCombat()` itself untouched — this was a copy-only fix confirming what the investigation already found, not a behavior change. Verified via DOM read of the live button's `.textContent`/`.title`.
**3 — Per-creature combat stat-blocks.** New "Stats" toggle button on every non-PC staging row (`sccToggleStatBlock(idx)`), same show/hide pattern as the existing "Loot" toggle (`.scc-statblock-panel.open`, mirroring `.scc-loot-panel.open`). `sccBuildCreatureStatBlockHtml(creature)` (plus helpers `sccStatRow`, `sccStatBlockAbilitiesHtml`, `sccStatBlockEntryListHtml`, `sccStatBlockAttacksHtml`) builds the panel from whatever the full `CREATURES` record has — AC/HP/Speed/CR+XP/saves/skills/resistances/immunities/condition immunities/senses/languages as label:value rows (each individually omitted when null/empty, not shown as blank), ability scores as a row of tiles (only rendered at all if at least one ability score is set), and Attacks/Traits/Actions/Reactions/Legendary Actions as named entry lists (each section omitted entirely when its array is empty or absent) — when literally nothing on a creature is populated, the whole panel is one honest line: "No stat-block data recorded for this creature yet." No estimated/invented values anywhere in this path. Verified live: staged Grusk Enforcer (one of the 4 real fully-statted creatures) and Fluffets (a lore-only creature, confirmed via `CREATURES.find(c => c.ac == null)`) side by side, toggled both panels open, screenshotted — Grusk Enforcer shows a complete block (ability tiles, AC/HP/Speed/CR/Skills/Senses/Languages, both attacks with damage dice and to-hit, Pack Tactics trait, Multiattack action); Fluffets shows only the empty-state line, nothing fabricated. Combat staging is pure client-side state (confirmed in a prior session, unchanged) — cleared after testing, no DB cleanup needed.
**4 — Tagged Notes fixes.** (a) `sccAddTagNote()` now resets both `SCC_NOTE_FILTER_CAT` and `SCC_NOTE_FILTER_Q` (and clears the search input's DOM value) as part of creating a note — chose reset-to-All over auto-switching the filter to the new note's category, since auto-switching would itself hide whatever notes the DM was already filtered to and looking at; resetting guarantees visibility while only ever *adding* back what was hidden, never hiding something new. (b) Rewrote the create call to POST directly with `Prefer: return=representation` (bypassing the shared `sccPost` helper's `return=minimal`, which would leave the new row's real id unknown) so the response hands back the created row's id immediately; `sccRenderTaggedNotes()` was changed to `return` its fetch promise (previously fire-and-forget) so `sccAddTagNote()` can chain onto the re-render finishing, find the matching `.scc-tagnote-card[data-note-id]` by direct DOM loop (not a CSS attribute-selector string, to sidestep any id-escaping concerns), scroll it into view, and focus its title input — then shows `showDMToast('Note added ✓', 'success')`. Verified live against the exact reported failure mode, not a synthetic case: set the active filter to "Player Actions" (0 notes visible), set the new-note category dropdown to "DM-Only" (deliberately mismatched from the active filter), clicked the real "+ New Note" button — confirmed via DOM read immediately after that `SCC_NOTE_FILTER_CAT` is now `''`, `document.activeElement` is the new note's own `.scc-tagnote-title` input, and the toast fired with the right text; then reproduced the same sequence in a fresh tab for a real before/after screenshot pair (before: "Player Actions (0)" active, "No notes match"; after: filter auto-reset to "All (2)," new note visible at the top with a focused, blinking-cursor-ready title field). Both test notes (one from the DOM-check pass, one from the screenshot pass) deleted afterward via a SQL delete scoped to blank title+content, confirmed nothing else in `session_note_entries` matched that filter.
**5 — Oro as the deity QA reference (no code change).** Restating the finding from the investigation round for permanent reference: `world_characters` has 5 real `category:'god'` rows (Oro, Nara, Thyun, Solvara, Grak); relationship state (ally/neutral/caution/hostile/deity) lives in `session_notes.npc_relationships`, a per-session DM-toggled cycle, not an NPC attribute — currently `{}` for the one real session, so nothing is set to "deity" yet. Oro (god of color/joy/laughter, most prominent, benevolent, most widely worshipped via the Brightcreed) is the recommended NPC to add to a session roster and cycle to "deity" (4 clicks on the relationship badge) whenever a deity-relationship test case is needed going forward.
**A real near-miss caught before shipping**: the new `showDMToast('Note added ✓', ...)` call landed with a literal ✓ glyph in the JS string on first write — the same recurring Edit-tool escape-normalization behavior on record from many prior sessions. Confirmed via the toast's own `textContent`-based rendering (not `innerHTML`) that an HTML entity wouldn't have worked here even as a fallback — fixed via the established Python exact-string-replacement script to the correct `✓` JS escape, confirmed via a byte-level grep (`e2 9c 93` → `✓`) before it ever reached a commit.
Zero console errors throughout, across every live check. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 17 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty after the fix above.

**2026-08-15 (new session, structured investigate-then-report QA round) — `dm.html`.**
Ask had an explicit shape: a Step 0 environment check, 7 investigate-and-report items (report findings before building anything dependent on them), and 2 build-immediately items with no investigation gate.
**Step 0 — environment check.** Confirmed the local-dev banner from the prior round renders correctly (fresh server, fresh reload, screenshotted) — rules out "banner never actually shipped" as an explanation for any report of stale/wrong behavior this round.
**Item 1 — Dran's DM-Only box reported as yellow/gold, a third color.** Investigated per instruction: grepped for every DM-Only-adjacent CSS system (found and re-checked the two already-fixed-to-teal systems from prior rounds, `.scc-npc2-dm-block` and `.scc-dm-block`, plus a third, unrelated system — `.scc-npc-card.rel-*`/`.scc-rel-badge.rel-*`, the NPC relationship-state coloring, where "caution" uses gold — considered as a possible source of the report before ruling it out). Went live rather than continuing to theorize: added the real Dran NPC to a session roster via `sccAddChipById`, expanded his real card, and read `getComputedStyle()` on the DM-Only box's border, title, and all 36 internal colored elements (icons + "SECRET"/"NOTES" labels) — every one resolves to `rgb(45,212,191)`, the correct teal; confirmed visually via screenshot too (clean teal border/label throughout, no gold anywhere). Dran's own relationship badge reads "NEUTRAL" (blue), not "caution" (gold), further ruling out the relationship-color theory. **No third hidden rule found — the current code is correct.** Reported rather than assumed: most likely explanation is a browser cache serving stale `dm.html`/`tokens.css` on the DM's machine (an on-record recurring issue in this file's own history) or the DM viewing the actual live GitHub Pages site, which carries none of this branch's fixes since the branch has never been merged into whatever Pages deploys from.
**Item 2 — category-specific loot/shop icons.** Checked the real `field_guide_sessions` block schema and the block editor's box-row markup: a box stores only `label` + a single semantic `color` (grey/green/cyan/blue/purple/orange/pink) — no sub-type field exists anywhere, and the editor's only box controls are a Label input and a Color `<select>`. No existing data could drive per-category icon selection. Recommended, not built (per instruction not to guess an auto-detection scheme from box text): add a `subtype` field scoped to orange boxes specifically (Drink/Food/General Store/Weapons/Armor/Magic), surfaced as one more dropdown in the block editor's box row next to the existing color select, DM-assigned per box at authoring time.
**Item 3 — Roll Initiative button behavior.** Read `sccStartCombat()` directly: for each combatant, it reads the manual init input; if a real number is present it's used exactly as typed (never overridden), and only a blank/invalid field gets a random d20+DEX roll — then every combatant (typed and rolled together) is sorted by the resulting initiative descending. So the real behavior is "fill blanks, then sort" — never "override everything," confirming the DM's third hypothesis. Recommended clearer copy: "Start Combat — Sort/Roll Initiative," or keep the label and add a `title` tooltip reading "Uses your typed values as-is; rolls a d20+DEX for anything left blank, then sorts everyone by initiative" — the current "Roll Initiative" wording overstates what happens whenever some or all values are already filled in by hand.
**Item 4 — creature stat-block data coverage.** Queried every field named in the ask across all 52 creatures, not assumed from the known CR gap: type/AC/CR/size/all 6 ability scores/languages/speed/senses/attacks/traits/HP are each exactly 4/52 — the same 4 creatures every time (Grey Fluffets, Still Frogs, Grusk Enforcer, Grusk Color Extractor). Within those same 4, coverage thins further: skill proficiencies 2/52, saving throws 1/52, actions 2/52, reactions 0/52, damage resistances/immunities/condition immunities all 0/52, legendary actions 0/52 (no legendary creatures exist, expected). No `spells` column exists anywhere in the schema, and no spellcasting data appears in any creature's `attacks`/`actions`/`traits` jsonb. Weapon/damage data (name, dice, type, attack bonus) lives inside `attacks` jsonb, populated for the same 4. Also checked whether any of this already reaches the Combat tab: `sccAddToCombatStaging()` only ever pulls `challenge_rating`, `hp_max`, and a DEX-derived initiative modifier into a combatant object — nothing else from a stat block reaches any UI today. Realistic picture reported to the DM: a full stat-block display is genuinely viable today for exactly 4 creatures; every other creature needs real data entry (starting with AC/HP/type/size/ability scores) before a display build would show anything beyond name/tier/category for it.
**Item 5 — Tagged Notes click-flow confusion.** Walked `sccAddTagNote()`'s actual path: it POSTs a blank note, then `sccRenderTaggedNotes()` re-fetches ordered `updated_at.desc`, so a new note should land at the very top of the list. Found two concrete, previously-unidentified failure modes rather than a vague "unclear UI": (1) if a category filter chip is active and the DM creates a note in a *different* category via the dropdown, the note is created successfully in Supabase but immediately filtered out of the visible list by `sccFilterTagNotes()` — the DM sees nothing happen and reasonably concludes the button is broken. (2) even when visible, nothing auto-focuses the new note's title input, scrolls it into view, or shows a success confirmation — it just silently appears after a brief loading flash, easy to miss. Recommended, not built: reset or auto-match the active category filter to the new note's category on create so it's guaranteed visible, auto-focus the title input immediately after render so typing can start right away, and add a brief visible confirmation (toast or highlight) — a structural fix to the interaction, not a cosmetic pass.
**Item 6 — Suggested Level informed by all available factors, built as a range + reasoning, never auto-filling the field.** New pure, side-effect-free `sccSuggestedLevelRange(quest)`: baseline from `first_available` (parses "session-N" → range N to N+1, modeling roughly one level of party growth per session), shifted +1 for `high` stakes / +2 for `campaign` stakes (`low`/`mid` leave the baseline untouched), and widened to include a related creature's CR-derived level whenever a `related[]` entry with `collection:'creatures'` resolves to a real `challenge_rating` — checked against real data first: today only one quest (The Greytalon) has any creature relation at all, and that creature's CR is null, so the CR nudge is real and wired but correctly produces no visible effect on any of the 10 current quests until CR data exists on a related creature. Rendered as a small muted `<span>` next to (never replacing) the existing manually-editable `#q-level-{id}` input — `quest.suggested_level` itself is never written by this code path, only read for its own display. Verified live via direct function calls (`session 2, high stakes` → lvl 3-4 for Anomaly 7-C; `session 1, low stakes` → lvl 1-2 for The Anomaly Reports) and via the rendered Quest Dashboard UI (screenshotted: "Suggested range: lvl 1-2 (session 1, low stakes)" sitting next to a still-blank, still-editable "Not set" input).
**Item 7 — a real deity NPC for future QA.** Confirmed `world_characters` has 5 real `category:'god'` rows (Oro, Nara, Thyun, Solvara, Grak), but "relationship" (ally/neutral/caution/hostile/deity) is a per-session, DM-toggled cycle stored in `session_notes.npc_relationships` — not an attribute of the NPC itself. Queried the one real session row and confirmed `npc_relationships` is currently `{}` (empty) — no NPC anywhere in the real data is presently set to "deity." Reported rather than fabricated: recommended Oro (most prominent, benevolent, most widely worshipped — best fit for a positive-relationship test case) as the NPC to add to a session roster and cycle to "deity" via 4 clicks on the relationship badge, for future QA reference.
**Item 8 — Combat tab layout, built immediately per instruction, no investigation gate.** Moved "+ Add All Players" out of its own row below the staging list and into `.scc-combat-top-row`, directly beside the "Add creature or player…" search box. Added `margin-top:0.75rem` both above `#scc-combatant-staging` (space between the search/add-all row and the list) and above the bottom button row (space between the list and Clear All/Start Combat) — previously flush against both neighbors with zero gap. "Start Combat — Roll Initiative" pushed to the far right of its row via `margin-left:auto` (Clear All remains left-aligned in the same row). No JS behavior touched — all three buttons keep their existing `getElementById`-based show/hide logic, which doesn't depend on DOM position. Verified live: staged 2 real creatures (Grusk Enforcer, Grusk Color Extractor) via the real add-to-staging path, screenshotted the resulting layout (Add All Players beside the search box, visible gaps above/below the staged list, Start Combat pinned right) — then cleared the client-side-only staging state (never written to Supabase, confirmed via existing code, so no DB cleanup was needed).
**Aside, not fixed (out of the requested scope for item 8):** noticed while verifying the layout that "+ Add All Players" is hidden via the same `if (!SCC_COMBATANTS.length)` guard as Start Combat/Clear All — meaning a DM can't bulk-add players to a *completely empty* encounter, only after at least one combatant already exists some other way. This looks like a pre-existing usability gap independent of layout, not something item 8 asked about — flagged here rather than fixed unilaterally.
Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 17 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

**2026-08-14 (new session, local-dev environment banner) — `dm.html`.**
Ask: a persistent, auto-detected "not the live site" banner with zero manual setup per session/checkout.
**Detection.** Pure `window.location.hostname` check against the literal live domain `carlosknightiii.github.io` — `hostname !== 'carlosknightiii.github.io'` shows the banner. No cookie, no localStorage flag, no build-time substitution — evaluates fresh on every page load, so there's nothing to forget to set up. Considered and rejected showing the git branch/folder name: this file is served as a static HTML page with no backend and no build step, so the browser genuinely has no way to know what branch or directory it was checked out into — faking a plausible-looking value would be worse than the honest fallback the ask itself specified (hostname:port). The banner text is `⚠ LOCAL DEV BUILD — NOT LIVE — {hostname}:{port}` (port omitted if the URL has none, e.g. a plain `file://` open falls back to a `'local file'` label).
**Layout.** `#dm-env-banner` is `position:fixed; top:0`, `z-index:10000` — deliberately above the password gate's own `z-index:1000` (`.gate-overlay` is a separate full-viewport fixed layer, so the banner needs to out-rank it to be visible even before login, not just after). Rather than hardcode a pixel height for the push-down, the banner's real `offsetHeight` is measured after paint (`requestAnimationFrame`) and written to a `--dm-env-banner-h` CSS custom property on `:root`, which both `.gate-overlay` (`top: var(...)`, overriding just that one property from its existing `inset:0` shorthand) and `.dm-app` (`margin-top` + a `min-height` reduced by the same amount, so total occupied height still equals exactly 100vh) read from — correct regardless of how the banner's actual rendered height ends up varying by font/OS.
**Verification.** Live on a fresh local server: banner text and bright-red styling confirmed via screenshot exactly as specified, sitting above both the password gate and (after a real login) the app header, in both cases with the app content cleanly offset below it — confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` that the offset is pixel-exact (33px banner height → 33px `--dm-env-banner-h` → `.dm-app` top at exactly 33px, no gap or overlap). Live-domain behavior verified by calling the exact same hostname-comparison function the shipped code uses with `'carlosknightiii.github.io'` passed directly — confirmed it returns `false` (banner hidden) — rather than by actually loading the real deployed site, since this branch (`anavale-wiki-scc-ux`) has never been merged to whatever branch GitHub Pages serves from, so nothing on this branch is reachable at the real URL to test against directly. Zero console errors. A literal ⚠ character landed in the new inline `<script>` on first write (the Edit tool's own text transport normalizing the typed `⚠` escape back into the glyph, the same recurring behavior on record from earlier sessions) — caught by the standing pre-push emoji grep before committing, fixed via the established Python exact-string-replacement script rather than retrying the same edit. Pre-push checks otherwise clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 17 script blocks (16 before this change), diff-scoped emoji grep empty after the fix.

**2026-08-14 (new session, DM's second QA pass — explicit instruction not to trust the first pass's "verified" claims, re-investigated everything from scratch) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`.**
Every item below was re-derived from a fresh grep/live check, not recalled from the prior session's own log — several genuinely contradicted what that log claimed was fixed.
**(1) Quest stakes vs deity purple.** The first purple audit searched direct `var(--purple)`/`#c084fc` usage and missed anything reached through an extra token hop. Re-grepped both `dm.html` and `tokens.css` fully this time and found `--dm-stakes-high-text: var(--purple)` — `.q-stakes-high` (quest "high stakes" badges) resolves to the exact deity hex through `--dm-stakes-high-text`, a level of indirection the narrower search never followed. Confirmed live: 1 real quest (Anomaly 7-C) has `stakes:'high'` today, genuinely rendering deity-purple. Sent for confirmation: reuse `--orange-vivid` (`#fb923c`, already Combat's "high" difficulty color) rather than adding a 10th distinct hue to an already-crowded palette — the two badges are in different UI surfaces (Quest Dashboard vs. Combat tab) and share the same underlying idea ("elevated severity/importance"), so reusing the hue is a feature, not a new collision. Confirmed and implemented — `--dm-stakes-high-bg`/`--dm-stakes-high-text` repointed to the orange-vivid RGB, verified live (`rgb(251,146,60)`, not purple) on both the sidebar quest card and the detail view's badge, deity's own `#c084fc` confirmed untouched.
**(2) DM-Only teal vs "Vareth presence" — investigated, did not reproduce.** Found every real Vareth-related visual element via grep: the location/nation lookup card's "VARETH" badge (`.scc-loc2-vareth-badge`) is a plain grey `#a0a0b0`/`#0b0e16`, nowhere near teal. Two CSS rules (`.scc-flag--vareth`, `.scc-flag--formery`) reference red (`var(--dm-red)`) but are dead code — confirmed via grep neither is referenced by any HTML/JS anywhere, leftover from an earlier design. Also checked `.scc-ql-tag` (generic tag pills, which could show a "vareth" tag) — neutral grey/white, no teal or purple involvement. Reported back rather than guessing at a fix, per instruction, since no real collision was found; most likely explanation is the "Vareth Presence" *row label* inside the big red DM-only box (see next item) — which was about to become teal as a side effect of that fix, correctly, since it's genuinely DM-only content sharing that box's styling.
**(3) A second, larger red DM-only surface the first pass never found.** Grepped the actual red hex values directly (`#d62b2b`, `#f87171`, `248,113,113`) across the whole file instead of trusting the prior session's "all 6 `.scc-dm-block` call sites fixed" claim — found `.scc-npc2-dm-block` (the NPC/location panel's own large "⚠ DM Only — Do Not Read Aloud" card, with nested "Secret"/"Notes"/"Vareth Presence" rows via `.scc-npc2-red`) still hardcoded to `#d62b2b` throughout (background border, title, row-label color) — a completely separate rendering surface (expanded NPC/location cards) from the lookup drawer's `.scc-dm-block` fixed last round, never touched by either earlier pass. Also found quest "Reveals" boxes (`.quest-reveal-item`, explicitly labeled "(DM eyes only)" in its own visible header) still on `rgba(248,113,113,...)`. Converted both to the teal system (`var(--cyan)` for the NPC/location card; `rgba(45,212,191,...)` for reveals, matching the `--scc-dm-only-*` token pattern). Verified live: Reveals boxes render with teal borders (`rgb(45,212,191)`) on Anomaly 7-C's real detail view, screenshotted.
**(4) The "teal border, red/brown body" report was real and pointed at a genuine miss.** Re-read `.scc-dm-text`'s actual CSS rule (the body-text class inside `.scc-dm-block`, the box fixed last round) — found `color: var(--dm-red)`, completely unrelated to the `--scc-dm-only-*` token set the rest of that box already correctly uses. Last round's fix only touched `--scc-dm-only-bg/border/label` (and a `--scc-dm-only-text` token that, it turns out, was defined but never actually consumed anywhere) — `.scc-dm-text` never referenced that token set at all, so its body copy stayed red throughout. Repointed to `var(--scc-dm-only-text)`, which — as a side effect — finally puts that previously-dead token to use.
**(5) Suggested Level — confirmed genuinely editable, affordance improved regardless.** Checked via the accessibility tree (`read_page`) and computed style, not assumption: real `type="number"` textbox, `disabled:false`, `readonly:false`, `cursor:text`, full opacity, exposed correctly to assistive tech as an editable textbox with its "Not set" placeholder. Not a functional bug — added `.q-level-input:placeholder-shown { border-style: dashed; }` plus a hover-border cue and a `title` tooltip so an empty field visibly invites a click instead of reading as inert label text next to the (very differently-affianced) Status `<select>` beside it.
**(6) Field Guide bleeding into Quests/Combat/Notes — confirmed a real CSS specificity bug, not an intended persistent widget.** Checked the actual render logic per instruction rather than guessing: `#scc-panel-fieldguide { display: flex; flex-direction: column; overflow: hidden; }` is a bare ID selector — higher specificity than the base `.scc-panel { display: none; }` rule every other sub-panel relies on for its `.active`-gated visibility — so this one panel's `display:flex` applied unconditionally regardless of the `.active` class, correctly toggled or not. Confirmed live before the fix: switching to Combat left `#scc-panel-fieldguide` with `classList` correctly lacking `.active`, but `getComputedStyle().display` still `"flex"` — the search bar/filter chips/stuck "Loading Field Guide…" state were genuinely always present underneath. Fixed by scoping the rule to `#scc-panel-fieldguide.active`. Verified both directions: `display:none` on Combat/Quests/Notes now, and `display:flex` plus the real document content (54,460 chars, unrelated regression check) still correct when Field Guide itself is active.
**(7) Combat roster quick-add chips didn't support multi-add — confirmed, unlike the search box the prior round fixed.** `sccRenderCombatSessionCreatures()` computed `inEncounter` via `SCC_COMBATANTS.some(id match)` and rendered a permanently non-interactive `<span class="added">` once true, plus `sccQuickAddCombatant()` had its own separate early-return guard — two independent blockers, neither touched when the search box's own dedup guard was removed last round (a different code path entirely). Replaced the binary added/not-added state with a live count (`✓ Name ×3`) and kept the chip as a real `<button>` throughout, so it stays clickable for repeat adds while still surfacing "already used" at a glance — arguably better than the old checkmark, which only ever said yes/no. `.scc-combat-session-chip.added`'s `cursor:default` removed (was actively communicating "not clickable," now wrong). Verified live: clicked the same roster chip 3 times, confirmed 3 real combatants added and the chip reading "✓ Grey Fluffets ×3", screenshotted.
**(8) Tagged Notes reorder + prominence.** Moved the entire `.scc-tagnote-section` block (previously the last thing in the Notes panel) to sit between the 5 structured recap fields and the Save Notes/Copy Summary/Session Completed controls — during-session content before end-of-session actions, matching the DM's own framing of why the old order "read backwards." Split the old single-row toolbar (search input + category select + "+ New Note", all inline) into two rows: a new visually distinct `.scc-tagnote-add-row` (gold-boxed, matching the app's existing quest-related accent) holding just the category select and a `.scc-btn-primary`-styled "+ New Note" button, sitting above a plainer search-only row — so adding a note reads as the section's one clear primary action instead of blending into a generic filter bar. Added a one-line hint under the section title explaining what Tagged Notes are for. Verified live end-to-end: real note created via the actual "+ New Note" button (correct DM-Only teal tag badge, confirming the color fix propagates here too), deleted via the real delete control, confirmed 0 leftover rows via SQL.
**Verification, all live, not assumed.** Fresh local server per the standing lesson, confirmed serving current code via `curl` before trusting it; hit and worked around the same browser tokens.css caching issue on record from prior sessions via a cache-busting query string. Computed-style and accessibility-tree checks throughout, not just visual glances — every color fix confirmed via `getComputedStyle()` returning the exact expected `rgb(...)`, not eyeballed. Zero console errors. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 16 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep's only 3 hits confirmed pre-existing (present in `git show HEAD:dm.html` before this session) and merely relocated by the Tagged Notes reorder, not newly introduced.

**2026-08-14 (same session as the investigation below — DM confirmed the block-based storage shape and the manual link syntax, built and shipped) — `dm.html`, new `field_guide_sessions` table.**
DM's answers: block-based storage as recommended, confirmed as-is. Manual `[[type:id|Label]]` markup + link-insert helper, confirmed as recommended (not auto-detection, not skipped).
**Migration, done with a script, not by hand.** Wrote a one-off Python script (BeautifulSoup, scratch directory only — not part of the shipped codebase) to parse the exact fragment between `<div class="scc-fg-doc-inner">` and its close (`dm.html:3238-3738` at the time) into the block schema from the investigation: `title`/`subtitle`/`h1`/`h2` as plain decoded-Unicode text, `tagline`/`meta`/`italic`/`p`/`footer`/`list` as HTML fragments (inline `<strong>`/`<em>` preserved verbatim, not stripped), `table` as genuinely structured `{variant, headerRow, rows}` (variant captures the real `--scenario`/`--shop` CSS modifiers), and `box` as `{color, label, html, table}` — `color` stores the *semantic* name (`cyan`, not the legacy `yellow` CSS class it actually maps to) so the editor never shows the confusing class name. Parsed cleanly with zero warnings: 140 top-level blocks — 48 boxes, 30 h2, 20 standalone tables, 14 h1, 15 p, 4 italic, 4 lists, plus the 5 document-level fields — matching the investigation's own counts exactly (22 total tables − 2 real box-nested ones = 20 standalone, confirmed programmatically, not just visually). Box label text came through with real decoded emoji characters (💠, 🟢 — the numeric HTML entities the original markup used) — these are Supabase **data**, fetched at runtime into a JS string, not literal characters typed into `dm.html`'s own script source, so the file's C2 no-literal-emoji rule (about parse-time `SyntaxError`s) doesn't apply to them; confirmed the pre-push diff-scoped emoji grep on `dm.html` itself still comes back empty. Inserted as one row (`session_number:1`) via direct SQL — confirmed `jsonb_array_length(blocks) = 140` immediately after.
**dm.html changes.** The 505-line static `#scc-fg-doc-inner` block was replaced with a 5-line dynamic shell (session `<select>` + Edit toggle + an empty content div) — the diff is almost entirely additions (new render/editor functions), not modifications to existing code, so the risk profile is closer to "add a feature" than "rewrite a feature." `sccFgRenderBlock()`/`sccFgRenderDoc()` reconstruct the *exact* CSS classes the old static markup used per block type (`scc-fg-doc-h1`, `scc-fg-box scc-fg-box--{cssColor}` via a `color→class` map that hides the `cyan→yellow` legacy mismatch from the data model, `scc-fg-table` + variant suffix, etc.) — zero CSS was touched. `sccFgLinkify()` handles `[[type:id|Label]]` → a clickable `<span onclick="sccOpenLookup(type,id)">`, applied to every HTML-bearing field including table cells, so a link can live inside an NPC reference table too, not just prose. The session selector (`SCC_ALL_SESSIONS`, already fetched for the unrelated Session History feature — no new fetch) sits above the document; changing it also fixes the real bug the investigation surfaced — `SCC_FG_DATA` (the sidebar's session-scoped cache) is now explicitly nulled on every switch so `sccRenderFieldGuide()` actually refetches for the newly selected session instead of staying stuck on whichever session loaded first. `sccRenderFieldGuide()`'s own `sessionId` line was changed to prefer this new selector's value over the global `SCC_SESSION` when one has been chosen, falling back to the old behavior otherwise — a one-line, backward-compatible change.
**Editor.** Groups the working (deep-cloned) blocks array by nearest preceding `h1` into collapsible sections — same shape as tonight's `session_scenarios` editor, but with a richer per-row control set since this document's real content isn't that editor's uniform two-field rows: plain text inputs for headings, HTML-capable textareas (with a link-insert helper button) for prose, and a genuinely structured table sub-editor (header-row toggle, add/remove rows, per-cell textareas, a style-variant dropdown) for both standalone tables and the 2 real box-nested ones — no raw-HTML editing anywhere in the whole document, matching the investigation's own design intent. Move-up/move-down/delete per block, add-block-to-section, add-new-section. Save does one PATCH (or POST, if the session has no row yet) of the whole `blocks` array — matching the scenario editor's own "local working copy, one save call" pattern, not a per-field autosave.
**Verification — rigorous, not visual, per the explicit instruction.** Fetched the real pre-migration `dm.html` via `git show HEAD:dm.html` (HEAD at the time, before this session's edits), extracted the original static fragment's rendered `textContent` in a real hidden DOM element, extracted the new block-rendered version's `textContent` the same way, and compared: with all whitespace stripped entirely (not just collapsed — collapsed whitespace still showed a real difference, since the old hand-indented HTML source had newlines between sibling tags that the new compact `.join('')` render doesn't reproduce, an insignificant formatting artifact, not lost content), both are **exactly** 34,733 characters and identical. This is the byte-for-byte confirmation the task asked for, not a "looks right" visual check. Separately: inserted a real link (searched "Pellam" through the actual search-and-click UI, landed on the real `formery-clerk-gobblewump` NPC id) into a real paragraph, saved, and clicked the resulting span in read view — the real lookup drawer opened showing Pellam Voss's full card (portrait, role, motivation, contradiction, appearance), confirmed via screenshot. Tested the empty-session path directly (pointed the session state at session 2, which has no `field_guide_sessions` row) — correctly rendered "No Field Guide written for Session 2 yet" instead of erroring. Reverted the test link afterward and confirmed via SQL the real Session 1 row is back to its exact pristine 140-block migrated state (byte-identical block 12, `jsonb_array_length` still 140). Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 16 extracted inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep on `dm.html` itself empty.

**2026-08-13 (new session, Field Guide multi-session support + inline entity links — investigation only, no code changed, 2 open questions sent for confirmation) — `dm.html` read in full detail, no writes.**
Explicit investigate-then-report instruction; nothing built, per the scope boundary (infrastructure + lossless Session 1 migration only, no new session 2+ narrative content).
**a) Exact current shape of the hardcoded document, confirmed by reading the whole thing, not sampling.** `#scc-fg-doc` (`dm.html:3236-3740`, 505 lines) is one static HTML block, no template, no session parameter anywhere. Counted precisely: 14 `scc-fg-doc-h1` sections, 30 `scc-fg-doc-h2` subsections, 6 `scc-fg-doc-italic` intro lines, ~86 plain `<p>` paragraphs, 48 colored callout boxes (`scc-fg-box scc-fg-box--{color}`) across exactly 6 CSS color variants — green (read-aloud), yellow (renders **cyan** today — the class name is a known leftover from the color-token rename 6 sessions ago, kept then because renaming ~20 hardcoded references wasn't worth it; DM-only), blue (world lore), purple (comedy beats/★), orange (loot/shop/food), and grey (used exactly once, the "HOW TO USE THIS GUIDE" box) — 22 `<table class="scc-fg-table">` tables (2 real modifier variants found: `--scenario` for 2-column action/response tables, `--shop` for 3-column item/price/notes tables; the rest are plain unmodified 2-column key/value tables with no `<thead>`, except one — Section 14's DM Confidence table — which does have a `<thead>`), and 4 `<ul>` lists. Confirmed via direct inspection (not assumed from the CSS existing) that 2 of the 22 tables are genuinely nested *inside* a box (Section 6's "AVAILABLE TO PURCHASE" and Section 7's "DRINKS," both orange shop boxes) — real structural nesting, not a false positive from the `.scc-fg-box .scc-fg-table` CSS rule existing defensively. The 5-color legend sentence ("Green boxes = read aloud... Orange boxes = loot and shop") lives inside the grey box as ordinary migratable content, not separately hardcoded chrome — so it's treated as Session 1's own content, not extracted into permanent site furniture, per the explicit "don't redesign, migrate as-is" instruction.
**b) Sidebar infrastructure — confirmed what transfers and what doesn't.** `sccRenderFieldGuide()` (`dm.html:11355`) fetches `world_characters`/`creatures`/`pois`/`items` plus one `session_notes` row keyed off `sessionId = SCC_SESSION ? SCC_SESSION.session_number : 1`, then filters all four down to whatever that session's Setup actually assigned (`npc_ids`/`creature_ids`/`location_ids`/`item_ids_awarded`). This fetch-and-filter logic is genuinely reusable and already close to session-parameterized — it just needs `sessionId` to accept an override instead of always reading the global `SCC_SESSION`. Everything downstream of that (`sccFgDraw()`'s card rendering, `SCC_FG_FILTER`/`SCC_FG_OPEN`/`SCC_FG_CARD_OPEN` UI state, the NPC/Creature/Location/Item/DM-Notes filter buttons) is sidebar-specific presentation logic with no bearing on the document itself — it doesn't transfer to document storage, it just needs to keep working when a new session selector changes which session's data it's showing. **Real pre-existing bug found in the process, not introduced by anything this session**: `SCC_FG_DATA` (the sidebar's fetched-data cache) is set once and never reset to `null` anywhere in the codebase — confirmed via grep. Today this is invisible because nothing ever changes which session the sidebar looks at after first load; once a session selector exists, this becomes a real staleness bug (switching sessions wouldn't refresh the sidebar) that has to be fixed as a direct, necessary part of wiring the selector, not a nice-to-have. `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` (already fetched by the existing Session History feature, real session_number list) is directly reusable as the new selector's option source — no new fetch needed.
**c) Recommended storage shape.** New table `field_guide_sessions` (`session_number integer unique`, `blocks jsonb`, `created_at`/`updated_at`) — one row per session, entire document as one ordered array of typed blocks, not a handful of named columns. Reasoning, not a default: the document's real content (confirmed in 1a) is too heterogeneous — arbitrary mixes of prose, boxes-with-nested-tables, and standalone tables per section — for a clean fixed-column schema to hold without either losing fidelity or growing an unbounded number of nullable columns. A flat ordered block list sidesteps both problems: each block is self-contained and stores its real content close to verbatim, and it's a natural fit for a collapsible-by-h1-section editor (blocks between one `h1` and the next form a group) mirroring the `session_scenarios` editor built earlier tonight — the same pattern, just with a richer per-row type set than that editor's uniform 2-field rows needed. Block types: `title`/`subtitle`/`tagline`/`meta`/`footer` (document-level text/HTML), `h1`/`h2` (plain text), `italic`/`p`/`list` (HTML-capable prose, edited as a textarea — same posture as existing freeform fields like `dm_notes` elsewhere in this app), `table` (**structured**, not raw HTML — `{variant, headerRow, rows}`, since tables are a large and genuinely edit-worthy fraction of the real content, not throwaway text) and `box` (`{color, label, html, table}` — `color` stores the *semantic* name, e.g. `'cyan'` not `'yellow'`, so the DM-facing editor never shows the confusing legacy class name; `table` is optional, covering the 2 real box-nested-table cases without inventing a separate nested block system). Considered and rejected: raw-HTML-per-section storage (simpler, but makes the "verify byte-for-byte" requirement meaningless to check structurally and gives up on any real per-block editing); a handful of named document columns (doesn't fit the section-to-section content variety without a pile of always-partially-null fields).
**Step 2 — inline entity links, one real open question sent, not guessed at.** Recommended manual `[[type:id|Label]]` markup embedded directly in a block's HTML/prose text, resolved at render time into a click that calls the existing `sccOpenLookup(type, id)` — paired with a small searchable link-insert helper next to each prose field so the DM finds the right entity and gets the correct id inserted without needing to memorize or hand-type ids. Recommended over auto-detecting known entity names in the text: this exact dataset has a real, elevated false-positive risk for auto-linking (Gerald, Grey, Vel, Fess, Mira, and others are all short, common-word-adjacent real names that would readily false-match inside ordinary prose), and every other inline-linking precedent already in this app (`quest.related[]`, made clickable 2 sessions ago) is explicit DM-authored collection+id pairs, never inferred from text — matching that established convention rather than introducing a new, riskier mechanism. Waiting on confirmation before building this piece specifically, per instruction.
No schema or UI code written this session — waiting on confirmation of 1c (storage shape) and Step 2 (link mechanism) before building, per explicit instruction.
