**2026-08-13 (continuing the same session — DM-reported bug fix) — Fixed Story-tab card title clipping (`sheet/index.html`, CSS-only).**
DM screenshotted Visenya's Your Background accordion: the Background card's title "GREYWITNESSED" was visibly cut off mid-word at the card edge, no ellipsis, just a hard clip.
**Root cause, confirmed by reading the real CSS before touching anything (not assumed from the bug report's own diagnosis, though it turned out to be exactly right)**: `.cs-story-card-head` is a flex row (image + `.cs-story-card-headtext`); `.cs-story-card-headtext` had no `min-width:0`, so its default `min-width:auto` resolved to its content's min-content size — for an unbreakable single-word title at the card's 26px display font, that min-content width can exceed the available row space, pushing the whole row wider than the card. Separately, `.cs-story-card-title` itself had zero overflow/wrap handling at all — no `overflow-wrap`, `white-space`, or `text-overflow` of any kind, unlike `.cs-equip-slot-name` elsewhere in the file which already has the equivalent ellipsis pattern for this exact class of problem.
**Fix**: `min-width: 0` added to `.cs-story-card-headtext`. `overflow-wrap: break-word` added to both `.cs-story-card-title` and `.cs-past-card-title` — wrap, not the ellipsis pattern `.cs-equip-slot-name` uses, per the bug report's own reasoning: these are lore/proper-noun names (background names, etc.) a player should be able to read in full, not a secondary label where truncation is an acceptable tradeoff. `overflow-wrap` only engages when a word would otherwise overflow its line, so normal multi-word titles keep wrapping at spaces exactly as before — confirmed this isn't a behavior change for the common case, not just assumed.
**Verified `.cs-past-card-title` needed the same fix for a genuinely different reason, not just for consistency** — traced `pastCard()`'s two layouts: the default (header) layout reuses `.cs-story-card-head`/`.cs-story-card-headtext` verbatim, so it shares the exact same bug. The `--full` layout (Left Behind, Why You Left) doesn't use `.cs-story-card-headtext` at all — its title sits directly in `.cs-past-card-body`, which already had `min-width:0` — but that alone doesn't prevent unbreakable text from visually overflowing its own rendered box; `min-width:0` controls how much space a flex item is allocated, not whether its own content wraps within that space. So `.cs-past-card-title` needed the `overflow-wrap` fix regardless of which layout it's used in.
**Checked all real title-bearing data, not just the one reported name**: pulled all 16 `backgrounds.name` values — "Greywitnessed" (13 chars) is the longest, several others (Threadpuller, Masquerader, Ringscarred, Stillsought) are close behind and would have hit the same bug once a character with one of those backgrounds loaded this accordion. Also checked `regions`/`organizations` names (other data sources feeding these same title classes) — confirmed those are naturally safe without any fix, since they're multi-word ("The Gigglegloom Conclave," "The Prism Exchange") and already wrap correctly at spaces; only single unbreakable words were ever at risk, confirming the fix's scope matches the actual risk surface rather than being broader or narrower than needed.
Verified live on Visenya Oryn (real `background_id: greywitnessed`, confirmed via SQL before testing, matching the DM's screenshot exactly) at three widths: normal (1100px, title renders on one line, no clip), a stress width (650px), and a forced worst-case 240px (`.cs-story-card`'s own `min-width` floor, applied via a temporary inline style override, not real data) — at 240px the title correctly wraps to multiple lines and `cardEl.scrollWidth === cardEl.clientWidth` (240===240) confirmed zero horizontal overflow, the direct signature of the fix working under the true worst case, not just "happens to fit" at the tested viewport. Confirmed all 7 Your Past cards (both layouts) show zero overflow (`scrollWidth <= clientWidth` on each). Zero console errors, zero page-level overflow at 375px mobile. `new Function()` syntax check clean (no JS touched, CSS-only change, checked anyway per habit). Pushed live.

Verified: `grep "^## "` confirms §1 through §11 sequential with no gaps or duplicates. `grep "See Section"` confirms only the one fixed cross-reference exists. **2026-08-13 (continuing the same session — last of the 5-item placement roadmap, Inventory tab piece) — Added Magic Item Attunement (`sheet/index.html`, `items`/`character_sheet` schema). Real overlap-avoidance call, a real deviation from the handoff's suggested data source, and a real bug caught before shipping — not a routine build.**
**Parallel-branch check, per the standing workflow agreed earlier this session.** This handoff's own plan touches `dm.html` (a checkbox on the item form). `git diff --stat main...anavale-wiki-scc-ux` showed that branch had grown to 1423 changed lines in `dm.html`, with hunks covering exactly the item-form region (~2200–3700) I'd need to edit. Rather than add to an actively-churning region of someone else's in-flight work, deferred just the DM Tools checkbox — confirmed nothing is blocked by this, since the handoff's own "Why" section already states no item is flagged attunement-requiring yet. Everything else (schema, all rendering/interaction logic) lives entirely in `sheet/index.html`, confirmed untouched by that branch, so built normally on `main`.
**Schema**: confirmed via `information_schema` that neither `items.requires_attunement` nor `character_sheet.attuned_items` existed (this time the handoff's "no existing column" claim held up, unlike the Hit Dice build two entries ago) — added both via migration, matching the handoff's suggested shapes exactly (boolean default false; `text[]` default `'{}'`, same key format as `character_sheet.equipped` already uses).
**Data source — deviated from the handoff's suggested `CATALOG_CACHE`.** Traced how that cache actually gets populated before trusting it: `runInventorySearch()` (the +Add Item search modal) writes it with a *partial* select (`id,name,category,cost_gp,weight_lb,summary,base_ac,damage_dice,damage_type,image` — no `requires_attunement`), while `fetchInventory()` writes it with a full `select('*')` for owned items — meaning `CATALOG_CACHE` is genuinely mixed depending on which path touched an entry last. `EQUIPPED_ITEMS` (populated by `fetchEquipped()`/`selectEquipItem()`, both always `select('*')`, and scoped to exactly the items in `EQUIPPED`) is the correct, purpose-built source for "full data for a currently-equipped item" — already used this exact way elsewhere in the file (AC calc, Special Effects). Used that instead.
**Real bug found and fixed before shipping, not after.** The handoff said to hook "clear attunement on unequip" into "whichever function currently handles unequip," naming `unequipSlot()`/`clearInventoryEquippedSlot()` as candidates to verify. Traced the real code: `clearInventoryEquippedSlot()` only manages an inventory row's own column, not `EQUIPPED`/attunement state — the real hook points are the three (then found a fourth, `autoUnequipOffHandForTwoHanded()`, which the handoff never mentioned) places that already compute "is this item still equipped anywhere else" right before cleaning up `EQUIPPED_ITEMS`. Added the new `clearAttunementIfUnequipped()` call at all four candidate sites initially — then, re-reading `selectEquipItem()`'s own comment ("Same physical inventory row moving from a different slot entirely — not... this row moving from elsewhere") realized one of the four, the same-item slot-move path, is NOT a real unequip: the item gets re-equipped into `targetKey` a few lines later in the same function call. Calling the new clear-on-unequip function there would have wrongly dropped attunement every time a player moved an already-attuned item to a different slot (e.g. swapping a ring from right hand to left) — a real regression, not a hypothetical. Removed that one call before it shipped; kept the other three (the true sibling-clear, the two-handed off-hand auto-clear, and explicit `unequipSlot()`).
**Rendering**: `getAttunableEquippedItems()`/`renderAttunement()`/`toggleAttunement()` per the handoff's shape, wired into `renderEquipped()`'s own end (alongside `renderACBadge()`/`renderRingsRow()`/`renderSpecialEffects()`) rather than a separate hook — guarantees the Attunement list refreshes exactly when the doll does, on load and on every equip/unequip, with no risk of drifting out of sync. Placed under the doll (inside `.cs-inv-top-col--doll`, after the ring row) rather than as a 3rd top-row column, to avoid re-tuning the doll/Special-Effects 3:2 flex ratio a DM live-review already tuned once. New `.cs-attune-*` CSS reuses `.cs-effect-panel`'s color tokens (`--blue-700`/`--blue-300`) for the outer box, `--gold` for the Attune/Attuned button (matches the existing "active resource" gold-fill convention).
**Verification, with 3 of Kael's real equipped items temporarily flagged `requires_attunement` (reverted after, including his `equipped_slot` inventory columns and `character_sheet.equipped`/`attuned_items`, confirmed via a final SQL read matching his real pre-test state exactly)**: attuned to all 3 with realistic delays between actions, confirmed the DB write landed correctly (`["leather-armor","longbow","braided-cord-ring"]`); separately reproduced this codebase's already-documented zero-delay `saveSheet()` race by firing all 3 toggles with no delay, confirmed it's the same pre-existing risk (not a bug introduced here) by rerunning with realistic delays and getting a correct result. Confirmed the 3-item cap blocks a 4th attempt with zero DB write. Confirmed unequipping Longbow correctly dropped it from `attuned_items` (both locally and via SQL) while leaving the other two attuned items untouched. Confirmed moving the still-attuned Braided Cord Ring from `ring_right` to `ring_left` preserved its attunement — the exact scenario the bug fix above protects. Zero console errors throughout, zero horizontal overflow at 375px mobile, attunement box confirmed hidden (not an empty box) once all test flags were reverted. `new Function()` syntax check clean. Pushed live.
This closes the original 5-item placement roadmap. Cantrips & Prepared Spells remains, explicitly scoped to its own future session.

**2026-08-13 (continuing the same session — third of the 5-item placement roadmap, Story tab piece) — Added Speed + Size to the Your Background accordion (`sheet/index.html`, `species` schema). Two real DM decisions resolved before writing any code, per the handoff's own instruction not to guess either one.**
**Decision 1 — placement.** The handoff offered two options (header `.cs-mi-triple-row`, or the "Who You Are" accordion) but flagged its own uncertainty, since the DM's stated reasoning was "near Species/Region, not header." Checked both candidates against the real code before presenting choices: `renderSpeciesDesc()` confirmed the existing Species description genuinely lives in the header (contradicting option (a)'s "not header" framing), and `buildWhoYouAreHtml()` confirmed "Who You Are" holds only personality/narrative fields (First Impression, Fears, Quest) — nothing species- or region-adjacent, a thematic mismatch for option (b). Found a third, better-fitting option the handoff hadn't listed: "Your Background" (a different accordion), which already has a real "Home Region" card sitting in it — the literal match to "near Species/Region." Presented all three to the DM rather than force-fitting one of the handoff's two; DM picked the third.
**Decision 2 — rules edition.** The handoff's own example ("Dwarves are 25 ft/Medium") is a 2014 PHB value, but a comment already in `sheet/index.html` (from the earlier skill-proficiency-stacking build) cites "2024 PHB stacking rules" as this campaign's actual basis — and 2024 changed Dwarf/Gnome/Halfling speeds from 25 ft to 30 ft, a direct conflict for 3 of the 12 species. Flagged the discrepancy rather than silently following either source; DM confirmed 2024 PHB.
**Schema**: confirmed via `information_schema` that `species` had no `speed_ft`/`size` columns (or anything similarly named) before adding them — not assumed either way, per the handoff's explicit instruction (which also called out the prior two builds' real schema surprises as the reason to check first every time). Real side-finding while reading the schema: `species.phb_equivalent` already exists as a real column, populated for all 12 species with the exact same mapping (`veilstepped→Changeling`, etc.) as the hardcoded `SPECIES_PHB` JS mirror restored in an Aug 12 session — that mirror could be replaced by reading this column live instead. Not fixed here (out of scope on a Speed/Size handoff), flagged in Next Priorities.
**Seed data**: rather than trust memory for real gameplay-facing 5e facts, cross-checked the 2024 PHB speed/size table against several independent web sources before writing the migration. Confirmed: Dwarf/Gnome/Halfling all raised to 30 ft (from 2014's 25 ft); Goliath is the one standout at 35 ft (unchanged from raising in 2024, confirmed by two sources independently describing it as the "only species" at that speed); Gnome/Halfling remain the only two Small species; everything else (Human/Elf/Tiefling/Aasimar/Dragonborn/Orc, plus the two non-core species Changeling/Warforged, which were already stable 30 ft/Medium across every edition they've appeared in) is 30 ft/Medium. Seeded all 12 real species (keyed off the existing `phb_equivalent` column, not re-typed by hand per Anavale name) plus left the `other`/"Various" placeholder row null. Verified the full seeded table via a follow-up SQL read before writing any render code against it.
**Rendering**: new `speedSizeCard` inside `renderBackgroundCardsHtml()`, positioned right after the Home Region card (matching "near Region" literally) — reuses `storyCard()` verbatim with no image/benefits box, just a label + a single value line ("30 ft · Medium"), since the handoff explicitly said this needed no tooltip or explainer (self-evident once labeled, unlike Saving Throws/Passive Perception). No new CSS — `.cs-story-cards`' existing `align-items: flex-start` (fixed in an earlier session specifically so shorter cards don't get stretched to match taller siblings) already handles a minimal card cleanly.
**Refresh timing**: `SPECIES_ROW` resolves via `fetchSpeciesData()`, a fetch not covered by the two (`fetchRegionData`/`fetchBackgroundData`) that already gate the existing `renderBackgroundCardsDynamic()` refresh — added a call to that same function inside `fetchSpeciesData()`'s `.then()`/`.catch()` instead of building a new refresh path, per the handoff's explicit "piggyback on the same refresh call" instruction.
Verified live on Kael Evander (Veilstepped → Changeling → 30 ft/Medium under the new seed): card reads "SPEED & SIZE / 30 FT · MEDIUM", confirmed via a DOM read (not just the screenshot) that it's the 3rd of 5 cards, immediately after Home Region. Screenshotted the full Your Background row at 1100px (5 cards, correct order, no stretching) and confirmed zero horizontal overflow at 375px mobile. Zero console errors. `new Function()` syntax check clean. Pushed live.

**2026-08-12 (later same day, second of the 5-item placement roadmap — Header piece) — Added Initiative + Hit Dice to the MainInfo header (`sheet/index.html`, `character_sheet` schema). Real mid-build schema correction, not just a straightforward build.**
**Initiative**: `computeInitiative(ch)` — `DEX mod + 5 if 'Alert' in ch.feats, else +0` — verbatim from the handoff. Confirmed `Alert` is the only initiative-affecting feat in `FEAT_TIPS` and that `ch.feats` is the same field/pattern `computeHpBase()` (`js/hp-calc.js`) already reads for the Tough-feat HP check, per the handoff's own instruction to reuse that source rather than re-deriving it. Checked the real render order before assuming "no async refresh needed" was actually true (not just trusting the handoff's claim) — `CH = ch` and `ABILITY_SCORES = scores` are both set synchronously in `renderCharacter()`, well before `buildMainInfoHtml()` is called, so the claim held up: computed once, inline, directly into the initial HTML string (`<div class="cs-mi-init-badge">`, styled as a smaller/secondary variant of the AC badge — `--blue-600`/`--blue-100`, same token family as AC's `--blue-100`/`--blue-800` but a dimmer step so it doesn't compete for attention). No separate render/refresh function needed or built, unlike every other header stat so far.
**Hit Dice — the handoff's data-model premise was wrong, caught via the standing "verify the DB write" step, not before starting.** The handoff said "no existing column tracks this — needs a new one" and gave a migration for `hit_dice_spent`; that migration ran and the sheet was built against it before the routine post-write SQL check (done for every build this session) surfaced that `character_sheet` already had `hit_dice_total`/`hit_dice_used` columns — populated (`1`/`0` across all 3 characters) but referenced by zero code anywhere in the repo (confirmed via grep across `sheet/index.html`/`dm.html`/`js/*.js`/`character.html`). This should have been caught by pulling the full column list before writing the migration, not after — noting the process gap plainly rather than glossing over it. Fixed immediately, before shipping: dropped the just-added `hit_dice_spent` column, consolidated all code onto the pre-existing `hit_dice_used` (added the column comment it never had), rather than leave two live, functionally-identical "spent" trackers in the schema for a future session to be confused by. `hit_dice_total` (the sibling column) stays dead/unused on purpose — the handoff's "don't cache max, derive it live from `ch.level`" instruction is the better design (matches `js/hp-calc.js`'s own derive-don't-cache principle for HP), so max is computed live in `renderHitDiceHtml()`, not read from that column. Flagged `hit_dice_total` as a known-inert column in Next Priorities so a future session doesn't wonder whether it's the real source.
**Rendering**: `renderHitDiceHtml()`/`toggleHitDie()`/`renderHitDice()` per the handoff, pip count = `ch.level` (currently 1 for the whole party — confirmed the single-pip case renders cleanly, not just the eventual multi-pip case). Own CSS (`.cs-mi-hd-pip`/`.spent`) rather than reusing `.cs-mi-ds-pip` — deliberately inverted semantics from Death Saves (available = bright gold fill, spent = dim hollow) since an available hit die is the *good* state here, the opposite of a filled death-save pip. No rest-flow exists anywhere on the sheet to auto-restore spent dice (same gap Death Saves/Spell Slots already have) — left as manual pip-clicking, per the handoff's explicit instruction not to scope-creep into building one; flagged in Next Priorities as a real future gap.
**Refresh timing**: wired `renderHitDice()` into `renderLivePlay()` (needs both `SHEET.hit_dice_used` and `CLASS_ROW.hit_die`) — verified against the real fetch chain rather than assumed, per the handoff's explicit instruction not to assume this time (it didn't even guess a hook, having been corrected twice already this session on this exact point).
Verified live on Kael Evander (Ranger, level 1, `d10` hit die, real Alert feat): Initiative badge reads "+7" with tooltip "2 Dex + 5 (Alert) = 7", hand-checked against his real DEX 15 (mod +2). Hit Dice shows one available `d10` pip. Clicked it — pip turns spent, `SHEET.hit_dice_used` updates to 1 locally; confirmed via a follow-up SQL read that `hit_dice_used=1` landed in Supabase (`hit_dice_total` unchanged at 1, confirming it's genuinely inert). Clicked again — reverted to available, confirmed via a second SQL read that it's back to 0. Screenshotted both desktop (1100px — AC/Init/HP/Temp HP all fit one row, Hit Dice wraps to its own line) and mobile (375px — wraps to 3 rows, zero horizontal overflow). Zero console errors throughout. `new Function()` syntax check clean. Pushed live.

**2026-08-12 (later same day, first of a 5-item placement roadmap — Class tab piece) — Added Spellcasting Modifier/Spell Save DC/Spell Attack Bonus (`sheet/index.html`, `classes` schema).**
DM handed over a build doc for the Class tab spellcasting stats — the highest-priority of 5 agreed placement gaps (Header Initiative/Hit Dice, Story tab Speed/Size, Class tab spellcasting stats + a separate later Cantrips/Prepared list, Inventory tab Attunement — this doc covered only the spellcasting stats).
**Investigated the data source before writing any render code, per the doc's own explicit instruction not to guess.** Confirmed via `information_schema` that `classes` has no structured spellcasting-ability column. Pulled real `primary_ability`/`casting_summary` for all 12 classes and confirmed `primary_ability` is unusable as-is for half-casters — it's a combined string (Paladin `"Strength & Charisma"`, Ranger `"Dexterity & Wisdom"`) that doesn't say *which* half is the casting ability; that's only stated in `casting_summary`'s prose ("...casting them with Charisma", "...cast using Wisdom"), and the doc explicitly said not to regex-parse that field. Reported this back to the DM with the real per-class table rather than picking a workaround — asked whether to (a) add a real `spellcasting_ability` column (recommended, matches how `saving_throws`/`hit_die` are modeled — real per-class mechanical data belongs in `classes`, not a hardcoded JS mirror like `CLASS_FLAVOR_NAME`/`SPECIES_PHB`, which are Anavale-flavor text, a different category) or (b) a hardcoded JS lookup instead. DM chose (a).
**Schema**: added `classes.spellcasting_ability` (nullable text, short ability key — `str/dex/con/int/wis/cha` — chosen to match the keys the sheet's own JS already indexes `ABILITY_SCORES` by, no translation layer needed) via migration, seeded from the real `casting_summary` text: `cha` for bard/sorcerer/warlock/paladin, `wis` for cleric/druid/ranger, `int` for wizard, `null` for barbarian/fighter/monk/rogue (confirmed non-casters via their own `casting_summary` — "Barbarians don't cast spells," etc.). Matches standard 5e rules exactly, so high confidence despite being newly authored data.
**Found mid-build, not assumed from the doc**: the doc's suggested companion step ("DM Tools content manager" field) doesn't apply — grepped `dm.html` for any class-editing UI and found none at all; the `classes` table has been maintained by direct migration only since its original Aug 7 seed (`hit_die_tip`/`saves_tip`/`weapons_tip` were all added the same way, no form ever built for any of them). Followed that established precedent rather than building a new admin UI nobody asked for — flagged the discrepancy rather than silently picking a side.
**Rendering**: new `.cs-ov-class-card` directly above the existing Spell Slots card (per the doc's placement ask — keeps "how my magic works" as one visual cluster). `spellStatRowHtml()`/`renderSpellcastingStats()` built per the doc's formulas verbatim (`8 + prof + abMod` for DC, `prof + abMod` for attack bonus) and reuse `.cs-ab-skill`/`.cs-ab-tip` — same "one stat-row component across the sheet" reasoning as the Saves build. Card starts `display:none` in its first, synchronous HTML (matches the pre-existing pattern every other CLASS_ROW-dependent block on this tab already uses, since `CLASS_ROW` is null at that point) and is shown/hidden by id (`cs-spellcasting-stats-card`) rather than via `el.parentElement` as the doc's snippet did — more explicit, same effect.
**Refresh timing — deviated from the doc's suggested hook, same reasoning as the Saves build**: the doc said to extend `renderClassTabDynamic()` (assuming that's where saves got wired), but that function actually runs *before* `SHEET` loads (`fetchClassData()` calls it directly, ahead of `fetchOrCreateSheet()` in the chain) — the Saves build's actual finding was that `renderAbilitySkills()`/`renderLivePlay()` is the correct hook, not `renderClassTabDynamic()`. Wired `renderSpellcastingStats()` into `renderLivePlay()` alongside `renderSpellSlots()`/`renderAbilitySkills()` instead, where both `CLASS_ROW` and `SHEET` are guaranteed resolved.
Verified live on Kael Evander (Ranger, WIS 16 → mod +3, proficiency bonus 2 — a real half-caster case, the exact scenario that made `primary_ability` unusable): Spellcasting Modifier `+3`, Spell Save DC `13`, Spell Attack Bonus `+5`, hand-checked against the formulas. Simulated the non-caster path in the live browser (temporarily nulled `CLASS_ROW.spellcasting_ability`, called `renderSpellcastingStats()`, confirmed the card's computed `display` goes to `none`, then restored the real value and confirmed it comes back) — no real character data touched, no SQL cleanup needed. Zero console errors, zero horizontal overflow at 375px mobile. `new Function()` syntax check clean. Pushed live.

**2026-08-12 (later same day, after the PHB-terms fix below) — Added Saving Throws + Passive Perception to the Ability Scores tab (`sheet/index.html`), per a DM-supplied handoff doc.**
Built per the handoff doc's spec, verified against real code/data before implementing rather than trusting the doc's snippets verbatim (read `abilitySkillsInnerHtml()`/`buildAbilityScoresHtml()`/`renderAbilitySkills()`/`renderClassTabDynamic()` and the real `classes.saving_throws` values first, per standing instruction to read files before editing).
**Saving Throws**: `classes.saving_throws` confirmed via SQL as a comma-separated full-ability-name string ("Strength, Constitution", etc.) across all 12 real classes — matches the doc's assumed format exactly, no surprises. New `AB_FULL` (short key → full name) and `parseSavingThrows(savesStr)` map it to the `str/dex/con/int/wis/cha` keys already used everywhere else on this tab. New `abilitySaveRowHtml(ab, modNum, profBonus, isProficient)` renders one row per ability card, reusing the existing `.cs-ab-skill*` classes verbatim (same "Prof." label, same positive/negative total coloring) — no new skill-row CSS, just a small `.cs-ab-save` wrapper for spacing. `SAVE_TIPS` (6 plain-language tooltips, one per ability) ported directly from the doc.
**Passive Perception**: formula is `10 + Perception's total` — rather than duplicating the skill list's proficiency/flat-bonus math a second time, extracted `computeSkillTotal(name, modNum, profBonus, ch)` out of `abilitySkillsInnerHtml()` (previously all inline) so both the skill row and the new `passivePerceptionBadgeHtml()` call the same function and can't drift on the "proficiency supersedes a flat +1" rule. Renders as a small gold-bordered pill (`.cs-ab-passive`, new but minimal CSS — no `tokens.css` changes, reused `--gold`/`--radius-sm`/etc. directly) on the WIS card only, above its Save row, per the doc's explicit placement ask.
**Refresh timing**: doc suggested a `CLASS_ROW`-triggered refresh via `renderClassTabDynamic()`, but tracing the real fetch chain (`fetchClassData(ch).then(fetchOrCreateSheet).then(fetchEquipped)`, all inside the `Promise.all` that gates `renderLivePlay()`) showed `CLASS_ROW` is always resolved by the time `renderAbilitySkills()` runs (it's only ever called from `renderLivePlay()`, after that `Promise.all` settles) — so extending the existing `renderAbilitySkills()` refresh to also update the 6 save rows and the passive badge is correct and sufficient; no second refresh hook needed. Deviated from the doc here deliberately, grounded in the actual code's timing rather than the doc's assumption — flagging the deviation rather than silently matching the doc's suggested wiring.
**Left undecided, per the doc's own instruction not to guess a DM's-call item**: no top-level "what's a saving throw?" explainer beyond the per-ability `SAVE_TIPS` — the doc explicitly named the tone/placement of that as the DM's call, not mine to pick.
**Not built, confirmed in scope note**: Passive Investigation / Passive Insight — doc says explicitly not this pass, same formula, add later only if useful at the table.
Verified live on Kael Evander (Ranger, real data) via the local static server: STR +1 mod → proficient save "+3", DEX +2 mod → proficient save "+4", CON/INT/CHA correctly show plain (non-"Prof.") saves matching their raw modifiers, WIS Passive Perception badge reads 15 (10 + WIS mod +3 + prof +2, since Perception is one of his real proficient skills) — all cross-checked by hand against his real ability scores and `classes.saving_throws` value ("Strength, Dexterity"), not just eyeballed. Confirmed the skill list itself (Athletics "+2 (+1)", Animal Handling/Perception/Survival "Prof." "+5") is unchanged after the `computeSkillTotal()` extraction — same output as before the refactor. Zero console errors, zero horizontal overflow at 375px mobile. `new Function()` syntax check clean. Pushed live.

**2026-08-12 (later same day, after the Skills-section review rounds below) — Added real PHB terms in parens next to the Anavale-flavor species and class-flavor names (`sheet/index.html`).**
DM asked directly, no investigation needed: add the real D&D 5e Player's Handbook term in parens next to the Anavale in-world flavor text for species and class names, "so we do not forget" the mechanical identity behind the flavor names. Checked where these two names actually render before touching anything (`grep` for `speciesDisplay`/`classDisplay`) — both appear in exactly one place each. `speciesDisplay` (the raw `ch.species` slug, e.g. "Veilstepped") is used only in the header blurb built by `buildMainInfoHtml()`. `classDisplay` (the real `ch.class_id`, e.g. "Ranger" — already a real PHB term, not a reskin) is used only on the Class tab's class card (`cs-ov-class-name`) — that one needed no change, since a class's real name and its PHB name are the same string; the Anavale-flavor stand-in for class is a *different* value, `CLASS_FLAVOR_NAME[classKey]` (e.g. "The Wanderer" for Ranger), also only used in that same header blurb.
Restored `SPECIES_PHB` (species-slug → PHB species name, e.g. `veilstepped:'Changeling'`) — this exact 12-entry table existed before, dropped as dead code in an earlier session's identity-pill removal (the pill row was found redundant with the blurb and deleted; the PHB lookup that had fed it was deleted too, since nothing else used it at the time). Re-added verbatim from git history rather than re-deriving it, since it was previously DM-approved data, not guessed.
`buildMainInfoHtml()`: added `speciesKey` (mirrors the existing `classKey` pattern) and, inside the blurb-building block, two new local strings — `speciesWithPhb` (species display + ` (SPECIES_PHB[speciesKey])` when a mapping exists) and `flavorWithPhb` (Gigglegloom class-flavor name + ` (real class name)`, derived inline via `cap(classKey.replace(/-/g,' '))` rather than threading `classDisplay` through as a new parameter, since class IDs are always single words and the existing `cap()` helper already produces the identical string). Both substituted into the existing blurb sentence in place of the bare names — no other blurb wording changed.
Verified live on Kael Evander (local static server on :8791, restarted fresh — nothing had been listening on the port from a prior session): header blurb now reads "A Veilstepped (Changeling) The Wanderer (Ranger) who carries the Featherflow — from Nombi." Confirmed via `getComputedStyle`-equivalent DOM read that the Class tab's `cs-ov-class-name` still shows plain "Ranger", unaffected. Zero console errors (network log confirmed every resource 200 OK; console `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` entries were leftover from navigation attempts before the local server was up, not from the loaded page). `new Function()` syntax check clean. Pushed live.

**2026-08-12 (later same day, after the Speaks-color fix below) — Added a Skills section to the Ability Scores tab (`sheet/index.html`). Investigated first per explicit DM instruction, reported findings, built after DM decisions on 2 open questions.**
**Investigation (Step 1)**: (a) Checked Figma for a Skills frame under Ability Scores — pulled `get_metadata` on the file's page tree, found the desktop (`51:1663`) and mobile (`51:1668`) Ability Scores frames, then `get_screenshot`'d both directly. Confirmed neither contains anything beyond the header + the 6 ability-score cards already built — no Skills section exists anywhere in the file, on any tab. Same situation as Special Effects: flagged clearly rather than guessing at a design that isn't there. (b) Confirmed `character_sheet.proficiency_bonus` is a real stored integer column (via `list_tables`) — populated `2` on all 3 characters (level 1, matches standard scaling), seeded once at first sheet creation (`sheet/index.html`, hardcoded `2` on insert) and never read anywhere in the codebase since. Recommended reading `SHEET.proficiency_bonus` directly rather than deriving it from `ch.level` via a hardcoded scaling table, since it's already the DM's own editable value (same pattern as `hp_max_modifier`) — confirmed correct by DM. (c) Checked real data for overlap between `ch.skills` (full proficiency) and `PAST_EFFECTS`' flat +1 `skill_bonuses`, rather than reasoning it out abstractly: Kael Evander is proficient in *and* has a flat +1 on both Animal Handling and Survival; Hana Sable is proficient in *and* has a flat +1 on Insight; Visenya Oryn has no overlap. No existing code anywhere combines the two. Recommended proficiency supersede the flat +1 (based on the existing "not full proficiency, **but still useful**" tooltip copy implying the flat bonus's purpose is specifically to help skills you *lack* proficiency in) — DM confirmed with a stronger, decisive rationale: the 2024 PHB's stacking rule (same-target bonuses don't add; the higher one applies), which lands on the identical outcome since proficiency bonus (≥+2) always beats the flat +1.
**Build (Step 2)**: new `ABILITY_SKILLS` constant — all 18 skills mapped to their parent ability (fixed D&D rules, not derived from data; CON gets `[]`). New `abilitySkillsInnerHtml(ab, modNum, profBonus, ch)` renders one ability's skill list (or a "No skills" state for CON) — each skill shows name + `SKILL_TIPS` tooltip (verbatim, no new copy) + total bonus (`modNum + profBonus` if in `ch.skills`, `modNum + 1` if not proficient but present in `ch.skill_bonuses`, else `modNum` alone) + one of three visually distinct states (proficient: filled gold dot, bold name; flat: gold-ring dot, normal weight; neither: no dot, muted name — new `.cs-ab-skill--proficient/--flat/--none` CSS, no existing classes reused since nothing like this existed). Rendered inside each `.cs-ab-card`, below the existing modifier line, with `id="cs-ab-skills-{ab}"` per card. Because `buildAbilityScoresHtml()` runs before `SHEET` (holding the real `proficiency_bonus`) finishes loading — same ordering gap `renderHeaderHP()` already works around — added `renderAbilitySkills()`, called from `renderLivePlay()`, which re-renders all 6 skill blocks in place once `SHEET` is available; initial render falls back to `2` (matches the real seed default). The old flat "Proficient skills: X, Y, Z" comma-list (`cs-story-block`/`cs-story-row`) is fully removed — the grouped list covers the same information plus bonus math and flat-bonus skills it never showed, so nothing was left rendering redundantly.
**Verification**: live on all 3 real characters via the local dev server, both breakpoints. Kael (1600px): Animal Handling/Perception/Survival correctly render `cs-ab-skill--proficient` at `+5` (WIS mod +3, prof +2) — confirmed via DOM read, not just visual, that the two real overlap skills (Animal Handling, Survival) render as proficient-only, not stacked (`+5`, not `+6`); Athletics correctly renders `cs-ab-skill--flat` at `+2` (STR mod +1, flat +1); everything else renders `cs-ab-skill--none` at ability-mod-only. To confirm the async refresh path (not just the initial fallback), temporarily set Kael's real `proficiency_bonus` to `3` via SQL, reloaded, confirmed the 3 proficient skills recomputed live to `+6` via `renderAbilitySkills()`, then reverted to `2` via a follow-up SQL read. Hana (her real overlap case, Insight): confirmed `+5` (WIS mod +3, prof +2), not stacked. Visenya (no overlap): confirmed all 3 states render correctly with no false overlaps. Mobile (375px): 2-per-row wrap, "No skills" state renders cleanly on CON, zero console errors, `document.body.scrollWidth - window.innerWidth` reads 1px (sub-pixel rounding, not a real overflow). `new Function()` syntax check clean on the extracted inline script. Committed (`ba13af1`), pushed after DM review; see the "Prof." label follow-up above for the one legibility fix that came out of that review.

**2026-08-12 (later same day, live DM review continued) — Fixed the Speaks block's language name text color (`sheet/index.html`).**
DM flagged live on Hana Sable's sheet (screenshot from `carlosknightiii.github.io`): "Jugabi Canopy" read as blue text next to its own green rune glyph, and asked for the text to match "the color of the region it is from." Checked the data model before assuming a fix: `regions` has no hex color column at all (only `color_health`, a status word — Dire/Excellent/Faded/Good/Moderate, confirmed via `information_schema`), so there's no literal region-color field to join against. `languages.color` is the real signal — confirmed via a full table read that it already tracks each language's origin region visually (`jugabi-canopy` `#4a9a3a` green, `nombi-frost` `#2266b8` blue, `sohot-old` `#c8882a` gold, `caparian-deep` `#2a7a3a` green, `conclave-script` `#6a3aaa` purple, `pre-partition` `#8a6a2a` brown) — this is what the glyph already used. Root cause confirmed live via `getComputedStyle()` before touching anything: `.cs-mi-speaks-glyph` got `LANGUAGE_ROW.color` as an inline style, `.cs-mi-speaks-name` never did, staying on a fixed `color: var(--blue)` CSS rule regardless of language. Fix: `renderSpeaksBlock()` now applies the same `LANGUAGE_ROW.color` inline to both spans; the CSS rule's `var(--blue)` is now just an unreachable fallback for the (currently nonexistent) case of a character with no `language_extra` row resolved. Verified on both Hana (Jugabi Canopy, glyph+name both `rgb(74,154,58)`) and Kael (Nombi Frost, both `rgb(34,102,184)`) via computed style, not just visual read. Zero console errors.

**2026-08-11 — Added a "Special Effects" panel to the Inventory tab (`items` schema, `dm.html`, `sheet/index.html`). Investigated first, reported findings, built after go-ahead.**
**Investigation**: checked real populated `items` rows before proposing any new column. `description`/`story_note`/`gigglegloom_relationship` are consistently flavor-only in practice, not just by schema comment — e.g. `+1 Chain Mail`'s `gigglegloom_relationship` is pure lore ("the enchantment made visible"), while the actual mechanical bonus lives entirely in the separate `magic_bonus` column; `story_note` is location lore or literal DM-only session notes, matching its schema-comment scope exactly with no drift. Confirmed `grants_advantage[]`/`grants_proficiency[]`/`magic_bonus` are the only pre-existing structured mechanical fields — nothing else in the 47-column schema supports arbitrary homebrew effects (resistance, speed multipliers, sentience, etc.). Also checked the `effect_type`/`effect_dice`/`effect_flat`/`effect_duration`/`uses_max` cluster (not obviously relevant to the ask, but close enough in name to warrant ruling out overlap): every one of the 9 populated rows is a consumable or tool (Health Potion, Antidote Kit, River-Treated Torch, etc.) with `uses_max` set — zero rows with a non-null `equip_slot` use these fields, confirming they're scoped to one-shot consumable-use effects with no overlap with "what an item does while equipped."
**Schema**: added two new nullable `items` text columns, `equipped_effect_mechanical` and `equipped_effect_flavor`, both optional, DM-written free text, naming deliberately distinct from the consumable `effect_*` cluster above.
**DM Tools** (`dm.html`): two new textareas in the item form's Content section, directly after History (lines ~2503–2519) — "Equipped Effect — Mechanical" / "Equipped Effect — Flavor", each with help text distinguishing "a real gameplay effect while equipped" from "pure narrative flavor... no mechanical weight." Wired into both halves of the save/edit round trip: `generateItem()`'s entry object (after `history:`) and the edit-populate `sv()` calls (a separate function, easy to miss — confirmed both by testing an actual edit-save round trip, not just reading the code).
**Sheet** (`sheet/index.html`): new `.cs-inv-top-col` panel titled "Special Effects" inserted into `.cs-inv-top-row` between the doll column and the Currency column — reuses `.cs-story-card`/`.cs-story-card-title` for the card shell (no new card component). Iterates all 13 `EQUIPPED` keys (10 doll slots + both rings); for each equipped item with any of `grants_advantage`/`grants_proficiency`/`magic_bonus`/`equipped_effect_mechanical`/`equipped_effect_flavor` set, renders a card with one row per effect. Visual split reuses existing tokens rather than inventing new ones: mechanical rows get a `var(--blue-600)` inset box with a `var(--red-strong)` left border (the same red already used for weapon damage text elsewhere in this tab — not a new color); flavor rows get plain italic `var(--char-text-muted)` text, no box, deliberately calmer. Panel column hides entirely (`display:none`, not an empty box) when zero equipped items have anything to show — implemented as a single `renderSpecialEffects()` call from inside `renderEquipped()` itself (one hook point covers all 6 mutation call sites — selectEquipItem/unequipSlot/etc. — rather than touching each one).
**Verification**: live end-to-end on Kael Evander via the local dev server. Confirmed the panel is genuinely absent (not just visually empty) when no equipped item has any effect field set — checked via `getComputedStyle(col).display === 'none'`, not just a screenshot, since the Browser-pane tool hit the same intermittent scroll/paint rendering glitch already on record from earlier sessions (worked around via `scrollIntoView()` + `form_input`/direct JS reads instead of trusting coordinate-based clicks, which were landing inconsistently under the glitch). Ran the real DM Tools round trip once: opened Leather Armor's real edit form (chosen over the first test item, Leather Cord, after discovering — and leaving alone, since it's unrelated pre-existing data — that Leather Cord has a null `description`, which the form's required-field validation correctly blocked saving on), typed both new fields, called the same `generateItem()` the Save button calls, confirmed via a follow-up SQL read that both values landed in Supabase, then confirmed the sheet picked them up live: a "Leather Armor" card with a red-accented mechanical row and an italic flavor row, positioned correctly right of the doll. All test values reverted to null on both items afterward, confirmed via a final SQL read. Zero console errors throughout. `node --check`-equivalent (`new Function()` on every extracted inline `<script>` block) clean on both `dm.html` and `sheet/index.html`.

**2026-08-10 — Fixed a real double-equip gap in `selectEquipItem()` (`sheet/index.html`), surfaced live right after the mechanical/cosmetic-split retirement below. DM reported Kael showing the same Braided Cord Ring in both Right Ring and Left Ring simultaneously.**
**Investigated before touching anything, per DM instruction.** Confirmed via SQL: Kael owns exactly one `braided-cord-ring` inventory row (not a migration duplicate) — but `character_sheet.equipped` had `ring_left` and `ring_right` both pointing at that same catalog id, meaning the one physical row was referenced from two slots at once. Root cause read from the code, not guessed: `getEquipCandidates()` lists an owned item as a slot candidate purely by category/`equip_slot` match, with no check for whether that same row is already equipped somewhere else; `selectEquipItem()`'s mutual-exclusion logic only clears *sibling keys within one slot's own `keys` array* (e.g. `hand_left` vs `shield`, both backing the Left Hand doll position) — it has no concept of "this item is already the value of a completely different slot." Confirmed this isn't ring-specific: `handL`/`handR` both accept category `'weapon'` (two separate slots, same collision shape), so a single owned one-handed weapon had the identical exposure — just not yet hit live. Every other slot has a unique `accessorySlotKey`, so no other pair collides.
**Fix, scoped exactly per DM instruction — same physical row, not same category**: added one general check in `selectEquipItem()`, keyed off the inventory row's own `equipped_slot` column (which slot *that specific row* currently occupies) rather than the catalog id shared by `equipped[key]` values. If the row being equipped already has a *different* `equipped_slot` than the target, that old key gets cleared as part of the same action — the item moves, it doesn't duplicate. Guarded with `equipped[priorSlotKey] === equipKey` before clearing, specifically because `unequipSlot()` never resets a row's `equipped_slot` column back to null (a separate, pre-existing gap, not fixed here) — without that guard, a stale column value could falsely clear an unrelated key that was never actually holding this item. Not hardcoded to rings/hands — works off any inventory row id match across any two keys, so it also covers the pair that hasn't been exercised yet.
**Confirmed does NOT affect Visenya's legitimate case** (two separate owned rings, one in each ring slot): the check is keyed off the literal inventory row id, and her two rings are two distinct rows with two distinct `equipped_slot` values — reopening either of her ring popovers does nothing (no `selectEquipItem()` call happens on view), and her state was confirmed byte-identical before and after.
Reset Kael's `ring_right` back to empty via direct SQL (should never have been set — he owns one ring, it belongs in `ring_left` per the original migration). Verified live end-to-end: Kael's Right Ring popover correctly shows "Nothing equipped" with the one owned ring listed as a legitimate candidate (not a false "already equipped" block — the fix allows selecting it, it just moves instead of duplicating); clicked it, confirmed `ring_left` cleared and `ring_right` populated, then reversed the click to move it back, confirmed clean round-trip. Visenya's two-ring state re-verified unchanged after opening (not clicking) her Right Ring popover. Hana's Quarterstaff (one owned one-handed weapon) used as the hand-slot equivalent test — moved Right→Left (confirmed `hand_right` cleared, `hand_left` populated, screenshotted), then moved back, confirmed restored to the original migrated baseline. All three characters' `character_sheet.equipped` confirmed via a final SQL read to exactly match their pre-fix-testing correct state. Zero console errors throughout.

**2026-08-10 — Investigated why Kael Evander has a Gold ring equipped (25gp) against a 10gp starting budget (Greywitnessed). Not a bug — logged here, no code changed.**
Checked all three possible explanations before concluding: (1) Stage 4 shopping overspend is a genuine hard block in `js/character.js` (`validateStage(4)` computes `getStartingGold() - calcGoldSpent()`, blocks `goNext()`/`jumpToStage()` on negative remainder) — not a soft warning, ruled out. (2) The ring's price (`cost_gp: 25` in `STATIC_OPTION_COSTS`) hasn't changed since May 27 2026, before Kael's July 6 creation, and isn't DM-Tools-editable at all (it's hardcoded in creator JS, not a Supabase `items` row) — ruled out. (3) The validation itself isn't buggy either — the ring alone (25gp) exceeds Greywitnessed's entire 10gp budget, so it's mathematically impossible for it to have passed the real Stage 4 hard block under any combination of other purchases. Real explanation: `appearance_data` (which is what actually stores "a gold ring") can only be written from two places — Stage 4's validated shopping flow (ruled out above) or the character sheet's own "Currently Wearing" cosmetic picker (`saveAppearance()`/`selectWearingOption()`), which has zero cost or ownership validation by design, since post-creation appearance edits are meant to be free. DM Tools never touches `appearance_data` at all (confirmed via grep), so that's not the path either. Conclusion: two intentionally separate systems — a one-time budget-gated shop and a permanently free-form cosmetic picker — both working as built.
**Flagging forward, not acting**: the DM confirmed a bigger change is planned (not yet built) requiring cosmetic items to be actually owned in inventory before they can be equipped, matching how weapons/armor already work via `getEquipCandidates()`. That would make this exact scenario structurally impossible going forward. Worth remembering when next touching `APPEARANCE_OPTIONS`/`STATIC_OPTION_COSTS`/`renderPopoverCosmeticRow()` — check whether that ownership-gating has landed before assuming the current free-form static-list behavior (hat/cloak/ring/necklace/earrings/shoes/lower/top, all unfiltered by real inventory) is still current. The hand-slot branch already partially does this (weapon-name cosmetic options are sourced from real inventory rows) — the other 8 option lists don't yet.

**2026-08-10 — `sheet/index.html`: ring selectors converted from plain `<select>` dropdowns into real slot tiles matching the other 10 gear slots. Same session, right after the weapon-slot changes below.**
`renderRingsRow()` rebuilt to emit the exact same `.cs-equip-slot-wrap`/`.cs-equip-slot-box`/`.cs-equip-slot-label`/`.cs-equip-slot-name` markup the 10 doll slots use (new `renderRingSlot()`) instead of `.cs-ring-item`/`.cs-ring-select` — clickable, opens the same popover via `openSlotPopover(ring.key)`. `RING_SLOTS` labels shortened to match the other slots' terse style ("Right Ring"/"Left Ring", was "Right Hand Ring"/"Left Hand Ring") — used both on the tile and as the popover title now that rings open a real popover instead of just an inline `<select>`. Dropped the now-fully-dead `.cs-ring-item`/`.cs-ring-label`/`.cs-ring-select` CSS (confirmed via grep no longer referenced anywhere).
**Sizing had a real trap, caught before shipping, not after**: `.cs-equip-slot-wrap`'s width comes from `cqi` (container query units) relative to `.cs-equip-doll`'s own `container-type:inline-size` — but `#cs-ring-row` is a *sibling* of `#cs-equipped-block` (i.e. of the doll), not a descendant, so it can't inherit that containment. Per spec, `cqi` with no qualifying container resolves to 0, which would have silently pinned every ring tile to its `clamp()` floor (~50px) forever, regardless of viewport — visually smaller than the other 10 slots at any width above the floor, the opposite of "matching." Fixed by giving `.cs-ring-row` the *identical* sizing formula as `.cs-equip-doll` (`width:100%; max-width:61.08rem; container-type:inline-size`) rather than trying to share containment — both derive their width from the same `.cs-inv-top-col` parent, so their `cqi` math always resolves to the same absolute px at any viewport. Verified via `getBoundingClientRect()`, not assumed: ring tile and doll tile boxes measured pixel-identical at both 1440px desktop (128.7×131.0px) and 375px mobile (70.4×71.7px, the mobile touch-target floor).
**Popover — rings skip the "Currently Equipped" section entirely**, not just its search box (the existing empty-candidates fix from an earlier round hides only the search input, for slots that *can* be mechanical but currently aren't — Back with nothing equipped). Rings can never be mechanical (`items.equip_slot` has never had a ring value), so this is a different case. Added `id="cs-equip-mechanical-section"` to that block's static markup; `openSlotPopover()` now branches on whether the target is a doll slot or a ring (new `ringSlotByKey()` lookup) and toggles that section's `display` accordingly, skipping `renderPopoverMechanicalRow()`/`renderEquipResults()` entirely for rings rather than calling them into an empty state.
**Real bug caught during this, not shipped**: the `.cs-slot-popover-section + .cs-slot-popover-section` adjacent-sibling CSS rule (the divider between the two sections) still matched `#cs-wearing-section` even with the mechanical section `display:none`, since CSS sibling combinators walk the DOM tree, not the render/paint tree — `display:none` removes an element from paint, not from the DOM the selector matches against. Caught live via a fresh screenshot before writing it off as done (would have shown a border/margin floating above "Currently Wearing" with nothing above it). Fixed with a new `#cs-wearing-section.cs-wearing-section-solo` override (ID+class specificity beats the two-class combinator rule), added/removed alongside the mechanical section's `display` toggle.
**Cosmetic picker reused, not rebuilt**: both rings now route through the exact same `renderPopoverCosmeticRow()`/`onWearingChange()` every other slot uses — required generalizing two lookups, not adding a parallel path. `SLOT_APPEARANCE_KEY` gained `ring_right`/`ring_left` self-mappings (their doll "area" and their `appearance_data` storage key are the same string, unlike e.g. `handL`→`hand_left`). Both rings share ONE options list (`APPEARANCE_OPTIONS.ring`) despite having two distinct storage keys, which the existing `appKey`-doubles-as-options-key convention couldn't express — added a small `APPEARANCE_OPTIONS_KEY` map (`ring_right`/`ring_left` → `'ring'`) and changed the one options-lookup line to check it first, falling through to `appKey` itself unchanged for every other slot (verified: 7 real ring options loaded in the popover, matching the shared list, not a truncated/wrong one).
Verification: live on Kael Evander (real Gold ring / Braided cord ring on both hands) at 1440px desktop and 375px mobile — tiles render the correct ring art/name, popover shows only "Right Ring"/"Left Ring" + "Currently Wearing" + its cosmetic explainer + a real 7-option select, zero "Currently Equipped" section, zero orphaned border. Cross-checked a normal slot (Back) immediately after closing a ring popover to confirm no regression — mechanical section correctly restores (`display:block`, "Nothing equipped", real search box). `document.body.scrollWidth - window.innerWidth === 0` at both breakpoints. Zero new console errors (ring art itself loaded clean — `gold-ring.webp`/`braided-cord-ring.webp` both 200 OK; the only 404 is the same pre-existing, already-documented `appearance/longbow.webp` fallback, unrelated).

**2026-08-10 — `sheet/index.html`: two Equipped Gear doll weapon-slot changes — per-slot red damage text, and a real two-handed-weapon mirror image on the locked hand (was just an empty grey box). Same session, right after the popover copy/UX fixes below. Also fixed a real, previously-latent bug the second change surfaced: two-handed detection only ever checked `hand_right`.**
1. **Per-slot damage text**: `renderEquippedSlot()` now appends a `.cs-equip-slot-damage` line ("1d8 (piercing)") under the item name for any hand slot holding a weapon with `damage_dice` — new `buildSlotDamageText()`, a plain-text sibling of the existing `buildWeaponDamageHtml()` (not a reuse of it: that one's spans are hardcoded gold for the combined summary row above the doll, `.cs-equip-damage-row`/`computeWeaponDamageHtml()`, which is untouched and still renders as before). Color is `var(--red-strong)` (`#d62b2b`, confirmed via computed style, not assumed) — the same red already used for the HP bar fill, not a new one. No area check needed to scope this to hands only — `damage_dice` is only ever present on weapon items, and only hand slots' categories ever admit weapons.
2. **Two-handed mirror image**: the locked "other hand" slot (`.cs-equip-slot-locked`, existing class, reused verbatim — still just `opacity:0.4`, no new grayscale/filter invented) now renders the two-handed weapon's own image instead of an empty box, with no name or damage line (that text only belongs on the hand actually holding it). Confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()`/computed-style checks, not just a screenshot glance: the mirrored `<img src>` is the identical `longbow.webp` URL on both hands, opacity 0.4, zero `.cs-equip-slot-name`/`.cs-equip-slot-damage` children.
   **Real bug found and fixed while generalizing this, not scope creep** — the DM's ask ("derive it from whichever hand is actually locked, don't hardcode always-right") required reading `renderEquipped()`'s two-handed detection first, which turned up that it only ever checked `EQUIPPED.hand_right` — a two-handed weapon equipped into the *Left* Hand slot (fully possible via `selectEquipItem()`, which routes a Left Hand pick to `hand_left` same as a Right Hand pick routes to `hand_right`, no hand privileged there) would never have triggered the lock at all, silently allowing both hands to hold weapons simultaneously. Replaced the hardcoded check with a new `detectTwoHandedLock()` that checks both hands and returns `{activeArea, lockedArea, item, lockedKeys}` — `lockedKeys` is `['hand_left','shield']` when Right Hand is active (Left Hand's doll position has two backing keys) or just `['hand_right']` when Left Hand is active (Right Hand only ever has one). `autoUnequipOffHandForTwoHanded()` now takes `keys` as a parameter instead of hardcoding `['hand_left','shield']`, so the same function clears whichever side is actually locked.
Verification: live on Kael Evander (real Longbow, two-handed, in `hand_right`) — Right Hand shows "1d8 (piercing)" in red, Left Hand shows the same bow image at 0.4 opacity with no text. Temporarily added a real Shortsword (1d6, one-handed) to Kael's own inventory via direct SQL, equipped it into Right Hand through the actual UI (replacing the Longbow) to verify the one-handed path shows its own damage text (confirmed "1d6 (piercing)") *without* triggering the mirror/lock on Left Hand (confirmed via class inspection: plain `cs-equip-slot-box cs-equip-slot-empty`, still clickable, no `cs-equip-slot-locked`) — then re-equipped the Longbow through the same UI flow and deleted the temporary inventory row, confirmed via a follow-up SQL read that `character_sheet.equipped` matches the original `{armor:"leather-armor", hand_right:"longbow"}` exactly. Zero new console errors (only the same pre-existing, already-documented `appearance/longbow.webp` cosmetic-fallback 404).

**2026-08-10 — `sheet/index.html`: 5 equipped-slot popover copy/UX fixes from live QC, same session right after the doll rebuild below.**
All 5 confirmed live on Kael Evander's Back slot (empty mechanical + no cosmetic chosen — a clean before/after test case since it exercises both empty states at once) before/after, desktop viewport.
1. **Title** — `openSlotPopover()` dropped the `' — Equipped Gear'` suffix; now just the slot name (e.g. "Back", was "BACK — EQUIPPED GEAR"). The old title implied the whole popover was about mechanical gear, which is why the cosmetic row below read as out of place.
2. **Duplicate "Currently equipped"** — `renderPopoverMechanicalRow()` no longer repeats the section heading's own words in the row below it; the row now reads just the item name (`<strong>Leather Armor</strong>`) or `Nothing equipped`, not `Currently equipped: nothing`.
3. **Search box hidden when nothing to search** — `renderEquipResults()` now toggles `#cs-equip-search-input`'s `display` based on `getEquipCandidates().length`, hiding it entirely for slots with zero inventory candidates instead of showing an empty search box above the empty-state message (a real live-QC question: "what is the search item field for?"). Confirmed the box still renders normally for slots that do have candidates (Chest, on Kael) — not a regression, this only fires on the zero-candidate path.
4. **Empty-state contrast** — `.cs-inv-empty` (shared by this popover's "No items in inventory for this slot"/"No matches." and the +Add Item modal's own "No matches.") was `rgba(255,255,255,0.2)`, close to unreadable. Changed to `var(--char-text-faint)`, the same muted tone already used for `.cs-wearing-empty` and other secondary copy in this file — a consistent existing tone, not a new one.
5. **"Currently Wearing" explainer** — added a new shared `WEARING_HEADING_HTML` (heading + a small `.cs-slot-popover-subtext` line, "Purely cosmetic — doesn't affect your stats") used by both branches of `renderPopoverCosmeticRow()` (the no-appearance-option slot and the normal picker), so every slot's cosmetic row explains itself at a glance — copy-only, the section's own logic/behavior is untouched per the DM's explicit instruction that this wasn't a redesign.
Verification: JS syntax validated via `new Function()` on the extracted inline script block. Zero new console errors (the only 404s are the pre-existing, already-documented `appearance/longbow.webp` cosmetic-art-fallback path from the entry below, unrelated to this change).

**2026-08-10 — `sheet/index.html`: Equipped Gear doll rebuilt against the real Figma layout (node `68:7354` "equipped-doll", pulled fresh this session via `get_design_context` — the Aug 9 build had shipped with no Figma reference for this specific node at all, DM has since designed the real layout there).**
DM supplied exact structure/geometry from the pull directly (flex row, gap-10 items-end justify-center; a Left Hand slot at gap-16 beside a 4-slot Head/Chest/Arms/Feet column, mirrored on the right with Neck/Earrings/Back/Legs + Right Hand; each stacked slot's 177.249px row bottom-justifies its 140.691x143.249 frame + label; silhouette 362.553x759.268 centered between the two groups) — re-pulled it myself before writing any code rather than building from the description alone, confirmed it matched verbatim.
**Silhouette asset check (done first, per explicit instruction not to assume)**: confirmed `equipped-gear-silhouete.png` is the DM's already-replaced version, not the original — its mtime (this session, after the last commit touching it) and its working-tree bytes (128,721) differ from HEAD's committed bytes (181,525) per `git diff --stat`, and its pixel dimensions (363x760) already match the Figma spec almost exactly. Left the image itself untouched.
**Structure**: old grid-template-areas layout (max-width:480px, silhouette absolutely overlaid via grid-column span + object-fit:contain) replaced with real nested flex markup mirroring the Figma node one-to-one — `.cs-equip-doll` > two `.cs-equip-doll-side` groups (each a flanking hand slot + a `.cs-equip-doll-col` of 4 `.cs-equip-slot-row`s) flanking `.cs-equip-doll-silhouette`, all `align-items:flex-end` for the shared bottom baseline the DM described. `renderEquipped()`/`renderEquippedSlot()` rewritten to build this nesting explicitly (using the existing `equipSlotByArea()` lookup, called earlier in the file now — safe, it's a hoisted function declaration) instead of relying on CSS `grid-area` placement; the `grid-area` inline styles are gone entirely. Slot order unchanged, per the DM's explicit instruction — only sizing/spacing/anchoring moved.
**Fluid sizing, not raw px**: build spec (below, then Aug 9 entry above) already flags that hardcoding Figma's canvas px onto a differently-shaped container was exactly what broke the silhouette twice before. Used CSS container query units (`container-type:inline-size` on `.cs-equip-doll`, sized via `width:100%; max-width:61.08rem` off its own flex parent so there's no circular sizing dependency) — every descendant dimension, including heights, is a `cqi` percentage of the doll's own resolved width, so the whole layout scales as one unit exactly like Figma's proportions instead of one fixed breakpoint. `clamp()` floors stop things shrinking below a usable size.
**Real bug caught during verification, not shipped**: first pass added `flex-wrap:wrap` on `.cs-equip-doll` as a "safety net." Since the cqi percentages are tuned to sum to ~100% of the container by construction, this landed within a sub-pixel of the wrap threshold at common widths and browsers rounded one side group onto its own line below the item table — confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect()` (the two side groups' rects were ~700px apart vertically) before catching it, not from a visual glance. Fixed by switching to `nowrap` and instead sizing the `clamp()` floors low enough (~35.8% of reference size, ~350px total doll width) to guarantee fit within this column's own pre-existing 358px architectural minimum (`.cs-inv-top-col { min-width: 358px }`, unrelated pre-existing CSS, not touched) — worked through the actual flex-wrap/flex-grow arithmetic of the shared Equipped-Gear/Currency row to find the real narrowest case (~397px column width around an ~850px browser window, currency sharing the row without wrapping) rather than assuming the mobile breakpoint was the only risk.
**Mobile (<=600px, existing DM-approved deviation, unchanged in principle)**: silhouette still hides — the real Figma mobile frame reuses this same desktop component with no mobile layout of its own (a gap already flagged in `docs/inventory-tab-build-spec.md`). Added a mobile-only override raising the slot-size floor for a better touch target now that the silhouette's gone and there's room to spare, rather than inheriting the tight ~350px-total floor sized for the silhouette-visible case.
**Label styling**: color changed to `--white` (was `--char-text-faint`, a muted gray) and size changed to a `clamp()` capped at the existing `--char-ov-h5` token (18px) — both explicit in the DM's spec ("Cinzel Decorative Bold, 18px, white"). Left the existing uppercase transform/letter-spacing alone — not mentioned in the ask, and consistent with the Inventory tab's other section headers (EQUIPPED GEAR/CURRENCY/CARRYING all render uppercase the same way).
Verification: JS syntax validated via `new Function()` on the extracted inline script block. Checked live via the local dev server (started fresh this session — `.claude/launch.json` only attaches to an already-running server, wasn't running) on Kael Evander (real leather armor/longbow/worn boots etc.) and Hana Sable (no mechanical items, but real cosmetic-only values on Back/Right Hand/Legs — a better edge-case test than a truly-blank character, confirmed the cosmetic-vs-mechanical badge distinction still renders correctly post-rebuild) at both 1440px desktop and 375px mobile. `getBoundingClientRect()`-verified the bottom-alignment and exact 10px gaps between groups and silhouette, and `document.body.scrollWidth - window.innerWidth === 0` (no horizontal overflow) at all four combinations. Zero new console errors — the only 404s (`appearance/longbow.webp`, `appearance/quarterstaff.webp`) are the pre-existing, already-documented weapon-cosmetic-art-doesn't-exist fallback path, unrelated to this change.
